Common multiplier

Make certified common-multiplier repair more useful through four investigations: a complete geometric application with protected survival and explicit costs; structural joint-error savings beyond summed ranks; effective homological and localization bounds for surviving annihilators; and a hardness-preserving extension with efficient extraction of a hard object. Pursue them in that order, preserving the Braun/Pang theorem and all foundational bridges.

Starting point

User-directed Common multiplier investigation. Braun's affine-block elimination and Pang's relative approximation-error annihilation motivate this thread. It is separate from the interrupted main-notebook IPS cycle. Braun's construction and the recorded Pang audit retain their original scopes.

Where we stand

The geometric application is complete; three objectives remain open. Marked curve recovery constructs a locator from unknown errors, recovers the message and fixes its value at a protected point. The explicit Hermitian family has unbounded positive genus, supplies its bases and points, and meets the complete pole and construction budgets. This is an application of established algebraic-geometry decoding, not a new decoder.

Joint-error savings is open. Overlapping curve recovery gives one protected common locator for partially overlapping supports below the summed-rank budget. In that model the joint rank is the size of the union of supports by definition, so the saving is inclusion–exclusion and leaves the structural joint bound unproved. Exact common-kernel selection lets the composition's selection step use any certificate of an exact stacked-rank criterion. Shared-span affine exclusion counts Braun's inventory by shared input subspaces, but gains only by domination, for clustered families. Increment counts gain at most a factor \((d+k)/k\) more, and the ceiling on the common-kernel step bounds every certificate of Braun's kernel step at a fixed degree. The coordinate count shows that the exact summed count already reaches the ceiling's main term on coordinate blocks; the window left is logarithmic at \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\), but grows at minimal degree. The route review kept the obligation open and tested a correlation certificate: the Harris certificate is correct but, at every computed parameter, below the greedy transversal certificate or the summed count, and at the goal's polylogarithmic degrees the summed count already suffices. That transversal does widen Braun's range for small coordinate inventories, to degree about \(n/(2M)\), by fixing one bit per block, and over every prime field at every degree; it is an elementary cover at polynomial degree, and at the goal's polylogarithmic degree the summed count already suffices. In Pang's contraction, where the summed count sets the degree, the domain-only stacked rank is a union-of-supports interpolation rank, so a saving of the admitted kind needs the truncation or non-domain relations of Pang's proper quotient. The goal review found that the pigeonhole evidence for such savings is a domain model on hole-respecting assignments: below degree \(n\) the product pigeon axioms are inert, and these systems are never proper above it; there single-input killers are supports, and their joint kernels are one annihilator by factor ideal domination. Domain-model savings need not survive in a proper quotient (correction). Where linear axioms are active, in the functional pigeonhole system at six and seven pigeons, the common kernels at \(K=1\) are still the monomial frame's plus the relations (constructed kernels), although linear axioms can transport killers in general; transported killers are an instance of factor span domination, which for survival is no stronger than a direct common killer of the factors.

Completed foundation. Certified repair composition remains the reviewed working theorem explaining Braun and Pang with original costs. Its finite-grid extension and the curve application have different domains: no bounded-PC interpretation of arbitrary curve errors or new circuit lower bound has been established.

Route decisions. Pursue four priorities in order: geometric application, joint-error savings, effective homology and localization, then hardness-preserving repair. The user requires all four; completing an initial subset does not complete the goal. Both homology and localization require substantive development with separately recorded outcomes. Native duality and further descent remain lower-priority queued investigations. Every research entry retains the Selection gain duty: identify an independently proved selection, survival or hardness-preservation improvement, or say unchanged. All six foundational bridges remain preserved.

The ledger-reference safeguard is implemented and committed, with test and mutation evidence; its administrative queue reconciliation remains separate.

Open statements

  1. Geometric application — resolved as a working proof. Marked curve recovery and the Hermitian family supply independent locator, detection and survival certificates, unknown-error recovery and explicit construction on unbounded positive-genus instances. The pole filtration and rational-function evaluation are specified. This application of established decoding does not settle the remaining objectives.
  2. Joint-error savings — open; second priority. Give independently checkable structural hypotheses and an all-parameter bound on the joint action of actual errors, strictly better than the sum of their individual ranks on a nontrivial unbounded family. Measure every rank in the same protected quotient and show that the saving improves a sufficient multiplier budget or admissible instance range after all composition costs. Duplicating errors, changing coordinates or merely representing the same kernel does not meet this endpoint. Nor does union-of-supports counting in an evaluation model, nor domination, the common kernel being the annihilator of one element of the window (factor ideal domination), nor a span of products of killers of individual factors, whose degree grows with the number of distinct factors (goal review, which flags this last exclusion as a change of target).
  3. Effective homology and localization — open; third priority. Develop both bridges toward a structural theorem bounding the first filtered degree of an annihilator that preserves a specified nonzero class, or constructing a strictly cheaper useful certificate on an unbounded family. Verify the hypotheses, nilpotent behavior and total costs in a faithful model, and show the consequence for repair. Identifying a Hom or Koszul cohomology space, or clearing finitely many denominators without a useful cost bound, does not resolve this obligation. An obstruction closes only the formulation it actually rules out; it does not count as completing the other bridge.
  4. Hardness-preserving repair — open; fourth priority. Extend certified repair with independently verified structural conditions yielding an efficient extraction of a specified hard object from every eligible repaired proof image or certificate. Prove the resulting circuit lower-bound transfer in a stated field and circuit model, with explicit size, depth, degree and representation overhead. Supply a nontrivial unbounded application. Nonzero survival, one hard certificate, an assumed hard output or uncosted division by the multiplier does not suffice.

Completed original obligations. Effective repair principle and portable composition are resolved for the original sufficient endpoint by certified repair composition. External application was resolved through established error-correcting pairs, with finite-grid repair adding a domain extension. These stable anchors preserve earlier links; the new goal does not retract those results.

The remaining route

  1. Joint-error savings next. Pursue a non-domination joint saving in one protected quotient, with an improved multiplier budget. Every saving found is a union of supports, domination by one annihilator, or a product of killers; next the general inertness lemma, then common kernels in the functional pigeonhole system, where the closure need not be a vanishing ideal; if only those three kinds appear, switch to effective-annihilator-degree. A certificate feeds exact selection. Support counting, domination and identical locator kernels are insufficient.
  2. Effective homology and localization third. Develop both existing queued investigations toward effective-annihilator-degree. Seek an explicit bound on the first useful surviving annihilator degree or a cheaper independently constructed certificate, retaining nilpotent behavior and the protected class. Record each approach's outcome separately.
  3. Hardness-preserving repair fourth. Develop hardness-preserving-repair with a hard target, affordable extraction and universal coverage of the eligible certificate class. Preserve the matched field, generators and circuit size/depth budget. Nonzero survival does not certify hardness.
  4. Geometric application complete. The marked Hermitian recovery theorem supplies the first objective. Further geometric work must bear on one of the remaining open statements rather than repeat this endpoint.

Proposed next step

On transfer and effective-annihilator-degree, take the queued item common-multiplier-localization, since the factor-span entry found that constructed joint kernels reduce to a direct common killer, whose first surviving degree is that statement's object: “Investigate an effective common-denominator or nilpotent-sensitive replacement with a useful degree bound and protected survival”. Start from the finite common-denominator theorem of the multiplier-transfer limits and the goal review's note on nilpotents modulo pseudoideals, and test a degree bound for a common killer on the functional pigeonhole closure, where the frame and its relations are now described.

Working mathematical context

Foundations and bridge map

Geometric recovery. On a smooth curve of genus \(g\), \(n\) distinct received points and disjoint pole divisors of degrees \(a,m\), two-system recovery works for \(t\) unknown errors if \(a\ge t+g\) and \(n>a+m+t\). A separate protected point can be normalized when \(a\ge t+2g\). Solve for locator and weighted message, then the message; never divide evaluation vectors. The Hermitian construction supplies all inputs in cubic field-operation cost. Its pole weights are not total polynomial degrees. For several words with support union \(u\), one common locator needs \(a\ge u+2g\); the exact criterion of exact selection holds there although summed and joint rank conditions cannot certify a protected point.

For effective-annihilator-degree. Boolean ideal quotients are products of copies of the field, so an ideal defect there is a reduced point set; nilpotents arise only modulo a degree-\(D\) pseudoideal, which defines no scheme, and an unsatisfiable source generates the unit ideal (note). A regularity bound needs a pseudoideal analogue or a non-Boolean encoding.

Native and auxiliary observations. The diagonal principle gives faithful scalar moments when the common diagonal kernel kills mixed products. Ordinary restriction satisfies it and retains Braun's ledger. Paid native moments use the sufficient reserve \(b+e+pK\le D\). Pang's recurrence needs only \(\Delta+K+d\le D\), plus its separate replay ceiling. After its adaptive choices, independent coordinate rows give a base-field symmetric form with the exact kernel, without native products. This construction works for every linear map and yields no rank estimate. The observable coding tester has the error kernel without knowing the error. Finite perfect-field diagonal zero remains weaker than radical membership.

Product-code limit. The primary Schur-product Kneser theorem bounds product dimension from below using its stabilizer. The existing paid stable-core construction already turns small absolute growth into a conditional source repair. The comparison found no independent small-growth estimate; the graded-envelope and selected-feature limitations remain applicable.

Fixed-action compression. For prime-field \(J_D,w,U\) and errors of degree at most \(e\), with \(\deg w\le b\), \(U\le V_K\), the generic pencil captures the joint kernel if \(b+K+e+(p-1)\min(K,e)\le D\). The multiplier-side proof uses a rational \(p\)-basis; a finite field of size larger than the joint rank admits a good specialization. Preserve the forbidden weight kernel when descending. This does not justify changing source coefficient domains, nor does it lower the separate old-kernel-preservation and gained-class-killing costs in Pang's contraction step.

The organizing principle is multiplicative separation: one affordable, operation-compatible multiplier annihilates every actual defect while preserving a protected class. Compatibility, annihilation and survival are separate obligations. The foundational investigation contains the full reviewed arguments, source attributions, Braun/Pang comparison and falsifying controls; the following map is navigation, not a replacement for those proofs. User direction, 15 October 2026: preserve every bridge as the foundations for a general theorem covering Braun, Pang and future applications.

  • Compatible repair and cyclic modules. A linear map commuting with the allowed variable actions is multiplication by its image of one. This explains why one shared weight preserves an argument. It supplies no cheap selection theorem; the filtered action and any weight-dependent substitution must be justified.
  • Coding theory and error-correcting pairs. A locator kills unknown errors while retaining the message. Product orthogonality, error detection, rank and distance give an explicit constructive theorem and polynomial-evaluation application. The unresolved transfer is an independent quantitative bound for the actual joint error action; a faithful tester alone preserves its kernel and rank.
  • Frobenius and Poincare duality. A fixed perfect multiplication pairing converts a linear separator into a multiplier preserving the protected element. Complementary degree gives the cost. A pairing chosen from the desired answer is circular, and a perfect pairing can still require an unaffordable degree.
  • Geometry and localization. Clearing finitely many denominators produces a common annihilator with additive filtration cost when the protected element survives localization. Nilpotent multipliers can work even when localizing at them destroys the whole algebra; reduced support alone cannot control the required degree.
  • Homological algebra and the Koszul complex. The common annihilator is degree-zero cohomology, equivalently a cyclic-module Hom space. This identifies the object precisely; a bound on its first useful filtered degree or on protected survival still needs a theorem.
  • Field descent and the Galois question. Fixed linear kernel-survival problems descend across finite field extensions without increasing degree, by coefficient extraction. This needs neither Galois symmetry nor norms. Joint nonlinear choices of approximators are outside that statement and retain their own descent obligation.

Completed repair theorem. The composition theorem selects an exact weight from observations whose total rank is below the surviving capacity at the final cutoff. Freeze its coefficients, then apply finite-grid adaptive repair: over \(\mathbb F_q\), let \(\mathcal N_A(j)\) count exponent-box monomials through degree \(j\), \(\Delta=(q-1)dt\), and \(\eta=(1-1/q)\mathcal N_A(K-d)/\mathcal N_A(K)\). If \(b+K+\Delta+d\le D\) and \(M(1-\eta)^t<1\), a degree-\(K\) factor leaves \(wf\notin J_D\) and kills the residuals. Earlier \(wE\in J_D\) persists when \(\deg(wE)+K\le D\). Fixed-substitution replay costs \(b+K+TB\le D\); Pang's approximate-map replay has its own retained ceiling. Explicit dense inputs give a Las Vegas construction with at most \(Mt(\mathcal N_A(K-d)+1)\) expected sampled rank tests, plus matrix formation, stacked ranks and final solving. This is not an implicit-PC-basis runtime claim. The curve provider retains degree bounds \(e+g\) for a nonzero section and \(e+2g\) for a protected value, with rational-function vanishing only under the stated support condition.

Write \(J_D\) for the old bounded-degree consequence space, with paid multiplication closure. Actual defects must satisfy \(fe_i\in J_D\), while the protected multiplier class satisfies \(f\notin J_D\); every product must fit the same declared window. Braun uses ordinary affine restrictions and bounded cofactors, followed by a weight-dependent substitution. Pang chooses approximators and controls ranks of their error multiplication maps modulo PC. The full ideal of an inconsistent system contains one and cannot replace \(J_D\).

The first record proves the cyclic-map universal property, bilinear error recovery, marked Frobenius separation and precise localization/descent limits. In recovery, beta is bilinear, Q kills clean products, Q detects every possible nonzero error product, each error action has rank below the multiplier dimension, and nonzero multipliers are injective on the clean code. These are independently specified coding hypotheses, not automatically PC hypotheses. In Frobenius separation, the algebra and perfect pairing are fixed before the ideal and protected element; a degree-d protected class may need multiplier degree s-d. Nilpotent multipliers can be useful even though localization at them kills the entire ring. Formal nonzero, point support and rank of the listed errors do not replace paid class survival and rank of error multiplication. Existing colon and adjoint results remain the comparison baseline; a faithful tester preserves the exact kernel. No formalization is assigned.

Gaps identified by formalization

No formalization is assigned. Inherited PC degree accounting uses upper bounds for companion degrees; equality requires the original hypotheses. The main notebook records the partial downstream audit. No Lean verification of this new abstraction is claimed.

Research record

Compatible repair, error correction and multiplicative separation

15 October 2026 · Working proofs of established mechanisms and transfer limits · Common multiplier abstraction. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On effective-selection, effective-common-multiplier needs an independently checkable quantitative selection theorem recovering Braun and Pang and giving another useful application. The user asked for the hidden abstraction, including bridges to other fields. This entry tests coding-theoretic detection and Frobenius duality against that requirement. Success means an explicit existence or construction theorem with survival and costs; a common-kernel renaming alone does not suffice. This is a new investigation, not resumption of the interrupted main-notebook cycle.

General statement. The all-parameter claims are lem:cyclic-repair-map, thm:bilinear-error-recovery, prop:frobenius-marked-separation and prop:multiplier-transfer-limits. The first is the elementary cyclic-module universal property; the second isolates a standard decoding mechanism and proves the error-correcting-pair specialization; the third is the standard Frobenius double-annihilator mechanism with a protected element; the fourth records precise transfer limits and counterexamples. No historical novelty is claimed.

Record search. Searches for colon multipliers, common moment adjoint, Pellikaan, error locating/correcting pairs, syndrome multiplier recovery, cyclic module intertwiners, Frobenius double annihilator, Poincare duality and bilinear error detection were followed by the exact relevant records. Read prop:colon-multipliers with its full support-absorption entry, lem:common-moment-adjoint, lem:weight-compatibility-factorization, lem:source-compatible-cover, prop:relative-code-bound-refuted and prop:freeness-propagation. The first already gives colon transport and warns against a duality pairing chosen from the desired answer. The adjoint gives a pairing for fixed paid error maps, not contraction. The cover result already models source-compatible cyclic actions. Freeness propagation already uses a complementary-monomial Poincare pairing. The relative-code counterexample forbids replacing an ambient rank bound by a quotient-normalized one without proof. Graph queries for the colon and adjoint claims were checked; missing edges do not establish independence. The present proofs are self-contained; these records calibrate what the synthesis does not newly solve.

Compatible repair

Statement. Let \(R=k[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\), let \(M\) be an \(R\)-module, and let \(T:R_{\le d}\to M\) be linear. Suppose \(T(x_iq)=x_iT(q)\) whenever \(\deg q

Proof. Induct on monomial length, starting with one, and apply the assumed identity to its last variable. Extend by linearity. Conversely associativity gives the identity. The filtration assertion follows by the same induction. A map on \(R\) factors through \(R/I\) exactly when every \(a\in I\) kills \(f\). This proves all assertions. The claim concerns linear maps on the whole declared polynomial window, not an arbitrary nonlinear transformation defined only on the lines of one proof.

Meaning. A common multiplier is the forced form of a linear repair that respects every paid multiplication step in a cyclic model. Separate line-dependent weights generally lose this compatibility. For a fixed substitution \(\Phi\), \(q\mapsto f\Phi(q)\) is the same statement for the source action transported through \(\Phi\). Braun's substitution itself depends on the selected weight, so this observation does not turn his selection problem into one fixed linear map. A bounded PC consequence space is not automatically an ideal and is not automatically a module quotient; the finite-window operations must be verified individually.

Detection becomes annihilation

Statement. Let \(U,V,W,Z\) be finite-dimensional vector spaces over a field, \(C\le V\), \(\mathcal E\subseteq V\), \(\beta:U\times V\to W\) bilinear and \(Q:W\to Z\) linear. Assume (i) \(Q\beta(U,C)=0\); (ii) for each \(e\in\mathcal E\), \(\operatorname{rank}\beta_e<\dim U\), where \(\beta_e(a)=\beta(a,e)\); (iii) \(\ker Q\cap\beta(U,e)=\{0\}\) for each such \(e\); and (iv) for every nonzero \(a\in U\), the map \(c\mapsto\beta(a,c)\) is injective on \(C\). Given \(y=c+e\) with \(c\in C,e\in\mathcal E\), find any nonzero solution of \(Q\beta(a,y)=0\), then solve \(\beta(a,c')=\beta(a,y)\) with \(c'\in C\). These two linear systems recover \(c\) uniquely. If \(\operatorname{rank}\beta_e\le r\), the first solution space has dimension at least \(\dim U-r\).

Proof. By (i), the first system is \(Q\beta_e(a)=0\). Its kernel contains \(\ker\beta_e\), whose dimension is at least \(\dim U-r>0\). Every solution satisfies \(\beta_e(a)\in\ker Q\cap\beta(U,e)\), hence \(\beta_e(a)=0\) by (iii). Consequently \(\beta(a,y)=\beta(a,c)\). Existence of the second solution follows, and uniqueness is (iv). With bases and the bilinear map supplied, ordinary linear algebra constructs both systems. The theorem supplies no efficient method for finding spaces satisfying the hypotheses.

Error-correcting pairs. This is a self-contained derivation of the established coding-theoretic mechanism in Pellikaan's On the existence of error-correcting pairs, Definition 2.1 and Remark 2.2 (printed page 230), also attributed there to independent work of Kotter. Let \(A,B,C\le\mathbb F_q^n\), coordinate product \(*\), and integer \(t\ge0\). Suppose \((A*B)\perp C\), \(\dim A>t\), \(d(B^\perp)>t\), and \(d(A)+d(C)>n\). Set the minimum distance of the zero code to \(+\infty\). Take \(U=A,V=W=\mathbb F_q^n\), \(\beta(a,v)=a*v\), and \(Q(w)=(\langle w,b\rangle)_{b\text{ in a basis of }B}\), with \(\mathcal E\) the vectors of weight at most \(t\). Orthogonality gives (i). The image of multiplication by an error is supported on at most \(t\) coordinates, giving rank at most \(t\) and (ii). A vector in that image and \(B^\perp\) has weight at most \(t

Explicit degree budget. For \(n\) distinct field elements \(\alpha_i\), let \(1\le k\), \(t\ge0\), \(n\ge k+2t\), \(C\) be evaluations of polynomials of degree less than \(k\), \(A\) evaluations of degree at most \(t\), and \(B\) the orthogonal complement of evaluations of degree less than \(k+t\). Polynomial multiplication proves (i). A nonzero polynomial of degree at most \(rt\); evaluation is injective by the same root bound. The distance sum is greater than \(n\). Equivalently find a nonzero polynomial \(\Lambda\) of degree at most \(t\) and \(N\) of degree less than \(k+t\) with \(N(\alpha_i)=\Lambda(\alpha_i)y_i\). The true solution exists using the product of the erroneous-point factors; any solution has \(N=\Lambda p\) by the argument above (or the root bound), and division recovers the message polynomial. This is the familiar error-locator mechanism, not a new decoding algorithm.

What transfers. The useful additional structure is a tester with no nonzero low-complexity error in its kernel. Passing the tester then proves actual annihilation; a separate injectivity condition preserves the signal. The hypotheses are specified before the received word and do not assume its locator. This is stronger information than merely calling a desired multiplier a kernel vector. For polynomial calculus one would need an independently constructed tester on the actual paid error products that detects nonzero classes modulo the old consequence space, together with a surviving multiplier direction. Neither an arbitrary pairing nor a tester defined from the desired answer supplies that missing structure.

Duality produces multipliers

Statement. Let \(A\) be a finite-dimensional commutative unital \(k\)-algebra with a fixed functional \(\tau:A\to k\) such that \((a,b)\mapsto\tau(ab)\) is nondegenerate. For every ideal \(I\) and \(p\in A\),

\[p\notin I\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad\exists f\in A:\ fI=0,\quad\tau(fp)\ne0.\]

If \(A=\bigoplus_{j=0}^s A_j\), \(\tau\) is supported in degree \(s\), and each pairing \(A_j\times A_{s-j}\to k\) is perfect, then for homogeneous \(I\) and homogeneous \(p\in A_d\setminus I\) one may take \(f\in A_{s-d}\). This is standard Frobenius duality, formulated to retain the protected element. It is not an assertion that a prescribed bounded PC space has these properties.

Proof. If \(p\notin I\), choose a linear functional \(\lambda\) vanishing on \(I\) but not on \(p\), by extending a basis of \(I\) through \(p\). Perfectness represents it uniquely as \(\lambda(a)=\tau(fa)\). For \(e\in I\) and every \(a\in A\), \(ae\in I\), whence \(\tau(aef)=0\). Nondegeneracy gives \(ef=0\). Conversely \(p\in I\) would force \(fp=0\). In the graded case choose \(\lambda\) supported in degree \(d\), zero on \(I_d\), and nonzero on \(p\); the perfect degree pairing represents it with \(f\in A_{s-d}\), and homogeneity makes it zero on all of \(I\). The preceding argument applies unchanged.

Geometric content. Perfect multiplication turns a linear separator into an actual multiplicative separator. The truncated polynomial algebra with complementary monomial bases is an explicit source of such a pairing, already used in prop:freeness-propagation. The same algebraic structure is often called a Poincare duality algebra; no theorem about arbitrary topological spaces is needed here. The concrete model \(k[u,v]/(u^2,v^2)\), with \(\tau\) extracting \(uv\), has dual basis pairs \((1,uv),(u,v),(v,u),(uv,1)\). For \(I=(u)\), \(p=v\), choose \(f=u\); then \(fI=0\), \(fp=uv\ne0\). Its one-point reduced geometry loses exactly the nilpotent information that makes the multiplier work. Miles Reid's Chapter 6 notes, Example 6.8, give the complementary-monomial pairing; the proof above uses the explicit pairing hypothesis, not an inferred pairing on the proof-complexity quotient.

Transfer and its limits

Statement and proof: localization. Let \(M\) be a module over a commutative ring \(R\), \(S\) a multiplicative subset, and let finitely many errors \(e_i\) become zero in \(S^{-1}M\), while \(p/1\ne0\). The definition of module localization gives \(s_i\in S\) with \(s_i e_i=0\); then \(s=\prod_i s_i\) kills all errors and \(sp\ne0\), since otherwise \(p/1=0\). In a multiplicative filtration the degree of this construction is at most the sum of the individual degrees. No subadditive improvement follows. The empty family uses \(s=1\). See the definitions of module localization in Stacks, Section 10.9. In the preceding duality example, localizing at \(f=u\) gives the zero ring since \(u^2=0\); thus useful common multipliers need not be localization denominators preserving the signal.

Statement and proof: scalar descent. Fix finite-dimensional \(k\)-spaces \(U,V_i\), linear error maps \(T_i:U\to V_i\) and a forbidden subspace \(L\le U\). A finite field extension \(K/k\) contains a vector in \(\bigcap_i\ker(T_i\otimes K)\setminus(L\otimes K)\) if and only if the base field contains one in \(\bigcap_i\ker T_i\setminus L\). Expand a vector as \(\sum_j\theta_j f_j\) in a field basis. Each \(T_i f_j=0\) by independence of the basis, and at least one \(f_j\notin L\). The reverse direction is scalar extension. Coefficients stay in the same degree-filtered base space, so there is no degree increase. This is linear descent, requiring neither a Galois extension nor norms. It does not descend a simultaneously chosen nonlinear approximation family, and does not replace Pang's Frobenius specialization argument.

Statement and proof: obstructions. In \(A=k[x,y]/(x,y)^2\), take \(I=(x)\) and \(p=y\). Then \(p\notin I\), but \(\operatorname{Ann}(I)=(x,y)\), whose elements all kill \(p\). Indeed \((a+bx+cy)x=ax\), so annihilation forces \(a=0\). Thus linear separation alone does not imply multiplicative separation without duality or another mechanism. In \(A_m=k[t]/(t^{m+1})\), \(m\ge1\), take \(I=(t)\), \(p=1\). Here \(\operatorname{Ann}(I)=kt^m\), so every nonzero annihilator has least representative degree \(m\). All these rings have one-point reduced spectrum, yet this degree is unbounded. Even perfect duality gives no uniform cheapness. Finally on \(t\ge1\) distinct evaluation points, the one-row error data matrix \((1,\ldots,1)\) has rank one, but a nonzero polynomial vanishing at all points has degree at least \(t\), by the root bound. Low rank of the error data is not low rank of its action on multipliers.

Homological reading. For a genuine module and \(I=(e_1,\ldots,e_r)\), the first map of the cochain Koszul complex is \(m\mapsto(e_1m,\ldots,e_rm)\). Its degree-zero cohomology is precisely \(\operatorname{Ann}_M(I)=\operatorname{Hom}_R(R/I,M)\), by the cyclic-map proof. This is an exact organization of the problem, not a bound on the first nonzero degree or a survival theorem. No assertion about higher Koszul homology is needed. For the definition of the Koszul differential see Stacks, Section 15.29.

What Braun and Pang share

Both exploit the compatibility of multiplying every relevant line by the same object. Both must annihilate actual defects and preserve a protected class within a paid degree window. Their selection engines differ. In Braun's affine-exclusion construction, the multiplier lies in a row-linear space and vanishes under ordinary restriction to every proper high-rank zero flat. The common-kernel estimate makes this possible; bounded cofactors express it in each input ideal, and Boolean idempotence makes the weight-dependent substitution eliminate companion errors. The final degree budget is \(k(D+1)\). Hardness of every nonzero row-linear separator supplies survival, not merely hardness of one.

In Pang's construction, the defects are \(g_{L,i}P_L\) modulo the old bounded-degree PC space \(C_D\), and the approximation polynomials \(P_L\) are selected along with a surviving multiplier. The recorded correctness audit retains the source's rank-contraction and degree hypotheses. The maps count the ranks of multiplication by actual defects on the allowed multiplier space. They do not count the rank of a matrix listing the defects. Pointwise vanishing on solutions of an inconsistent system cannot replace membership in \(C_D\). The coding decoder instead has real evaluation coordinates, a tester excluding sparse nonzero errors, and distance guaranteeing message survival. These are different realizations of compatible repair, not interchangeable hypotheses.

New mathematics. This entry proves a self-contained bilinear detection/recovery formulation and its quantitative coding instance, a marked Frobenius separation criterion, and the stated transfer/obstruction package. The universal property, decoding mechanism, duality and linear descent are established mathematics or elementary syntheses; no new literature theorem is claimed. There were no numerical computations or experimental checks. The controls are exact all-parameter algebraic proofs.

Goal progress. The effective-common-multiplier implication remains unchanged: no theorem with independently verified quantitative hypotheses yet recovers both Braun and Pang. The constructive coding application is established, but does not discharge that transfer. Transfer is settled for the operations explicitly stated, not for every possible encoding. The next necessary issue is an independently proved advantage for the actual joint action maps and protected survival, rather than just a smaller representation of those same maps.

Degenerate cases. The cyclic theorem at degree zero says only that a linear map on constants is determined by one; for no variables the same holds. In recovery, zero error has rank zero and every nonzero solution works; a zero multiplier space is excluded by (ii), and a zero clean code is allowed with distance infinity. For duality, \(I=A\) and \(p\in I\), including \(p=0\), make both sides false; \(I=0\) gives ordinary perfect-pairing separation. Nonhomogeneous data are covered by the ungraded statement but not its degree formula. An empty error family in descent leaves just \(U\setminus L\), so the same basis argument applies. The degree obstruction excludes \(m=0\), where the ideal is zero and one is an affordable multiplier.

Nonvacuity. In the decoding statement take five distinct points over \(\mathbb F_5\), \(k=2,t=1\). Then \(\dim A=2\), the root bounds give \(d(A)\ge4,d(C)\ge4,d(B^\perp)\ge3\), so all hypotheses hold and one error is corrected. For duality the displayed algebra \(k[u,v]/(u^2,v^2)\) has \(I=(u),p=v,f=u\). For localization take \(R=k\times k\), \(e=(1,0),p=(0,1)\) and \(S=\{1,(0,1)\}\). The projection multiplier kills the error and retains the protected element. These are explicit witnesses, not reported computation counts.

Frobenius on either factor preserves the common kernel

15 October 2026 · Working kernel-compression proof and exact cutoff control · Two-sided Frobenius. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. On effective-selection for effective-common-multiplier, the coding-product queue head asks for joint action control beyond adding individual error ranks. Its computed spell is zero, with boundary four. Test whether one generic error combination captures the entire joint kernel in the actual paid quotient, and whether its cost can be improved by applying Frobenius to the multiplier rather than the errors. The quantitative selection theorem covering Braun, Pang and additional applications is still the necessary target; compression without an independent rank bound does not achieve it.

Owed attempt. The foundational entry and its queued sub-idea, the full common-moment adjoint proof, and the relative-code quotient-collapse obstruction were read. The direct attempt to compress the joint tuple recovers the already recorded Frobenius companion lemma, rather than a new rank theorem. Reading that proof exposed two different costs: Frobenius on an error charges its degree, while Frobenius on a multiplier charges the multiplier degree. The second route below gives an alternative paid window. The fixed-map matrix-blowup calibration was read too: changing representation alone retains the unknown joint rank.

General statement. lem:two-sided-frobenius-compression proves common-kernel equality under the smaller of the two Frobenius reserves, finite specialization and protected-weight recovery. prop:compression-buffer-necessity gives a proper Boolean pseudoideal where equality fails at the bare product cutoff. These are all-parameter algebraic arguments, not finite computations.

Two-sided Frobenius compression

Setting. Let \(p\) be prime, \(V_d=\mathbb F_p[x_1,\ldots,x_n]_{\le d}\), and let \(J\le V_D\) be a degree-\(D\) pseudoideal: linear combinations and multiplication by a variable stay in \(J\) whenever the resulting degree is at most \(D\). It contains the old Boolean or \(\mathbb F_p\)-field equations and their paid consequences. Let \(w\in V_b\), \(U\le V_K\) be an \(\mathbb F_p\)-linear subspace, and \(E_1,\ldots,E_m\in V_e\), with \(m\ge1\), \(b,K,e\ge0\). Put \(Q_D=V_D/J\), \(M_i:U\to Q_D\), \(u\mapsto[wE_i u]\), and \(M=(M_i)_i\). Assume

\[b+K+e+(p-1)\min(K,e)\le D.\tag{1}\]

Statement. Over \(F=\mathbb F_p(t_1,\ldots,t_m)\), the pencil \(M(t)=\sum_i t_iM_i\) has exactly the scalar-extended common kernel:

\[\ker M(t)=\bigcap_i\ker(M_i\otimes F),\qquad \operatorname{rank}_F M(t)=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathbb F_p}M=:r.\tag{2}\]

For any finite extension \(L/\mathbb F_p\) with \(|L|>r\), some \(a\in L^m\) has the same kernel equality. A uniform \(a\) has probability at least \(1-r/|L|\) of doing so. Let \(L_0=\{u\in U:wu\in J\}\) and \(H=\dim U/L_0\). Then \(L_0\subseteq\ker M\), and \(r

Scalar extension and paid products. For every extension, \(J_F\cap(V_d\otimes F)=(J\cap V_d)\otimes F\), by taking a basis adapted to the degree filtration. Consequently variable closure, and hence multiplication by any polynomial whose product degree fits the window, remains valid after extension. Every \(h\in V_a\) satisfies \(h^p-h\) in the domain ideal through degree \(pa\). Successive monic division by the univariate domain equations proves this: reductions never increase degree and the reduced remainder vanishes on the domain grid, so interpolation makes it zero. These facts justify all products below without replacing \(J\) by a full ideal.

Proof when the error is cheaper. If \(e\le K\), (1) is \(b+K+pe\le D\). Set \(A_j=\sum_i t_i^{p^j}E_i\). From \(wA_j u\in J_F\), multiply the completed relation by \(A_j^{p-1}\) and subtract \(wu(A_j^p-A_{j+1})\). Both operations fit degree \(b+K+pe\), and the subtraction is a domain consequence. Thus \(wA_{j+1}u\in J_F\). The same \(u\), including its rational coefficients, is retained at every step. The Moore matrix \((t_i^{p^j})_{0\le j

Proof when the multiplier is cheaper. If \(K\le e\), (1) is \(b+pK+e\le D\). Choose a prime-field basis \(u_1,\ldots,u_h\) of \(U\), and write a kernel vector as \(u=\sum_j c_j u_j\), \(c_j\in F\). Multiply \(w(\sum_i t_iE_i)u\in J_F\) by \(u^{p-1}\). Domain reduction gives

\[\sum_i t_i\sum_j c_j^p[wE_i u_j]=0\quad\text{in }Q_D\otimes F,\tag{3}\]

because \(u^p=\sum_j c_j^p u_j^p\) and each \(u_j^p-u_j\) has a domain certificate through \(pK\). All products fit \(b+e+pK\); \(w\) is never raised to the \(p\)-th power. Choose a prime-field basis of \(Q_D\). In any coordinate, (3) has the form \(\sum_i t_i h_i=0\), where \(h_i\in F^p\), since the coordinates of \([wE_i u_j]\) are in \(\mathbb F_p\). The elements \(t_1,\ldots,t_m\) are linearly independent over \(F^p=\mathbb F_p(t_1^p,\ldots,t_m^p)\). To see this directly, clear a common denominator in the \(p\)-th powers: monomials in the \(i\)-th summand have exponent vector congruent to the \(i\)-th unit vector modulo \(p\), disjoint for distinct \(i\). Hence every \(h_i=0\). Each coordinate now says \((\sum_j c_j v_{ij})^p=0\), where \(v_{ij}\in\mathbb F_p\) is its coordinate in \([wE_i u_j]\). A field has no nonzero element with zero \(p\)-th power, so \(\sum_j c_jv_{ij}=0\). This is \(wE_i u\in J_F\) for every \(i\). The converse kernel inclusion is a linear combination, proving (2).

The field-theoretic bridge. The multiplier-side proof uses independence over the subfield of \(p\)-th powers, a small instance of a \(p\)-basis. The definition and its differential characterization were read in Stacks, Field extensions revisited, Definition 15.47.1 and Lemma 15.47.2. The monomial proof above supplies all independence used here, with no separability theorem imported. The generic field is imperfect; no root of a rational coefficient is taken. Finite extensions are perfect and do not have that independence. Their conclusion comes from specialization of the already proved rank identity, not from replaying the independence proof there. This is distinct from Galois averaging or a norm construction.

Finite specialization and survival. A nonzero maximal minor of \(M(t)\) has total degree at most \(r\). For a nonzero polynomial of total degree at most \(r\) on \(L^m\), the number of zeros is at most \(r|L|^{m-1}\): induct on variables, writing the leading coefficient in the last variable, counting its exceptional fibers and using the univariate root bound on the others. Thus the minor is nonzero with the stated probability. Every specialization kills the common kernel, so its rank is at most \(r\); where the minor is nonzero, ranks and kernels coincide. For \(r=0\), every matrix and specialization is zero and the conclusion is immediate. Bounded multiplication proves \(L_0\subseteq\ker M\). Rank-nullity gives a useful kernel class when \(r

Precise improvement. For \(K

The bare cutoff fails

Prior control. The resemblance check identified prop:shortening-buffer-gap, whose full proof was read: an alternating-matrix construction already shows failure of row shortening at degree two on the full affine domain, and its degree-three closure removes the discrepancy. The smaller family below is an all-error-degree scalar-kernel control using a selected two-dimensional domain. It illustrates the alternative reserve and is not a discovery that the Frobenius buffer is necessary, nor a strengthening of that full-domain shortening obstruction. The nearest rank-retention statement, lem:binary-rank-retention, was also read in full; it uses the input-side reserve and Pang's specialization argument, without the multiplier-side bound.

Statement. For every \(e\ge1\), take binary variables \(x_1,\ldots,x_e,y_1,\ldots,y_e,u,v,z\), set \(X=\prod_i x_i\), \(Y=\prod_i y_i\), and \(D=e+1\). Let \(B_D\) be the Boolean domain-ideal part through \(D\), and set

\[J=B_D+\langle Xu+z,\ Xv,\ Yu,\ Yv+z\rangle_{\mathbb F_2}.\tag{4}\]

Then \(J\) is a proper degree-\(D\) pseudoideal. On \(U=\langle u,v\rangle\), weight one and actual errors \(X,Y\), the stacked rank is two but the generic scalar-combination rank is one. Thus a bare product reserve \(K+e\le D\) does not imply common-kernel equality, even for genuine Boolean polynomials. At \(K=1,p=2\), the new sufficient buffer is one degree above this counterexample.

Proof. Boolean reduction is degree-nonincreasing. The four extra generators have distinct squarefree top monomials \(Xu,Xv,Yu,Yv\), all of degree \(D\). No nonzero combination of them can reduce to degree below \(D\), and none can belong to \(B_D\). Hence every element of \(J\) of degree less than \(D\) belongs to \(B_D\); its allowed variable multiples remain Boolean consequences. This proves pseudoideal closure. Reduction also shows that neither one nor any nonzero linear combination of \(u,v,z\) belongs to \(J\). The two multiplication maps on \((u,v)\) are the row vectors \((1,0)\) and \((0,1)\), with output in the surviving line \(\langle[z]\rangle\). Their stack has rank two; their generic combination \((t_1,t_2)\) has rank one, with spurious kernel vector \(t_2u+t_1v\). All products used are of degree \(e+1\). The zero assignment satisfies the generators, so this is a satisfiable control, not a hard unsatisfiable instance.

What the extra degree does. In the actual degree-\(e+2\) PC closure of these same generators, multiply \(Xu+z\) by \(u\) and use \(X(u^2+u)\) and the original relation to derive \(zu+z\). Multiplying \(Yv+z\) by \(u\), and \(Yu\) by \(v\), derives \(zu\); hence derive \(z\). All displayed degrees are at most \(e+2\). The four error products then vanish. Thus the counterexample does not persist after the theorem's additional degree is paid. It exposes precisely a truncated action, not a contradiction with simultaneous diagonalization in a full Boolean function algebra.

Nonvacuity. For binary \(e\ge2\), take \(w=1\), \(U=\langle1,x\rangle\), and errors \(x\prod_{j=1}^{e-1}a_j\), \(x\prod_{j=1}^{e-1}b_j\) on otherwise disjoint Boolean variables, with only Boolean consequences through \(D=e+2\). The domain has dimension two, the common kernel is \(\langle1+x\rangle\), and joint rank is one; each individual map has rank one. This is an unbounded-degree example inside the new reserve but below \(1+2e\), the old sufficient reserve. It is an elementary shared-factor control, not a new source family or evidence of arbitrary-source coverage. Formula (4) is nonvacuous at every \(e\ge1\), with \(2e+3\) variables, and has the proved rank discrepancy.

Degenerate cases. Zero errors or zero \(U\) give rank zero. A weight annihilating the whole domain gives \(H=0\), so the strict survival conclusion is unavailable. Constant multipliers have \(K=0\) and the alternative bound is the bare \(b+e\); their coefficient separation is immediate. Constant errors have \(e=0\) and use the original-side bound \(b+K\). The empty error tuple is excluded for notation and trivially has all of \(U\) as kernel. Propriety is not required for (2), but is used in the counterexample and in nonzero protected classes. Non-prime-field base relations are outside the new multiplier-side statement.

Selection gain. No new joint-rank upper bound is obtained. The generic pencil equals the joint action in a cheaper independently verified window when \(K

New mathematics. The multiplier-side \(p\)-power argument supplies the alternative \(b+pK+e\) reserve and combines with the known input-side reserve by taking their minimum. Product-code growth still needs an independent estimate

General statement. No new registered all-parameter claim applies: this is a source comparison and unsuccessful transfer attempt. The source theorem and its direction

Mirandola–Zemor, Critical pairs for the Product Singleton Bound, was fetched as TeX and identified by title and authors. Read the theorem labelled thm:kneser, its support reduction and full proof, and the algebra/projector and stabilizer-decomposition lemmas. For nonzero subspaces \(S,T\le k^n\), with coordinatewise product span \(ST\), it states

\[\dim ST\ge\dim S+\dim T-\dim\operatorname{St}(ST),\qquad \operatorname{St}(C)=\{a\in k^n:aC\subseteq C\}.\]

The stabilizer has a basis of disjoint coordinate projectors; on full-support codes it gives the indecomposable direct-sum decomposition. The proof uses a product-space transformation, finitely many partition subalgebras and extension to an infinite field. Its finite-field descent preserves the stabilizer through its linear defining equations. These are actual coordinate products, not arbitrary quotient bilinear maps.

Beck–Lecouvey, Additive combinatorics methods in associative algebras, Theorem 4.1 and Lemma 4.2 with their proofs, were read along with Section 2's hypotheses. The base field is infinite and the unital ambient algebra satisfies \(H_w\): it is finite-dimensional, or a subalgebra of a Banach algebra or of a finite product of field extensions. Both input spans contain invertible elements; elements of the first factor commute, and its generated algebra has finitely many finite-dimensional unital subalgebras. No filtration cost is supplied. The coordinate-code theorem has a direct support reduction. Neither theorem says that the product dimension is small merely because the spaces have concise polynomial descriptions.

Three attempted translations

Full coordinate algebra. In a genuine function model, each error multiplication is a coordinate product, so a bound on a containing product space could bound its image. But the source theorem is a lower bound on that containing space, not the required upper bound. Reversing its use says that a small product forces a large stabilizer; smallness must still be established independently. In a proper truncated PC quotient, the full old coordinate algebra with all inconsistent old equations cannot replace the quotient: the full ideal contains one. The exact generic-pencil theorem does not create a pointwise model or supply a small product-space dimension. The recorded quotient-collapse counterfamily also forbids moving an ambient code estimate to the surviving quotient denominator without a proof.

Graded algebra. I reread the complete prop:truncated-stabilizer-algebra proof and its Boolean control rather than silently treating the truncated quotient as a ring. The graded envelope represents paid multiplication, but its positive-degree ideal is nilpotent and its idempotents are only zero and one. The marked old unit in a positive grade is not the algebra unit. Its one-bit control already has near-unit relative product growth and full stabilizer without a useful nontrivial selector; over an infinite field its subalgebra inventory can violate the outside theorem's hypothesis. Thus the standard graded envelope does not repair the required coordinate-code translation. This repeats the recorded control as a test of this source application, not as new mathematics.

Paid tuple module. The full lem:paid-stable-core proof already adapts the product-space transformation directly to represented tuple spaces in the actual Boolean pseudoideal. Its reserve \(K+3\delta\le D\) is independent of the number of transformations. It produces a stable core and a common eigenvector; cor:stable-core-source-exclusion then gives legal maps under the actual absolute-growth parameter \(q\) and its original replay reserve. This is a positive construction, not a failed ambient-ring analogy. The comparison therefore reaches an existing conditional theorem. What remains missing is a source-dependent bound on \(q\), or a useful selected character with affordable residuals. The later selected-core test and stabilizer-scope review were read: reducing the control space loses the uniform residual bound, while closing selected features under words recovers historical conditioning and its degree sum. No new estimate arises from relabelling that construction as a product code.

A common moment tensor detects the actual error kernel

15 October 2026 · Working all-prime moment-radical theorem and scope controls · Common moment radical. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Chosen. Develop idea:common-multiplier-duality before the coding head, on effective-selection and effective-common-multiplier. Its computed history has no development. The last comparison left the required rank estimate unchanged and named a scalar moment test as the next operation. The missing general selection implication still requires an independent bound, but first test a faithful affordable duality on the actual error kernel rather than a full algebra that kills the old unit. Success means a pairing or tensor with exactly the right radical at an explicit cutoff, constructed without assuming a successful multiplier.

General statement. thm:common-moment-radical constructs one normalized old annihilator and one scalar combination of each fixed error tuple so that a symmetric \(p\)-linear moment tensor has precisely its joint kernel. A finite family can share the same old functional. prop:moment-degree-limits records why the binary bilinear degree cannot be carried unchanged to odd primes and why generic diagonal detection cannot be transferred to a finite perfect field. These are symbolic all-parameter proofs; no computation is used.

Record search. Searches covered generic weighted moment form radical common kernel; symmetric pencil diagonal derivatives Frobenius nonsingular; common moment radical weighted scalar Gram; generic symmetric matrix derivative kernel diagonal characteristic two; moment pairing error degree two multiplier; all-prime rectangular pairing; multilinear moment radical Frobenius tensor; odd-prime square pairing; and weighted rectangular pairing common error radical. Read the complete proofs of lem:prime-retained-rectangular-pairing, lem:finite-family-moment-pairing, lem:common-moment-adjoint, and the current two-sided Frobenius compression. Read the exact perfect-field control prop:perfect-binary-moment-rank, including its warning about imperfect transcendental fields. Reviewed graph links for the pairing and adjoint were inspected. The proof below extends their Frobenius moment mechanism to fixed weighted error tuples; it is not a claim of a new general duality method or a rank-contraction theorem.

Common moment radical

Setting. Let \(p\) be prime and \(J\le V_D=\mathbb F_p[x]_{\le D}\) a proper degree-\(D\) pseudoideal containing the old Boolean or prime-field domain consequences within that degree window. Fix \(w\in V_b\), an \(\mathbb F_p\)-linear subspace \(U\le V_K\), and errors \(E_1,\ldots,E_m\in V_e\), \(m\ge1\). All are fixed before choosing the functional or scalar coefficients. Assume

\[b+e+pK\le D.\tag{1}\]

Write \(T:U\to(V_D/J)^m\), \(u\mapsto([wE_i u])_i\), \(N=\ker T\), and \(r=\operatorname{rank}T\). For a symmetric \(p\)-linear form \(B\) on \(U\), its radical means \(\{u:B(u,u_2,\ldots,u_p)=0\text{ for all }u_2,\ldots,u_p\in U\}\), not just the diagonal zero set.

Statement. Over any finite extension \(L/\mathbb F_p\) with \(|L|>2r+1\), there exist a linear functional \(\mu:V_D\otimes L\to L\) annihilating \(J_L\), with \(\mu(1)=1\), and coefficients \(a_i\in L\), such that

\[B(u_1,\ldots,u_p)=\mu\!\left(w\Big(\sum_i a_iE_i\Big)\prod_{j=1}^p u_j\right),\qquad \operatorname{rad}B=N\otimes L.\tag{2}\]

Equivalently the first-factor flattening \(U_L\to(U_L^{\otimes(p-1)})^*\) has rank \(r\). For a fixed finite family of such data over the same \(J\), one \(\mu\) works for every member, with separate error coefficients, whenever \(|L|>1+2\sum_a r_a\) and each member satisfies its own reserve (1). These are sufficient field sizes. The functional is chosen from the full old annihilator space independently of a prospective successful multiplier, though it may depend on the fixed error data.

Proof: the easy radical inclusion. If \(u_1\in N_L\), every \(wE_i u_1\in J_L\). Multiplying by the other \(p-1\) arguments costs at most \(b+e+pK\). Scalar extension preserves degree intersections and paid pseudoideal multiplication, so all terms of (2) vanish. Thus \(N_L\subseteq\operatorname{rad}B\) for every \(\mu,a\). Every product in the form is an actual polynomial inside the window; no multiplication on an unrestricted quotient algebra is assumed.

Proof: a generic form has no extra radical. Choose a prime-field basis \(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_q\) of \((V_D/J)^*\). Introduce independent indeterminates \(t_1,\ldots,t_m,z_1,\ldots,z_q\), let \(F=\mathbb F_p(t,z)\), and set \(\mu_z=\sum_\ell z_\ell\lambda_\ell\), \(E_t=\sum_i t_i E_i\). For any \(u=\sum_j c_j u_j\in U_F\) in a fixed prime-field basis of \(U\), domain reduction through \(pK\) gives

\[B_{t,z}(u,\ldots,u)=\sum_{i,\ell}t_i z_\ell\sum_j c_j^p\lambda_\ell([wE_i u_j]).\tag{3}\]

Indeed \(u^p=\sum_j c_j^p u_j^p\), and \(u_j^p-u_j\) is a domain consequence through \(pK\); multiplication by \(wE_i\) fits (1). Each inner sum in (3) is a \(p\)-th power in \(F\), because the displayed \(\lambda_\ell\)-values lie in \(\mathbb F_p\). The monomials \(t_i z_\ell\) are linearly independent over \(F^p\). Clear denominators in the \(p\)-th powers: their exponent vectors modulo \(p\) have one in the specified \(t_i\) and \(z_\ell\) positions and zero elsewhere, distinct for different pairs. Thus if (3) is zero, every inner sum is zero. Injectivity of Frobenius in a field gives \(\sum_j c_j\lambda_\ell([wE_i u_j])=0\) for every \(i,\ell\), and the dual basis then gives \(Tu=0\). A radical vector in particular has zero diagonal value, so it belongs to \(N_F\). Along with the easy inclusion this proves generic radical equality. In fact the diagonal zero set equals \(N_F\) over this imperfect generic field; this stronger fact will not be specialized.

Proof: finite specialization and normalization. In bases of \(U\) and its \((p-1)\)-fold tensor power, every flattening entry is bilinear in \(t,z\). Generic rank \(r\) gives a nonzero \(r\)-minor of total degree at most \(2r\). Properness makes \(\mu_z(1)\) a nonzero linear polynomial. Their product is nonzero and has total degree at most \(2r+1\). A nonzero polynomial of total degree \(s\) on a finite grid of size greater than \(s\) has a nonzero value, by induction using the univariate root bound. Select such a value and divide \(\mu\) by \(\mu(1)\). The minor stays nonzero and the easy radical inclusion holds at every specialization, so rank is exactly \(r\) and the radical is exactly \(N_L\). If \(r=0\), use the empty minor one. For a finite family take the product of one such minor for each member and the single normalization polynomial. The polynomial ring is a domain, so the product is nonzero despite sharing all moment parameters. This proves the claimed common field size and common functional.

Protected weights. Put \(L_0=\{u\in U:wu\in J\}\), and \(H=\dim U/L_0\). Paid multiplication gives \(L_0\subseteq N\). The form descends through \(L_0\) in every argument, by the same reserve, and its radical there is \(N/L_0\). A common surviving multiplier exists exactly when the flattening rank is less than \(H\). If a useful vector is found over \(L\), coefficient components in an \(\mathbb F_p\)-basis give an original-field useful vector in \(U\). This is an exact detection criterion, not an estimate that makes the rank small.

Binary interpretation. At \(p=2\), (2) is one symmetric bilinear moment form with precisely the actual error kernel as radical, at cost \(b+e+2K\). It uses one old functional and one error combination. The earlier common-moment adjoint uses unweighted input/output pairings through \(2(b+K+e)\) and supplies adjoint identities; this form has a cheaper stated reserve for kernel detection but supplies neither that adjoint nor its squared-operator formula. The prime retained rectangular pairing already uses the Frobenius mechanism at cost \(pK\) when no error weight is present. Here the error and weight are paid once, and the right-hand test space is generated by \(p-1\) elements of the actual allowed \(U\), not necessarily all of \(V_{(p-1)K}\).

Moment degree limits

Odd-prime bilinear control. For every odd prime \(p\), let \(D=2\), \(J=C_2(x^2-x,y^2-y,(x+y)^2)\), \(f=x+y\), and \(U=\langle f\rangle\). The three generators' quadratic leading parts are independent, since the coefficient of \(xy\) in the last is \(2\ne0\). Their span has no nonzero affine polynomial, so no nonzero legal variable multiplication is available through degree two; it is the actual PC closure. It is proper, also witnessed by \((x,y)=(0,0)\), and \(f\notin J\). With weight and single error both one, the actual action on \(U\) has rank one. But every old annihilator satisfies \(\mu(f^2)=0\), so its bilinear moment form on \(U\) is zero, over every scalar extension. Thus the uniform binary bilinear reserve \(2K\) cannot simply replace \(pK\) at odd primes. This is a satisfiable two-variable control, not a claimed sharpness theorem for every alternative rectangular construction.

Perfect-field diagonal control. For a finite perfect field \(L\) of characteristic \(p\), the diagonal of a moment form (2), on a base-field basis \(u_j\), has the shape \(\sum_j d_j c_j^p\), by the same field reduction. It is \((\sum_j d_j^{1/p}c_j)^p\), so its zero set has codimension at most one. If the flattening rank exceeds one, that zero set cannot equal the radical. The familiar binary special case is prop:perfect-binary-moment-rank, reread in full. Finite specialization in the theorem preserves the flattening minor, not the generic field's anisotropic diagonal detection. Testing only \(B(u,\ldots,u)=0\) after specialization is therefore unsound for finding a common multiplier.

Normalization control. On the one-bit Boolean quotient, \(U=\langle1,x\rangle\), \(w=E_1=1\), \(K=1,e=b=0,D=2,p=2\), a normalized binary functional has Gram matrix \(\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1&a\\a&a\end{smallmatrix}\right)\), whose determinant is zero for both \(a\in\mathbb F_2\). Over \(\mathbb F_4\), \(a\notin\mathbb F_2\) gives determinant \(a+a^2\ne0\). Thus even this old moment control may need a functional-value extension. It leaves source variable domains unchanged, and provides no nonzero annihilator of the unit error. The extension bound in the theorem is sufficient, not minimal.

Nonvacuity. Over every prime, in the one-bit Boolean quotient take \(U=\langle1,x\rangle\), \(w=1,E_1=x,K=1,e=1\), and \(D=p+1\). The joint action has rank one, its kernel is \(\langle1-x\rangle\), and that weight survives. Evaluation at \(x=1\) is an old normalized annihilator with \(\mu(x)=1\); coefficient one yields (2) with exactly that radical. Tensoring with any number of spectator Boolean variables preserves this witness. The odd-prime control has \(K=1,b=e=0\) but \(D=2

Degenerate cases. A zero error tuple, zero weight action or zero \(U\) gives rank zero and radical all of \(U\); no surviving conclusion is inferred when \(H=0\). Constant-domain \(K=0\) is permitted. Properness is used for normalization and would fail for \(J=V_D\). No unit is assumed in \(U\). The finite-family statement covers only fixed data specified before the functional; an unlisted later functional-dependent error choice is not automatically covered.

New mathematics. A weighted actual-error version of the recorded Frobenius pairing mechanism, with one common functional, exact radical, explicit finite-field bound and degree \(b+e+pK\); plus the stated scope controls. No literature priority, positivity or general rank-contraction claim is made.

Selection gain. No new estimate forces a nonzero useful radical. What is gained is a faithful moment-tensor representation in the stated paid window, without a full Gorenstein quotient or a functional chosen from the desired solution. All fixed maps and their forbidden weight kernel remain visible.

Goal progress. Unchanged at effective-common-multiplier. The construction settles a costed fixed-error duality interface, but does not produce the independent rank bound or adaptive approximation choices needed to recover both motivating constructions as one quantitative selection theorem. Bearing. No matched application at the motivating constructions' own full parameters is established in this cycle. The attempt could have supplied an affordable single-form representation for their actual errors; the proved result remains conditional on its displayed degree window and fixed data, without a completed Braun/Pang source ledger or a rank deficit.

Follow-up. Continuing the duality item. Next calibrate this interface against Braun's ordinary affine restrictions and its original cofactor/replay ledger, using the published restriction map directly rather than silently replacing ordinary polynomial vanishing by finite-point vanishing. Determine which part of the generic moment construction actually transfers and whether its independent rank bound is recovered without an extra source-degree cost. This is recovery of a motivating construction, not a claim of a new lower bound. The generic-minor criterion and perfect-field diagonal control remain safeguards; a selected singular pairing is insufficient.

The generic flattening has rank equal to the actual error rank, so a useful multiplier exists exactly when every \(H\)-minor vanishes as a polynomial in the full independent moment and error parameters. This is the rank/minor criterion applied to the proved theorem, not a new rank bound.
