Coefficient moments preserve Braun's ordinary restriction kernel

15 October 2026 · Working proof and source calibration · Ordinary restriction moments. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Chosen. Continue idea:common-multiplier-duality on effective-selection and effective-common-multiplier, in its second development of the four-entry spell. The necessary unification must recover the motivating constructions with their actual hypotheses and costs. The previous moment theorem asked: “Next calibrate this interface against Braun's ordinary affine restrictions and its original cofactor/replay ledger,” while handling fixed PC error data itself. This cycle tests that mismatch directly. The target is the published restriction rank estimate and replay ceiling, not another abstract form without its source ledger.

Owed attempt. The last entry asked to calibrate the moment interface on Braun's ordinary affine maps while preserving literal vanishing, bounded cofactors and replay. I read Section 5 of the publication in full, the row-linear definition and separation theorem with proof in Section 4, and the exact ordinary-kernel and family-exclusion records. The construction below uses polynomial coefficient functionals on the restriction targets, not measures on their Boolean points. The finite-point alternative was also checked against its already recorded Boolean-relative cofactor proof; it is not substituted for the published argument.

General statement. thm:diagonal-observation-principle isolates the finite-dimensional structural hypothesis behind the preceding Frobenius moment proof. cor:ordinary-restriction-moments verifies that hypothesis for ordinary polynomial restrictions and matches Braun's quantitative source ledger. The corollary recovers an existing exclusion, not a stronger lower bound or new proof-system theorem.

Diagonal observation principle

Setting. Let \(U,V\) be finite-dimensional \(\mathbb F_p\)-spaces, \(p\) prime, with a specified \(v_0\ne0\) in \(V\). Let \(\beta_i:U^p\to V\), \(1\le i\le m\), be fixed symmetric \(p\)-linear maps. Define their diagonal maps \(d_i(u)=\beta_i(u,\ldots,u)\), and \(C=\bigcap_i\ker d_i\). Assume

\[\beta_i(C,U,\ldots,U)=0\quad\text{for every }i.\tag{DO}\]

Every \(d_i\) is \(\mathbb F_p\)-linear. Indeed expansion of \(\beta_i(u+v,\ldots,u+v)\) groups mixed terms with coefficients \(\binom pj\), zero in characteristic \(p\), and scaling by \(c\in\mathbb F_p\) gives \(c^p=c\). Let \(r\) be the rank of \(u\mapsto(d_i(u))_i\).

Statement. For any finite extension \(L/\mathbb F_p\) with \(|L|>2r+1\), some \(a_i\in L\) and \(\lambda:V_L\to L\), with \(\lambda(v_0)=1\), give a scalar symmetric \(p\)-linear form

\[B(u_1,\ldots,u_p)=\lambda\!\left(\sum_i a_i\beta_i(u_1,\ldots,u_p)\right),\qquad \operatorname{rad}B=C_L.\tag{4}\]

For a finite fixed family over the same \(V,v_0\), one \(\lambda\) works for every member, with separate coefficients, when \(|L|>1+2\sum_a r_a\). If a forbidden subspace \(L_0\le C\) is specified and independently established bounds \(\operatorname{rank}d_i\le R_i\) satisfy \(\sum_i R_i<\dim U-\dim L_0\), then \(C\setminus L_0\) is nonempty over the original prime field. In a filtered application the cost of realizing each \(\beta_i\) must be supplied separately; abstract vector-space scalarization licenses no unproved multiplication or degree reset.

Proof. Choose prime-field bases \(u_j\) of \(U\) and \(\lambda_\ell\) of \(V^*\), and independent parameters \(t_i,z_\ell\). Over \(F=\mathbb F_p(t,z)\), use \(B_{t,z}=\sum_{i,\ell}t_i z_\ell\lambda_\ell\beta_i\). For \(u=\sum_j c_j u_j\), symmetry and characteristic \(p\) give

\[B_{t,z}(u,\ldots,u)=\sum_{i,\ell}t_i z_\ell\sum_j c_j^p\lambda_\ell(d_i(u_j)).\]

The monomials \(t_i z_\ell\) are independent over \(F^p\), by clearing denominators and comparing exponent vectors modulo \(p\). Their coefficients are \(p\)-th powers, so a zero diagonal value implies \(d_i(u)=0\) for every \(i\). Conversely (DO), extended by scalars, makes \(C_F\) lie in the radical. The generic radical is therefore exactly \(C_F\), and the first-factor flattening has rank \(r\). Its entries are bilinear in the two parameter groups. A nonzero maximal minor has degree at most \(2r\). Multiply it by the nonzero linear polynomial \(\sum z_\ell\lambda_\ell(v_0)\), specialize over a field larger than that degree, and normalize \(\lambda\). The elementary nonzero-polynomial grid bound proves existence; the radical inclusion from (DO) holds at every specialization, so a nonzero minor gives equality. Products of the minors prove the finite-family assertion. When \(r=0\), use the empty minor one. Finally \(r\le\sum R_i\), and rank-nullity gives \(\dim C>\dim L_0\); all these are prime-field spaces. This proves the selection statement without selecting a field-valued source assignment.

Why the structural hypothesis matters. It says that diagonal vanishing of the vector observations already kills their mixed products; it is verified from the native algebra in the applications, not assumed from a scalar moment. It is not automatic for symmetric forms. Over \(\mathbb F_2\), let \(U=\mathbb F_2^3\), \(V=\mathbb F_2\), \(v_0=1\), and \(\beta((a,b,c),(a',b',c'))=aa'+bc'+cb'\). Its diagonal map is \((a,b,c)\mapsto a\), so \(C\) is a plane, but \(\beta\) is nonsingular. A nonzero scalar multiple has zero radical, and the zero multiple has radical all of \(U\); neither has radical \(C\), even after scalar extension. This is the already recorded perfect-field moment matrix used as a control on (DO). The generic diagonal argument is used only over the imperfect parameter field. Finite specialization preserves radical rank, not the stronger diagonal-zero criterion.

Relation to the preceding theorem. In the paid-PC setting take \(V=V_D/J\), \(v_0=[1]\), and \(\beta_i(u_1,\ldots,u_p)=[wE_i\prod_j u_j]\). At \(b+e+pK\le D\), field reduction gives \(d_i(u)=[wE_i u]\) on base-field \(U\), and paid absorption proves (DO). Thus the common-moment-radical theorem, with its exact normalization and finite-family bound, is recovered. The abstract statement isolates that proof mechanism; it supplies no new automatic rank bound.

Ordinary restriction moments

Statement. Let \(U\le\mathbb F_p[x]_{\le k}\) be finite-dimensional and let \(\rho_a\) be finitely many affine substitutions into polynomial rings \(\mathbb F_p[y_{a,1},\ldots,y_{a,d_a}]\). There is a scalar symmetric \(p\)-linear coefficient-moment form on \(U\), over a finite extension of cardinality greater than \(2r+1\), whose radical is exactly \(\bigcap_a\ker(\rho_a|U)\) extended by scalars, where \(r\) is the joint ordinary restriction rank. It uses restriction products of degree at most \(pk\). In the binary published affine-family setting below, this retains Braun's common-kernel dimension bound and the original old-PC ceiling \(k(D+1)\).

Proof of the restriction representation. Set \(V=\bigoplus_a\mathbb F_p[y_a]_{\le pk}\), and let \(\beta_a(u_1,\ldots,u_p)\) have only its \(a\)-th component nonzero, equal to \(\prod_j\rho_a(u_j)\). The diagonal observation is \(\rho_a(u)^p\). Frobenius is injective on the ordinary polynomial ring: a nonzero polynomial has a nonzero \(p\)-th power, since its distinct monomials retain distinct exponent vectors and nonzero coefficient powers. Hence \(\ker d_a=\ker\rho_a\) over \(\mathbb F_p\), and their joint ranks agree. If \(u\) restricts to zero in every target, all mixed products containing it vanish, proving (DO). Take \(v_0=(1)_a\) when the family is nonempty, and apply the principle. The scalar functional is a tuple of polynomial-coefficient functionals on the restriction targets; it is not asserted to annihilate the old pigeonhole axioms. Empty families need no form construction: every \(u\in U\) is in the common kernel.

Matched Braun parameters and replay

Use exactly the publication's \(\mathbb F_2\) one-level complete affine source on \(m\ell\) bit variables, with \(m>0\), \(\ell\ge2\), \(n=2^\ell\), \(h=3\ell\), and \(M,k\ge1\). Retain old-only inputs, disjoint fresh coefficients, all companions and all fresh Boolean equations. Assume

\[k\le m,\quad m\log(4M)\le k^2,\quad D\ge2h+1,\quad 4(k-1)

and at most \(M\) blocks have proper input spans of rank greater than \(h(k+1)\). Put \(U=\mathcal L_k\), the ordinary row-linear space, with

\[H=\dim U=\sum_{j=0}^k\binom mj\ell^j,\qquad q=\binom mk\ell^k>0.\]

For each proper high block of rank \(r_a\), take its ordinary affine restriction to \(d_a=m\ell-r_a\) free coordinates. Its rank is at most \(\binom{d_a+k}{k}\). If any such block exists, \(r_*=3\ell(k+1)+1\le m\ell\). With \(d=m\ell-r_*\), the source calculation gives

\[\frac{\binom{d+k}{k}}{\binom mk\ell^k}\le\left(1-\frac{k}{m}\right)^k\le e^{-k^2/m},\qquad r\le M e^{-k^2/m}q\le q/4

For completeness, \(k!\binom{d+k}{k}\le(d+k)^k\), \(k!\binom mk\ge(m-k+1)^k\), and \(d+k\le\ell(m-2k)\le\ell(m-k+1)(1-k/m)\), yielding (6). If no proper high block exists, the joint rank is zero and choose \(f=1\); no estimate with negative free-coordinate count is used.

The observation form therefore has a nonzero radical. Its radical is the scalar extension of the original ordinary restriction kernel, so coefficient extraction gives a nonzero \(f\in\mathcal L_k\) over \(\mathbb F_2\), of degree \(s\le k\), with every required ordinary restriction zero. The source's affine coordinate argument supplies literal identities \(f=\sum_i a_{a,i}g_{a,i}\), \(\deg a_{a,i}\le k-1\). It does not supply merely pointwise identities.

The rest is the existing simultaneous weighted removal, with its ledger checked here. Low ranks \(r_a\le h(k+1)\) use basis packing with coefficient degree at most \(k\) and weighted Boolean companion certificates through \(s+r_a+1\le s+kD\). High unit spans use a constant first row. High proper spans use the literal cofactors, giving product \(1-f\) and weighted companions \(-g_{a,i}(f^2-f)\), certified through \(2s+1\le s+kD\). Fresh coefficient-field images cost at most \(s+2k\le s+kD\); used old axioms cost at most \(s+D\le s+kD\). Thus every primitive substituted PC step fits \(kD+s\le B=k(D+1)\). A hypothetical degree-\(D\) source refutation would derive \(f\) from the old bit base at \(B\), contradicting the published row-linear separation theorem under (5).

No additional source charge. The moment construction handles coefficient data of ordinary restriction products through degree \(2k\) in auxiliary free coordinates. Those functionals and extension-field coefficients are analysis devices, not fresh-variable substitutions or PC lines. The selected multiplier is returned to the original binary row-linear space before choosing any source coefficients. The actual coefficient maps remain binary old polynomials of degree at most \(k\), and the source replay ceiling remains exactly the existing \(B\). The auxiliary moment functional is not confused with the separate old-PC functional that proves survival of nonzero row-linear polynomials.

Unbounded original regime. The publication's parameter lemma, read with its proof, gives for every fixed natural \(a,A,d\), eventually with \(m=n+1\), \(n=2^\ell\), \(k=\lceil(\ell+1)2^{\ell/2}\rceil\), inventory \(M\le A2^{a\ell}+1\), and degree \(D\le A(\ell+1)^d\), all conditions (5) after raising a smaller proof ceiling to \(\max(D,6\ell+1)\). The increase is covered there by the constants \(A+7,d+1\). The moment calibration changes none of those parameters. This is an application to the actual published regime, not just a conditional inequality without an instance.

Ordinary versus finite-point observations

For two bits \(x,y\) in distinct rows, the row-linear polynomial \(f=xy+x\) has ordinary restriction \(t^2+t\ne0\) on the flat \(x=y=t\), but vanishes at every binary point of that flat. Thus replacing coefficient moments by binary point measures changes the kernel. This persists among high-rank blocks: for \(k\ge2\) and \(m\ell\ge3\ell(k+1)+2\), use the equation \(x+y=0\) and pin \(3\ell(k+1)\) other coordinates to zero. The resulting proper flat has rank \(3\ell(k+1)+1\), retains a free \(t\), and has the same discrepancy. The large-board regime just displayed has room for these pins. Literal affine-ideal membership would force the ordinary restriction to be zero, so this example has no such literal representation.

This is not a claim that pointwise affine vanishing can never support removal. The earlier lem:affine-common-vanishing-learning and lem:mixed-packing-common-vanishing-learning already prove a different route: after affine coordinate change and Boolean reduction, pointwise vanishing gives \(f-\sum_i a_i g_i\in I_k(\mathrm{BOOL})\), with \(\deg a_i\le k-1\). Their weighted companion identity pays for that Boolean residual and reaches the same hybrid \(k(D+1)\) ceiling. Those full proofs and their listed later relative-learning/fresh-block variants were read and their distinct fields, PC/NS scopes and costs compared. The current calibration preserves the published ordinary kernel; it neither re-registers the Boolean-relative route nor rules it out.

Nonvacuity. Ordinary restriction to a point or positive-dimensional affine flat satisfies the structural condition by polynomial-ring Frobenius injectivity. The asymptotic published regime above verifies nonvacuity of the complete Braun ledger at unbounded variable counts. The alternating-form control falsifies deleting (DO), and the row-linear \(xy+x\) control distinguishes the two notions of restriction before any rank comparison. No finite computation or extrapolation was performed.

Degenerate cases. A zero observation rank gives the entire domain as radical. The abstract principle assumes \(v_0\ne0\) only to request normalization; zero target spaces and empty restriction families are handled separately. Zero \(U\) cannot satisfy a strict survival inequality. Zero-dimensional flats have constant polynomial targets and cause no Frobenius exception. Unit-span blocks are not nonempty zero flats and use the source's separate cleanup; zero spans are low-rank blocks. The Braun application requires \(k\ge1\), whereas the abstract principle has no degree convention of its own.

New mathematics. A finite-dimensional diagonal-observation formulation of the common moment mechanism, with the exact hypothesis that makes scalarization faithful, and its ordinary-polynomial restriction application. Braun's rank estimate and exclusion are recovered unchanged; no new bound or historical novelty is claimed.

Selection gain. The ordinary restriction case now has a faithful moment representation with an independently proved rank deficit (6) and the matched original source ledger (5). The deficit is Braun's existing estimate, not a gain from scalarization. The general principle still needs quantitative rank estimates supplied by each application.

Goal progress. Unchanged at the full effective-common-multiplier statement until Pang's adaptive construction and the external application are checked against the same principle. The Braun calibration itself is complete in the stated source setting. Bearing. The matched ledger above recovers the published finite-parameter and polynomial-inventory/polylogarithmic-degree regimes without an additional old-degree charge. Not covered. A stronger Braun bound, arbitrary nonlinear or multilevel inputs, or Pang's full approximation-selection recurrence.

Follow-up. Continuing the duality item. Next test the diagonal-observation principle on Pang's actual adaptive error-selection recurrence and original degree window, separating what is only an analysis form from paid PC multiplication. The known error-correcting-pair application must also retain its constructive tester and signal-survival hypotheses. No generic moment rank identity is itself a source-wide contraction estimate.

Pang's adaptive rank estimate survives auxiliary scalarization

15 October 2026 · Working calibration and representation limit · Pang observation calibration. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Chosen. Continue common-multiplier-duality on effective-selection and effective-common-multiplier, its third development. The first development asked to “Next calibrate this interface against Braun's ordinary affine restrictions and its original cofactor/replay ledger”; the second completed that and asked to “Next test the diagonal-observation principle on Pang's actual adaptive error-selection recurrence and original degree window”. This has priority over the coding-rank head because a claimed unification must preserve both motivating arguments before seeking stronger estimates.

Owed attempt. Read Pang's primary TeX Theorem 4.3 first, then Sections 2–4 and Proposition 5.1 with proofs. Read the near-half notebook's complete binary rank proof, leveled selection proof and the newer derived-affine and weighted versions. Read the foundational bilinear recovery proof and the prior common-moment adjoint. The requested adaptive and coding interfaces are checked below. No numerical computation is needed.

General statement. lem:coordinate-radical-realization is elementary linear algebra showing that unrestricted auxiliary pairings always encode a fixed kernel, over its original field. cor:pang-observation-calibration preserves Pang's original all-prime quantitative theorem and the existing constructive coding application in that representation. It does not replace their rank estimates or create a new lower bound.

Coordinate radical realization

Statement. Over any field, let \(T:U\to W\) be a linear map between finite-dimensional spaces, of rank \(r\), and \(C=\ker T\). Choose \(r\) independent coordinate rows \(\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_r\) spanning its row space. For every integer \(s\ge2\),

\[ B_s(u_1,\ldots,u_s)=\sum_{j=1}^r\prod_{a=1}^s\ell_j(u_a) \quad\text{has}\quad \operatorname{rad}B_s=C. \tag{1}\]

Here the radical uses all choices of the other arguments. The construction is over the original field, with no positivity or polynomial multiplication assumption. It preserves the rank exactly and supplies no bound on it. With matrices supplied it requires only a row basis. For \(r=0\) use the zero form. For \(L_0\le C\), the induced form on \(U/L_0\) has a nonzero radical exactly when \(r<\dim U-\dim L_0\).

Proof. The row-basis map \(R:U\to k^r\) is surjective and has kernel \(C\). If \(u\in C\), every term in (1) vanishes. Conversely, for each \(j\) choose \(v_j\in U\) with \(R(v_j)=e_j\); then \(B_s(u,v_j,\ldots,v_j)=\ell_j(u)\). A radical vector therefore lies in every row kernel. The quotient and rank statements follow immediately from this equality. In particular \(B_2=R^{\mathsf T}R\) in input coordinates, and \(R^{\mathsf T}\) is injective. No extension field is needed.

Relation to diagonal observation. Over \(\mathbb F_p\), when \(r>0\), put \(s=p\), \(V=\mathbb F_p^r\), and \(\beta(u_1,\ldots,u_p)_j=\prod_a\ell_j(u_a)\). Its diagonal is \(R(u)\), because coefficients are in the prime field; its mixed products vanish on \(C\). Thus it realizes thm:diagonal-observation-principle, and the explicit coefficient sum already works over the base field, normalized at \(v_0=e_1\). At rank zero handle the zero form directly, or take \(V=\mathbb F_p,\beta=0,v_0=1\). This verifies the previous reviewer's auxiliary-coordinate suggestion, in a stronger elementary form. The output rows are chosen from the full map; they need not arise from one old polynomial functional, a fixed trace, an operation-compatible projection or a low-degree multiplication pairing.

Control. Using every row without selection is unsound over finite fields: over \(\mathbb F_2\), \(T:u\mapsto(u,u)\) has zero kernel but \(T^{\mathsf T}T=0\). Its independent row basis \(R=(1)\) works. This is the duplicated-output control already recorded for lem:common-moment-adjoint, not a new counterexample. More generally any prescribed subspace \(C\le U\) is the kernel of a quotient map and hence a radical in (1). Consequently existence of an unrestricted auxiliary form imposes no extra restriction on which common kernels can occur. A structural selection principle must constrain more than this representation.

Matched Pang recurrence

Use precisely Pang, arXiv:2610.00837v1, Theorem 4.3: Boolean variables over \(\mathbb F_p\), old degree-\(D\) PC consequence space \(J=C_D\), \(1\notin J\), \(S\ge1\) families \(g_{\nu,i}\) of degree at most \(d\), and integers \(1\le d\le K\le n\), \(t\ge1\). Put

\[\Delta=(p-1)dt,\quad \Delta+K+d\le D,\quad N_n(k)=\sum_{j=0}^k\binom nj,\quad H=\dim V_K/(J\cap V_K),\quad \eta=(1-1/p)\frac{N_n(K-d)}{N_n(K)}.\tag{2}\]

Calibrated conclusion. There are prime-field choices \(q_{\nu,j}\in\operatorname{span}\{g_{\nu,i}\}_i\) and \(P_\nu=\prod_{j=1}^t(1-q_{\nu,j}^{p-1})\) with the actual maps \(T_\nu:u\mapsto([P_\nu g_{\nu,i}u])_i\) satisfying \(\operatorname{rank}T_\nu\le H(1-\eta)^t\). Once these choices are fixed, their stacked map on \(U=V_K/(J\cap V_K)\) has an auxiliary symmetric bilinear form over \(\mathbb F_p\) with exactly its kernel as radical, and also a \(p\)-linear diagonal-observation realization. If \(S(1-\eta)^t<1\), that radical contains a nonzero class; a multilinear representative \(f\in V_K\setminus J\) kills every actual error \(P_\nu g_{\nu,i}\). All original degree bounds remain (2). This is a representation of the source theorem with its quantitative hypotheses, not an improvement of its rate.

Where the contraction comes from. For a fixed prefix \(P\) of degree \(a\), write \(Z_P(k)=\{u\in V_k:Pg_i u\in J\ \forall i\}\) and \(c_P(k)=\dim V_k/Z_P(k)\). These kernels respect degree intersections and absorb multiplication inside \(a+k+d\le D\). Their standard monomials form a downward-closed family of squarefree monomials. Counting consecutive layers gives \((j+1)b_{j+1}\le(n-j)b_j\), so

\[c_P(K-d)\ge\frac{N_n(K-d)}{N_n(K)}c_P(K).\tag{3}\]

On \(V_{K-d}\), define \(M_i u=([Pg_\alpha g_i u])_\alpha\). Their stacked kernel is \(Z_P(K-d)\): absorption gives one inclusion; the diagonal products \(Pg_i^2u\), multiplied by \(g_i^{p-2}\) and corrected using \(g_i^p-g_i\), give the other. Pang's multiplication-map Frobenius descent at \(a+K+pd\le D\) equates generic pencil rank with stacked rank. Its rank-specialization lemma gives a prime-field \(q=\sum c_i g_i\) retaining at least \((1-1/p)c_P(K-d)\). Its proof uses the nonzero maximal minor, whose rank loss at a point is at most its multiplicity there, and the grid multiplicity sum bounded by its degree times \(p^{s-1}\); it does not assume a nonvanishing evaluation on the prime grid.

The retained rank is the dimension of \((qV_{K-d}+Z_P(K))/Z_P(K)\). For \(P'=P(1-q^{p-1})\), bounded absorption preserves \(Z_P(K)\), and \(P'g_i(qu)=Pg_i u(q-q^p)\in J\) puts \(qV_{K-d}\) into \(Z_{P'}(K)\). Both operations fit \(a+K+pd\le D\). Hence \(c_{P'}(K)\le c_P(K)-(1-1/p)c_P(K-d)\le(1-\eta)c_P(K)\). At round \(j\le t\), \(a\le(j-1)(p-1)d\), and

\[a+K+pd\le(t-1)(p-1)d+K+pd=\Delta+K+d\le D.\tag{4}\]

Initially \(c_1(K)\le H\). Iteration proves the stated individual bounds. Every final error map kills \(J\cap V_K\) by the same paid absorption; their stacked rank is at most the sum, below \(H\) under the strict condition. Formula (1) now preserves exactly this already constructed common kernel. A representative may be multilinearized because its change and every error multiple of that change are Boolean consequences within (2).

Adaptive choices. At each round the current prefix and family are fixed before the rank-retaining \(q\) is selected. The final coordinate form is constructed only after all approximators are chosen. It is not used to choose them, and no source coefficient is extended. For the already proved binary leveled lemma, choose families level by level before forming one final stack: \(\Delta_i=\Delta_{i-1}t_i\), \(\Delta_{i-1}\le K\le n\), \(\Delta_i+K+\Delta_{i-1}\le D\), and \(\sum_i S_i(1-\tfrac12N_n(K-\Delta_{i-1})/N_n(K))^{t_i}<1\). Lower-level choices fix the next inputs, and the final sum of ranks is still below the same \(H\). This uses lem:leveled-common-multiplier with its imported premise discharged by thm:binary-approximator-rank; it is not a new all-prime layered theorem.

Native versus auxiliary cost. A native moment \(\mu(P_\nu g_{\nu,i}u_1\cdots u_p)\) invokes the preceding full-space moment theorem under its sufficient reserve \(\Delta+d+pK\le D\). Condition (2) does not imply this: already \(p=2,d=t=1,2\le K\le n,D=K+2\) satisfies (2) and violates \(2+2K\le D\). Formula (1) instead multiplies scalar coordinates of an already paid map; it is not a new old-PC product. Thus the auxiliary representation costs no extra old degree but does not establish a native normalized old moment at Pang's cutoff. This is a missing implication for the native route, not a proof that no alternative native construction can work.

Downstream realization. Pang's Proposition 5.1 uses exactly these original error memberships and a degree-\(K\) multiplier. For equation degree \(r\ge1\), \(d=(p-1)r\), and clause width \(w(F)\), its separate simulation condition is \(K+\max\{2\Delta+2d,\Delta+w(F)\}\le D\). The expansion \(1-P_L=\sum_i U_{L,i}g_{L,i}\) has \(\deg U_{L,i}\le\Delta-d\). Reading its topological induction checks input clauses, Boolean/true axioms, weakening/contraction, linear combination and multiplication at that ceiling; the empty line gives \(f\in J\), contradicting survival. The auxiliary form enters none of these operations. No change to the syntactic DAG, prime-field, Boolean-variable or node-size conventions is claimed.

Constructive coding remains constructive

In thm:bilinear-error-recovery, the observable map is \(T_y:a\mapsto Q\beta(a,y)\), for \(y=c+e\). Since \(Q\beta(U,C)=0\), it equals \(Q\beta_e\); injectivity of \(Q\) on \(\beta(U,e)\) gives \(\ker T_y=\ker\beta_e\) and equal ranks. Thus a row basis of the received-word map supplies (1) without knowing the error. The rank bound \(\operatorname{rank}\beta_e\le t<\dim U\) supplies a nonzero radical, and the separate injectivity of \(c'\mapsto\beta(a,c')\) recovers the clean message uniquely. One can solve \(T_y a=0\) directly; constructing a tensor is optional and yields no speedup.

For error-correcting pairs over any \(\mathbb F_q\), the recorded hypotheses remain \((A*B)\perp C\), \(\dim A>t\), \(d(B^\perp)>t\), and \(d(A)+d(C)>n\). Error support bounds rank by \(t\), the dual distance detects the error product, and the last distance sum preserves the message. The bilinear realization works directly over \(\mathbb F_q\). To place it literally in the prime-field diagonal theorem, restrict scalars to \(\mathbb F_p\); all dimensions and ranks multiply by \([\mathbb F_q:\mathbb F_p]\), kernels stay the same, and use the \(p\)-linear coordinate construction. The actual decoder stays over \(\mathbb F_q\), with the recorded two linear systems and cubic field-operation bound. Polynomial evaluation codes still use \(n\ge k+2t\); no decoding radius or algorithm is improved.

Nonvacuity. For every prime and any \(n\ge1\), take only Boolean axioms, one family \((x_1)\), \(d=K=t=1,D=p+1\). Then \(\eta=(1-1/p)/(n+1)>0\), the strict one-family condition holds, and \(P=1-x_1^{p-1}\), \(f=1\) kill the error while \(f\notin J\). This gives unbounded variable counts and fits the selection ledger. It does not fit the displayed simulation reserve; the Boolean-only control is not a proof-complexity lower-bound instance. The unrestricted coordinate lemma also applies to identity maps of arbitrary rank, where the radical is zero: it cannot force selection by itself. The existing polynomial-evaluation code instances verify the constructive application's parameter range.

Degenerate cases. Zero maps and empty observation lists give the zero form and full radical; zero \(U/L_0\) cannot satisfy the strict survival inequality. An empty polynomial family has no error condition and may use all \(q=0\). Properness is required for \(H>0\), not for coordinate scalarization. Constant inputs are allowed within the positive upper degree bound \(d\). None of the arguments replaces bounded PC by the full ideal of an inconsistent system.

New mathematics. An elementary coordinate realization and an exact calibration of Pang's adaptive quantitative theorem and the established decoder to it. Native multiplication moments and unrestricted auxiliary forms are now explicitly separated. No new rank estimate is proved.

Selection gain. Unchanged. The actual gains remain Braun's restriction-dimension estimate, Pang's normalized rank and kernel-enlargement argument, and coding support/distance bounds. An auxiliary radical packages all three without explaining a stronger common bound.

Unchanged at effective-common-multiplier: the motivating constructions and external application now share a faithful observation language with their original quantitative inputs, but unrestricted scalarization alone is the kernel reformulation the goal excludes. Bearing. It should test whether the current independent quantitative engines yield an adequate general theorem, or which structural condition beyond arbitrary coordinate pairings would actually predict a useful multiplier.

Effective repair needs quantitative certificates, not a preferred pairing

15 October 2026 · Working architecture review and scope controls · Repair architecture. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Question. Six research entries have preserved the same effective-common-multiplier obligation. They produced a constructive coding theorem, transfer controls, cheaper Frobenius compression, native moment forms and matched Braun/Pang calibrations, but no new selection estimate. Does the user's generalization require a stronger estimate, or a theorem composing independently certified mechanisms? The goal is a reusable tool explaining both constructions and future applications, not an unrestricted Frege lower bound.

General statement. The route seeks effective multiplicative separation: one affordable compatible multiplier kills the specified defects while its protected class survives. This review adds the standard Riemann–Roch consequence cor:curve-locator-budget and the elementary scope controls prop:repair-cost-controls. They guide the choice of architecture; they do not prove the full synthesis.

Obstacle

The faithful kernel representations preserve the answer but do not predict it. lem:coordinate-radical-realization shows that every linear kernel has an auxiliary symmetric form, while a native old-functional form needs additional structure and degree. Thus demanding a nicer form can be a stronger convenience than the actual selection goal. What is missing is a complete composition theorem whose quantitative certificates are obtained from the objects themselves, with common survival and all costs verified. The existing weighted adaptive lemma already supplies a substantial composition step; it must be used rather than rediscovered.

Architecture review

Necessary target. Give a finite, constructive calculus of sufficient conditions for a multiplier in a filtered commutative action, valid for every instance satisfying those conditions. Its certificates must be independently checkable structural data: ordinary restriction dimensions, a proved adaptive rank recurrence, or a faithful error tester and signal-injectivity bound. Recover Braun's literal affine vanishing and replay at \(k(D+1)\), Pang's \(\Delta+K+d\le D\) selection and separate simulation ceiling, and at least one distinct constructive use. State input representation and construction cost; no efficiency claim can hide the cost of finding a bounded-PC basis. Downstream. cor:ordinary-restriction-moments already supplies Braun's original exclusion, cor:pang-observation-calibration preserves Pang's common multiplier and subsequent simulation, and thm:bilinear-error-recovery supplies decoding. Those exact applications are proved; composing their structural certificates into one reusable theorem is still weaker than any new unrestricted proof-complexity lower bound.

Extra requirements. A single normalized native functional, a perfect multiplication pairing, arbitrary source choices, or a better rank estimate are not logically necessary for this goal. A stronger bound would be valuable, but the user also asked for generalization and future use cases. Conversely, a generic kernel form is insufficient even when beautifully expressed: its existence holds for all maps. The constructive coding import must retain attribution and is not a newly discovered decoder.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the preferred moment route, the sole medium reviewer received only the goal and bounded available-result statements. It proposed an effective repair calculus combining exact cleanup with adaptive contraction, normalized to the protected image and an additive degree budget. It explicitly rejected imposing an improved-rank requirement on the user's goal. Its proposal and limitation are in the first-stage record. The current route is now revealed for comparison: its native forms are useful representation tools, but the coordinate realization shows why they need not be the organizing theorem.

Rank normalization. Always measure remaining errors on the same protected image \(V_K/\ker(f\mapsto[wf])\). Exact cleanup may change the weight and therefore that denominator. The weighted adaptive lemma already uses its actual dimension. Relative conditional ranks, including the existing Shearer criterion, can replace absolute summed ranks only with their dependence hypotheses proved. Disjoint polynomial support does not establish them after total-degree truncation.

Decision. Develop the compatible-repair item next, with a lemma-first synthesis of certified exact cleanup followed by the existing weighted adaptive step. Keep the duality item and its narrower native question queued, but do not require a native form to complete the general theorem. The Riemann–Roch and overlap leads below offer independent structural certificates. They are chosen for the actual unification goal, not as a promise of a new Frege estimate.

Decisive test. Trivial cases. Duplicating an error or changing its presentation leaves the actual kernel and protected quotient unchanged; a certificate may become looser, but may not claim an improved mathematical answer from duplication. Zero errors require no repair, and an injective unit error cannot admit a surviving annihilator. Power. The weighted adaptive lemma already proves contraction after a supplied surviving exact weight; the coordinate theorem proves that scalarization adds no selection power. The narrow rectangle control below rejects a gratuitous one-functional requirement at an original Pang cutoff. No finite computation is needed. Attempt. Substituting an exact-cleanup weight into lem:weighted-adaptive-approximation proves the algebraic composition in a few lines, provided that weight survives at the final cutoff and earlier products still fit. Therefore this is not posed as an unanswered rank experiment: the next entry must write the complete lemma, its certificate extraction, the Braun/Pang adapters and costs, and identify precisely what remains beyond the existing lemma. Test disposition. New operation: newly adopted in this notebook, using an already proved composition; no new contraction claim. Operation. Compose certified exact repair with residual adaptive repair in one fixed final consequence space, carrying the actual weight image between them. Prediction. The existing weighted lemma supplies survival and residual contraction; failure can occur only at an unproved certificate, changed input grammar, or unpaid preservation/replay degree. A composition that merely renames supplied final kernels does not meet the endpoint. Record search. Exact cleanup weighted adaptive composition common multiplier recovered lem:weighted-adaptive-approximation, the weighted binary refinement, and existing source-specific shared-core and codimension-batch tools; no novelty is claimed for that algebraic step. This is a lemma-first synthesis task, not an empirical test whose answer is already known.

Curve locator budget

Statement. Let \(X/k\) be a smooth projective geometrically integral curve of genus \(g\), \(A\) a divisor, and \(E\) an effective divisor of degree \(e\). If \(\deg A\ge e+g\), then \(L(A-E)\ne0\). If a rational point \(P\) lies outside the supports of \(A,E\) and \(\deg A\ge e+2g\), there is \(f\in L(A-E)\) with \(f(P)=1\).

Proof. Riemann–Roch gives \(\ell(A-E)\ge\deg A-e+1-g\ge1\). Under the second bound, the dual divisor \(K_X-(A-E-P)\) has degree at most \(-1\), hence no nonzero section. Duality gives \(H^1(A-E-P)=0\); the point-evaluation exact sequence makes \(L(A-E)\to k\) surjective. This is a standard consequence of Riemann–Roch and the negative-degree section criterion, not a new curve theorem. Vanishing along \(E\) is vanishing as a section of \(\mathcal O(A)\); the representing rational function vanishes there when the supports of \(A,E\) are disjoint. Evaluation at the stipulated \(P\) supplies protected survival.

Nonvacuity. On the projective line over any field with distinct finite points \(\alpha_1,\ldots,\alpha_e,P\), take \(A=e\infty\), \(E=\sum_i\alpha_i\); the product \(\prod_i(x-\alpha_i)\), divided by its nonzero value at \(P\), realizes the bound. There are examples for every \(e\) over an infinite field, and over finite fields large enough to contain the named distinct points. For \(e=0,g=0\), the constant one works. At higher genus the stated degree bounds are sufficient, not asserted minimal. This is a function/section theorem; it does not identify a bounded PC quotient with a curve.

Repair cost controls

Native rectangle. For every \(n\ge3\), over the Boolean-only degree-four consequence space in \(\mathbb F_2[x_1,\ldots,x_n]\), take \(K=2,d=t=1\), family \((x_1,x_2)\), and any \(q\in\operatorname{span}(x_1,x_2)\). Let \(P=1+q\) and \(T:f\mapsto([Px_1f],[Px_2f])\) on multilinear \(U=V_2\) modulo Booleanity. No single scalar native rectangle \(B(f,h)=\mu(Efh)\), with \(E\) a scalar combination of the two written errors, full left space \(U\), full right space of degree at most \(L\), and reserve \(\deg E+2+L\le4\), has radical on the left equal to \(\ker T\).

Proof. If \(q\ne0\), the three possibilities are \(x_1,x_2,x_1+x_2\). A nonzero error component takes value one respectively at \((0,1),(1,0),(1,1)\). Varying the spectator \(x_3\) proves that its products by \(1,x_3\) are independent modulo Booleanity, so \(\operatorname{rank}T\ge2\). Every nonzero written error combination equals \((1+q)(a x_1+b x_2)\), of degree two, even over a scalar extension. Thus \(L=0\), and a single scalar rectangle has rank at most one. A zero combination has rank zero. If \(q=0\), its nonzero combinations have degree one and \(L\le1\), so rectangle rank is at most \(n+1\). A degree-two Boolean function killed by both \(x_1,x_2\) is a scalar multiple of \((1+x_1)(1+x_2)\): its function support lies at \(x_1=x_2=0\), and multiplying any nonconstant spectator term by that indicator has degree at least three. Hence \(\operatorname{rank}T=\dim U-1=n(n+1)/2>n+1\). Equal left kernels would require equal ranks, a contradiction. The original Pang selection window \(1+2+1=4\) holds. The result concerns the displayed single rectangle, not several functionals, a changed left domain, or arbitrary constrained pairings.

Artin–Rees delay. For every \(m\ge1\), take \(R=k[t]\), \(I=(t)\), \(M=R\), \(N=(t^m)\). The Artin–Rees equality \(I^nM\cap N=I^{n-c}(I^cM\cap N)\) needs \(c\ge m\): at \(n=m\) and \(c<m\), the left ideal is \((t^m)\) while the right is \((t^{2m-c})\). Conversely \(c=m\) works for every \(n\ge m\). Thus the theorem's eventual stabilization has no presentation-independent constant delay.

Observability horizon. On \(k^m\), let \(Ae_i=e_{i-1}\) for \(i>1\), \(Ae_1=0\), and \(C=e_1^*\). The first \(h\le m\) observations \(C,CA,\ldots,CA^{h-1}\) are the first \(h\) coordinate rows. Their common kernel has dimension \(m-h\), and it becomes zero only at \(h=m\). This is the sharp elementary shift control for the observability horizon; stabilization alone does not give a short degree budget.

Degenerate cases. The rectangle assertion excludes \(n<3\); its rank comparison uses a spectator. The zero error combination never increases rank. Boolean-only quotients are proper and contain the actual products through degree four. Artin–Rees at \(m=0\) has zero delay and is excluded only for a positive-delay family. Observability at \(m=1\) is settled by one observation. None of these controls refutes common multipliers themselves: for instance \(q=x_1,f=x_1\) is a surviving multiplier for the rectangle example.

Outside leads

  1. Riemann–Roch, Stacks Lemma 53.5.2. The curve-locator bound above gives a structural degree budget from defect length and genus, with a separate marked-value guarantee. It targets effective-common-multiplier through geometric certificate extraction and future error-locator applications. Answers. A faithful curve model would replace an unknown kernel dimension by explicit divisor data. Test. Passed: evaluate the projective-line locator at the defect points and protected point. Coordinatewise multiplication kills every error vector supported at the defect points and fixes the protected coordinate because the locator equals one there, with polynomial degree \(e\). Break point. The map into actual PC or ordinary restriction data must preserve multiplication, filtration and the protected class; no such curve model for arbitrary Pang errors is established. Develop the curve application as a concrete additional provider of repair certificates, retaining its genus cost and distinguishing sections from rational functions.
  2. Mayer–Vietoris, Stacks Lemma 29.4.6. The exact sequence for a scheme-theoretic union can subtract overlap from separate restriction dimensions. On \(U=\operatorname{span}\{1,x,y,xy\}\), restrictions to \(x=0\) and \(y=0\) each have rank two, but their joint rank is three because constants are shared. Thus the summed bound four misses the surviving \(xy\), while the exact overlap count detects it. Answers. Degree-faithful overlap certificates could improve the static provider without altering compatible repair. Test. Passed: direct coefficient restriction gives the stated ranks over every field. Break point. Exactness of the unfiltered scheme sequence does not imply exactness for an arbitrary selected low-degree domain. The full lem:degreewise-gluing proof and its recorded failures were read; any transfer must prove its degreewise ideal-sum condition. Develop that explicit condition, not naive inclusion-exclusion on arbitrary overlaps.
  3. Artin–Rees, Stacks Lemmas 10.51.2–3. Eventual filtration compatibility could bound the cost of lifting an annihilator from an associated graded module. Answers. An effective bound on its stabilization constant in a faithful model would control the extra multiplier degree. Test. Falsified for a uniform cost-free import: the \((t^m)\) control above has exact delay \(m\). The source proof establishes finite generation and chooses the maximum generator degree; it supplies no uniform numerical bound. This rejects that shortcut, not all effective Artin–Rees estimates or the existing localization bridge.

Absurd bridges

  1. Frustration-free quantum systems: Ambainis–Kempe–Sattath's subspace local lemma. Translate annihilator spaces into satisfying subspaces and normalized dimension into probability. Its primary asymmetric theorem and full dimension induction were read; imp:relative-dimension-local-lemma and the stronger cor:weighted-shearer-kernel already give the finite-field translation. Answers. Verified conditional-rank dependence can improve on a global sum of ranks. Test. Falsified for using disjoint polynomial supports as nonedges after truncation: on degree-one \(\operatorname{span}\{1,x,y\}\), kernels for \(x,y\) are \(\langle1-x\rangle,\langle1-y\rangle\), with zero intersection. This is the earlier prop:truncated-kernel-dependence, not new mathematics. The full tensor product restores independence; a changed domain must still pay its total degree and preserve the old quotient. The existing conditional-rank route, including its later failed projector-lift attempt, remains the baseline.
  2. Control theory: Dahleh–Dahleh–Verghese, Theorems 24.1–24.2. View defect observations and their propagation as \(C,CA,\ldots\); the multiplier should live in an unobservable subspace. Cayley–Hamilton makes the horizon finite, while the shift control above shows its exact size can be the state dimension. Answers. A structurally short observability index could bound the required propagation budget. Test. Falsified for obtaining that short budget from finite dimensionality alone. The source's theorem gives at most the full dimension, and the shift attains it. In a truncated polynomial model, the required globally defined action is itself an additional hypothesis. No real positivity or physical interpretation is imported into a finite field.

New mathematics. The standard curve-locator consequence and elementary cost controls make the review's transfer tests explicit. The native rectangle control develops the preceding reviewer's suggestion, including the zero-row case. The overlap and quantum comparisons are structural calibrations with their existing limitations retained.

Goal progress. Unchanged: this entry does not establish the proposed unified theorem with independently extracted certificates. The review removes an unnecessary stronger-rank demand and selects a synthesis that can meet the user's actual goal. Bearing. The matched Braun/Pang ledgers remain available unchanged, and the native rectangle control applies at an actual Pang selection cutoff. Not covered. A complete general theorem, a new source-wide contraction, or a stronger lower bound.

Certified repair composes exact cleanup with finite-grid approximation

15 October 2026 · Working proof with explicit structural certificates · Certified repair. Produced by Codex (gpt-6-astra, medium reasoning).

Chosen. Develop common-multiplier-compatible-repair on effective-selection and effective-common-multiplier, its first development. The scheduled review selected a lemma-first composition from structural certificates, rather than requiring a preferred pairing or an improved unrestricted rank bound. The missing implication is a complete costed construction recovering Braun and Pang and supporting a distinct use. The construction below separates exact certificate extraction, adaptive contraction, protected survival and final replay.

Owed attempt. The review's exact-cleanup-then-adaptive operation is carried out with all earlier products charged at one final cutoff. The known Boolean weighted lemma already gives that algebraic composition. The additional work here is an explicit sampling cost and an extension to finite coordinate grids over any finite field, using the normalized-matching theorem for products of chains. The input representation is stated; a bounded-PC basis is not assumed inexpensive in the original formula length.

Architecture test. Decided. The proposed composition works under its supplied structural certificates and joint ledger, as predicted. It does not automatically extract such certificates for arbitrary source families. The finite-grid and sampling statements provide a concrete generalization and construction guarantee beyond a scalar kernel representation. This answers the review's lemma-first operation; no new unrestricted Frege bound is claimed.

General statement. lem:rank-retention-sampling gives a checked Las Vegas choice rule for a finite-field matrix pencil. thm:finite-grid-repair extends the weighted adaptive mechanism from Boolean variables over a prime field to product grids over every finite field, with the appropriate rank profile and original-style degree reserve. thm:certified-repair-composition combines independently bounded exact observations with this adaptive provider while preserving previous repairs at the final cutoff. Novelty of the underlying linear algebra, product theorem or approximation method is not claimed.

The core abstraction. A common multiplier repairs a finite algebraic process on a surviving cyclic part of its target. Every approximate relation has a defect; multiplication by the same weight kills those defects while commuting with the permitted paid operations. Survival prevents the repair from being the zero map. The power comes from selecting that one weight by a quantitative dimension certificate, rather than multiplying a separate locator for every defect. Braun obtains the certificate from ordinary restriction geometry and then chooses a weight-dependent substitution; Pang first reduces the defect ranks by adaptive approximation and then selects the weight. The common algebraic structure does not erase this difference in how the certificates are obtained.

Rank retention sampling

Statement. Let \(k=\mathbb F_q\), with \(q\) any prime power, and let \(M_1,\ldots,M_s\) be matrices over \(k\). Suppose their generic pencil has rank \(R\), and that \(R\) is known or structurally certified. A uniform independent \(a\in k^s\) satisfies

\[\Pr\!\left[\operatorname{rank}\sum_i a_iM_i\ge R-\lfloor R/q\rfloor\right]\ge\frac1{R+1}.\tag{1}\]

Repeated independent draws, accepting only after an exact rank test, therefore terminate almost surely in at most \(R+1\) expected draws. At \(R=0\), choose the zero vector immediately. This is a bound in matrix-rank tests, not a promise that the input matrices or generic rank are cheaply available.

Proof of the rank expectation. Pang's rank-specialization proof works over every finite field. To make the extension explicit, take a nonzero maximal minor \(H\) of the generic pencil, of total degree at most \(R\). At a point \(a\), constant row operations make at least \(R-\operatorname{rank}M(a)\) rows of that minor vanish, so its multiplicity at \(a\) is at least that deficiency. For a nonzero polynomial of degree \(d\) on \(k^s\), the sum of point multiplicities is at most \(dq^{s-1}\). Here is the induction: write \(H(y,z)=\sum_{j=0}^tH_j(y)z^j\), \(H_t\ne0\); at \(y=b\), a lowest-degree nonzero coefficient of \(H_t(b+y)\), of degree \(\mu\), is the leading coefficient of a degree-\(t\) polynomial in \(z\). Thus each full multiplicity is at most \(\mu\) plus that univariate multiplicity. Summing over \(z\in k\) gives at most \(q\mu+t\); induction on \(H_t\), and \(\deg H_t+t\le d\), finish. The univariate case is the root-multiplicity bound. Averaging the determinant deficiencies gives \(\mathbb E\operatorname{rank}M(a)\ge(1-1/q)R\).

Integer acceptance bound. Write \(R=qv+r\), \(0\le r<q\), and \(b=R-v\). If acceptance has probability \(\theta\), the rank is at most \(b-1\) on failure and at most \(R\) always. Therefore \(\theta\ge(q-r)/(q(v+1))\). This is at least \(1/(R+1)\), since \((q-r)(R+1)-q(v+1)=R(q-r-1)\ge0\). The geometric waiting-time formula proves (1). For \(q=2,R=1,M(a)=a\), acceptance probability is exactly one half, so the bound is attained in a nontrivial case.

Finite-grid repair

Setting. Work over \(k=\mathbb F_q\). Fix nonempty sets \(A_i\subseteq k\), \(a_i=|A_i|\), on variables \(x_1,\ldots,x_n\), and their monic domain polynomials \(F_i(x_i)=\prod_{\alpha\in A_i}(x_i-\alpha)\). Put \(L=\sum_i(a_i-1)\). Let \(J_D\le k[x]_{\le D}\) be a degree-\(D\) pseudoideal containing all degree-at-most-\(D\) domain consequences. Thus \(u\in J_D,\deg u+\deg v\le D\) implies \(uv\in J_D\). An actual bounded-PC consequence space containing the \(F_i\) is an example. It is not replaced by an unrestricted ideal.

Let \(V_k=k[x]_{\le k}\), and let \(\mathcal N_A(k)\) count exponent vectors \(0\le\alpha_i<a_i\) with \(|\alpha|\le k\). Equivalently it is the coefficient sum through \(k\) of \(\prod_i(1+z+\cdots+z^{a_i-1})\). Fix \(w\notin J_D\) of degree at most \(b\), \(M\ge1\) finite tuples \(g_{\nu,i}\) of degree at most \(d\), and integers \(t\ge1\), \(1\le d\le K\le L\). Assume

\[\Delta=(q-1)dt,\qquad b+K+\Delta+d\le D,\qquad \eta=(1-1/q)\frac{\mathcal N_A(K-d)}{\mathcal N_A(K)},\qquad M(1-\eta)^t<1.\tag{2}\]

Statement. There are \(q_{\nu,j}\in\operatorname{span}_k\{g_{\nu,i}\}_i\), \(P_\nu=\prod_{j=1}^t(1-q_{\nu,j}^{q-1})\), and a grid-reduced polynomial \(f\) of degree at most \(K\), such that

\[wf\notin J_D,\qquad wfP_\nu g_{\nu,i}\in J_D\quad\text{for every }\nu,i.\tag{3}\]

For each tuple, its final actual error rank is at most \(h_w(K)(1-\eta)^t\), where \(h_w(K)=\operatorname{rank}(u\in V_K\mapsto[wu])\). Every previously proved \(wE\in J_D\) persists as \(wfE\in J_D\) when its actual product ceiling \(\deg(wE)+K\le D\) holds. The output is constructed over the original field; no analysis-field extension changes source coefficients.

Grid reduction. Successive division by the monic \(F_i\) expresses every polynomial as its unique reduced representative, with each exponent below \(a_i\), plus domain multiples of no larger total degree. Uniqueness follows by induction from the univariate root bound on the distinct points \(A_i\). In particular a polynomial vanishing on the grid is a domain consequence through its actual degree. For \(g\in k[x]_{\le d}\), \(g^q-g\) vanishes there and is a domain consequence through \(qd\). This replaces Boolean reduction, with its full degree charge retained.

Imported combinatorial input. A product of finite chains has the normalized-matching property: for a subset of a rank level, its upper shadow occupies at least the same fraction of the next level. The full product theorem for normal posets with log-concave rank numbers is read in Madiman–Wang–Woo, arXiv:1712.00913v3, Section 3.2, Proposition 3.1, attributed there to Hsieh–Kleitman and Engel. Each unweighted chain is normal with log-concave rank numbers, so repeated products give the stated input, including singleton factors. This published combinatorial theorem is imported, not reproved here; it concerns integer counts and has no field-characteristic restriction.

Normalized error rank. For a fixed prefactor \(A\) of degree at most \(a\), define \(Z_A(k)=\{u\in V_k:Ag_i u\in J_D\ \forall i\}\) and \(c_A(k)=\dim V_k/Z_A(k)\). In the window \(a+K+d\le D\), these spaces respect filtration intersections and multiplication within degree \(K\). Their standard monomials under a degree-compatible order form a downward-closed subset of the exponent box: domain leading monomials exclude \(x_i^{a_i}\), and bounded absorption makes the excluded set upward closed within the window. A level's excluded monomials have their upper shadow excluded too. Normalized matching therefore makes the fractions of retained monomials nonincreasing by degree. Cumulative weighted averages give

\[c_A(K-d)\ge\frac{\mathcal N_A(K-d)}{\mathcal N_A(K)}c_A(K).\tag{4}\]

This uses the full degree domain, not an arbitrary selected subspace whose rank profile need not be normal.

One adaptive step. Suppose \(a+K+qd\le D\). On \(V_{K-d}\), put \(M_i u=([Ag_\alpha g_i u])_\alpha\). Its stacked kernel equals \(Z_A(K-d)\): one inclusion is absorption; the other follows from \(Ag_i^2u\in J_D\), multiplication by \(g_i^{q-2}\), and subtraction of \(Au(g_i^q-g_i)\). Those operations fit \(a+K+(q-1)d\le D\).

For independent parameters \(\lambda_i\), put \(E=k(\lambda_1,\ldots,\lambda_s)\). The generic pencil \(M(\lambda)\) has the same kernel as the stacked map after scalar extension. Indeed \(g_\lambda=\sum\lambda_i g_i\) and \(Ag_\alpha g_\lambda u\in J_D^E\) imply \(Ag_\alpha g_\lambda^q u\in J_D^E\); subtracting \(\sum_i\lambda_i^q Ag_\alpha(g_i^q-g_i)u\) gives \(M(\lambda^q)u=0\), within \(a+K+qd\). Scalar extension preserves the degree intersections and bounded absorption: expand a degree-\(j\) consequence in a base-field basis of \(J_D\cap V_j\) before multiplying it. Corresponding minors of \(M(\lambda^q)\) are \(q\)-th powers of those of \(M(\lambda)\), so the inclusion of kernels is equality. The kernel is stable under coordinatewise \(q\)-Frobenius; the unique reduced row-echelon basis must have entries fixed by that map, hence in \(k\). Parameter independence then identifies it with the stacked kernel. This is Pang's descent argument with the field cardinality \(q\), not an assertion that an arbitrary extension-valued polynomial satisfies \(u^q=u\).

Consequently the computable stacked rank \(R=c_A(K-d)\) is the generic rank. By (1), choose an original-field \(q_0\in\operatorname{span}\{g_i\}\) with the rank of \(u\mapsto([Ag_\alpha q_0u])_\alpha\) at least \((1-1/q)c_A(K-d)\). For \(A'=A(1-q_0^{q-1})\), its kernel contains both \(Z_A(K)\) and \(q_0V_{K-d}\): preserve the former by absorption, and use \(q_0-q_0^q\) for the latter. Both cost at most \(a+K+qd\). Counting the gained subspace modulo the old kernel gives

\[c_{A'}(K)\le c_A(K)-(1-1/q)c_A(K-d)\le(1-\eta)c_A(K).\tag{5}\]

Iteration and survival. Start with \(A=w\). The kernel of \(u\mapsto[wu]\) lies in the initial error kernel, so \(c_w(K)\le h_w(K)>0\). At step \(j\le t\), \(a\le b+(j-1)(q-1)d\), and the maximum required degree is \(b+K+j(q-1)d+d\le D\). Thus each final tuple rank is at most \(h_w(K)(1-\eta)^t\). All final maps kill the same forbidden weight kernel. Their joint rank on its quotient is below \(h_w(K)\) by (2), giving a nonzero common kernel class. Choose a representative and reduce it on the grid; the changes to the weighted multiplier and every error product are domain consequences within the same bounds. This proves (3). The earlier-error assertion is direct bounded absorption of the already completed consequence \(wE\); it does not reweight its entire old derivation.

Construction cost. Given explicit dense polynomial coordinates and a basis/reduction map for \(J_D\), all maps above can be formed by bounded polynomial arithmetic and linear algebra. Each round computes its stacked rank once. Exact rank testing of independent coefficient draws finds its retained row in at most \(R+1\le\mathcal N_A(K-d)+1\) expected trials. Conditional on any previous choices this bound still holds, so all tuples and rounds use at most \(Mt(\mathcal N_A(K-d)+1)\) expected sampled rank tests, plus the initial stacked ranks and final kernel computation. Matrix dimensions and coordinate formation are polynomial in the explicit degree-\(D\) dense dimension, tuple lengths and degree parameters. This does not make them polynomial in \(n\), in a circuit description, or in the original proof size. Computing a PC consequence basis is part of the input-preparation cost, not free. Every accepted row and final multiplier is checked exactly, so randomness affects runtime, not correctness.

Certified repair composition

Static certificate. At one fixed final cutoff \(D\), let \(0\le b\le D\) and \(S\le k[x]_{\le b}\) be a supplied finite space, with a certified lower bound \(H_0\le\dim S/(S\cap J_D)\). Let \(\rho_a:S\to W_a\) be explicit linear observations with independently proved rank upper bounds \(r_a\), and \(\sum_a r_a<H_0\). Suppose that any chosen \(w\in\bigcap_a\ker\rho_a\setminus J_D\) admits a specified exact-repair construction: it freezes all earlier coefficient choices and yields actual old errors \(E_j\) with certificates \(wE_j\in J_D\). The observations may be ordinary polynomial restrictions, a faithful coding tester, or another proved provider; their implication to the exact errors must be established, not stipulated from the desired answer.

Statement. Such a \(w\) can be selected by linear algebra. If there is no residual family, take \(W=w\). Otherwise, when \(k=\mathbb F_q\) and the grid hypotheses and (2) hold for the frozen residual tuples, thm:finite-grid-repair constructs \(W=wf\) of degree at most \(b+K\), outside \(J_D\), killing every adaptive residual error. It also kills every earlier \(E_j\) whose certificate has \(\deg(wE_j)+K\le D\). If a fixed polynomial substitution \(\Phi\), possibly chosen after \(w\), has variable degree at most \(T\), a source derivation ceiling \(B\) replays under multiplication by \(W\) whenever all weighted image axioms have their certificates and \(b+K+TB\le D\). With no adaptive stage use \(K=0\). Construction cost is the static linear algebra and exact-repair algorithm, plus the explicitly represented adaptive cost above.

Proof. Rank-nullity gives \(\dim\bigcap_a\ker\rho_a\ge\dim S-\sum_a r_a>\dim(S\cap J_D)\). Therefore the common kernel contains \(w\notin J_D\), found by a kernel basis and reduction modulo \(J_D\), or by a separate injectivity certificate making every nonzero kernel vector safe. Freeze its exact-repair output before forming the residual maps. The preceding theorem supplies \(f\), and bounded absorption preserves the earlier errors at the displayed ceilings. For replay, linear combinations commute with \(F\mapsto W\Phi(F)\). A source variable-multiplication step becomes multiplication by \(\Phi(x)\); expand that polynomial into monomials and use the completed previous image line. Every intermediate product fits the original \(b+K+TB\) ceiling, using the actual degree of the source line before that multiplication. Constant or zero images only lower the degree. This gives the weighted derivation with the same fixed \(W,\Phi\), without assigning separate weights to its lines. The terminal image is \(W\) if the source terminal polynomial is one. This is the costed cyclic-repair principle, not a claim that every approximate interpretation is a fixed substitution.

Source adapters and a new domain

Braun. Use the published binary row-linear space \(S=\mathcal L_k\), its separation from the old \(J_D\) at the final \(D=k(B+1)\), and the ordinary high-flat restriction maps. The matched result cor:ordinary-restriction-moments supplies \(\dim S=\sum_{j\le k}\binom mj\ell^j\) and total restriction rank at most one quarter of \(\binom mk\ell^k\), under its full hypotheses \(m>0,\ell\ge2,M,k\ge1\), at most \(M\) proper blocks of input-span rank greater than \(h(k+1)\), \(h=3\ell\), \(m\log(4M)\le k^2\), \(k\le m\), \(B\ge2h+1\), \(4(k-1)<2^\ell\), \(2k(B+1)-1\le2^\ell\). The source is one complete old-affine level with disjoint fresh coefficient variables, all companions and all Boolean fields. Ordinary vanishing yields literal cofactors of degree at most \(k-1\); the existing low-rank packing, high-unit cleanup and high-proper cofactor construction produce the simultaneous substitution of degree at most \(k\), with weighted image certificates and replay through \(k(B+1)\). This is the static stage with no residual family. Its substitution depends on the selected weight, which is allowed because it is fixed before replay. No finite-point replacement is made.

Pang. Take \(S=\langle1\rangle\), no static observations, \(H_0=1\), \(w=1\), and Boolean coordinate grids \(A_i=\{0,1\}\) over the original prime field \(\mathbb F_p\). Then \(\mathcal N_A(K)=N_n(K)\), \(L=n\), and (2) is precisely \((p-1)dt+K+d\le D\) and \(M(1-\eta)^t<1\), with Pang's original \(\eta\). The one-step and final error statements reproduce Theorem 4.3. Its inference simulation is an approximate-map adapter rather than the fixed-substitution clause: Proposition 5.1 uses the same actual error identities at \(K+\max\{2\Delta+2d,\Delta+w(F)\}\le D\), giving the terminal \(f\in J_D\). That separate ledger is unchanged. The previously checked binary leveled choice order also remains available once earlier levels are frozen; no new all-depth source coverage is asserted.

Finite-grid application. The adaptive statement now applies directly to systems over \(\mathbb F_q\) whose coordinates have specified finite sets \(A_i\), using the product-of-chains profile rather than the Boolean binomial profile. For example, on \(\mathbb F_3^n\) it uses coefficient sums of \((1+z+z^2)^n\), with factors \(1-q_{\nu,j}^2\) and reserve \(b+K+2dt+d\le D\). This is a distinct native finite-domain repair theorem; it is not a claim of a new lower bound for such systems or a comparison with an unanalysed Boolean encoding.

Coding comparison. The structural pattern also retains thm:bilinear-error-recovery: the observable received-word tester has the exact error kernel, its rank is bounded by error support, and signal injectivity preserves the message. The original two linear systems and polynomial-evaluation budget \(n\ge k+2t\) remain the constructive application. This is an established parallel provider, not a new decoding algorithm. Curve locators remain a queued extension with their section and protected-value costs.

Nonvacuity. For the finite-grid selection budget, take every \(A_i=\mathbb F_q\), the domain-only \(J_{q+1}\), \(w=1\), one family \((x_1)\), \(d=K=t=1\), and \(D=q+1\). Then \(P=1-x_1^{q-1}\), \(f=1\) give \(Px_1=x_1-x_1^q\in J_D\) and a surviving unit, for every \(n\ge1\). The strict rank-sum condition holds since \(\eta=(1-1/q)/(n+1)>0\). This example verifies selection only. Braun's unbounded regime and its separate replay conditions remain those in the matched source adapter, not claims about this satisfiable control.

Nonvacuity. A mixed instance of the composition budget uses the Boolean-only \(J_4\), \(n\ge3\), \(S=\operatorname{span}\{1,x_1\}\), and ordinary restriction \(x_1=0\). Its rank is one and \(S\cap J_4=0\), so choose \(w=x_1\), which kills the exact error \(E_0=1-x_1\) through degree two. The residual tuple is \((x_2,x_3)\), with \(b=d=K=t=1,M=1\); its reserve is \(1+1+1+1=4\). Choose \(q_0=x_2\), \(P=1-x_2\), and \(f=x_2\). Then \(W=x_1x_2\) survives, kills \(E_0\) through degree three and both residual errors through degree four. Neither \(w\) alone nor the selected prefix \(P\) alone kills the residual error involving \(x_3\); the final multiplier is used. This is a concrete use of the known weighted composition, not a lower bound or a claim that every unweighted strategy fails.

Degenerate cases. Zero error tuples need no sampled row and have zero rank. At generic rank zero no random search is required. A zero protected image cannot meet a strict survival condition. The adaptive theorem assumes \(L\ge K\ge d\ge1\); an all-singleton grid is outside that stage and reduces to constant data. Singleton factors in a larger grid cause no problem. Static observations may be empty, as in Pang, and no adaptive stage is required, as in Braun. Two separately nonzero weights can multiply to zero; only survival of \(wf\) in the same final quotient is asserted. No basis of an inconsistent full quotient is substituted for the proper bounded consequence space.

New mathematics. A quantitative sampling rule and the finite-grid/all-finite-field extension of the weighted approximation mechanism, assembled with explicit exact-repair composition. The Boolean composition and source adapters use existing proofs with their costs unchanged. The product-of-chains theorem is an attributed import.

Selection gain. The gain is a proved selection theorem for additional finite domains and a checked construction cost, not a sharper Boolean/Pang contraction rate. Independent structural certificates are explicit; the theorem does not assume its final common multiplier. Its static rank input still needs a genuine geometric or coding bound, and its adaptive input needs the stated full degree domain and field equations.

Goal progress. Advanced through thm:finite-grid-repair and thm:certified-repair-composition. The notebook's sufficient-theorem goal is achieved as a reviewed working proof: independent structural certificates, exact source adapters, an additional domain class and construction costs are supplied. Bearing. The selection budget recovers Pang exactly and extends to coordinate grids; the source ledger retains Braun's original substitution and both original replay conditions. The composition budget preserves exact obligations during residual repair. Not covered. A universal characterization of cheap multipliers, automatic certificates for arbitrary nonlinear sources, new Frege lower bounds, or efficient construction from implicit consequence spaces.
