Joint-savings review: the coordinate count matches the kernel ceiling to first order

16 October 2026 · Review with a working proof (coordinate blocks only) · Joint-savings route review. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Question. Six research entries since the last review worked on effective-selection, the last four on joint-error-savings with goal progress unchanged. This review asks what the line has established about joint savings, what joint-error-savings still needs, whether the line's evidence can be falsified, and which test comes next.

General statement. The line's general claim is that joint error actions can be bounded below the sum of their ranks by structure of the errors, in the protected quotient, with a gain in a sufficient multiplier budget or admissible instance range. Established: thm:overlapping-curve-recovery (support counting in an evaluation model), lem:exact-common-kernel-selection (an exact selection criterion), lem:restriction-rank-overlap, thm:shared-span-affine-exclusion and thm:increment-count-exclusion (containment and increment savings in Braun's kernel step), and prop:common-kernel-ceiling (a ceiling on any certificate of that step at a fixed degree). This review adds prop:coordinate-count-window: for coordinate blocks the exact summed count reaches the ceiling's main term up to a factor \(1+O((r+k)/v)\).

Obstacle

Every saving found so far is either support counting in an evaluation model or domination: blocks whose restrictions factor through one coarser restriction, or a union of a few. The line has not produced a saving on a structured family that is neither, and its worst-case results do not decide whether one exists. For coordinate blocks the common kernel exists exactly when a row-linear monomial meets every pinned set; the exact summed count is a union bound for that event, and the coordinate count and the ceiling sandwich the threshold between \(-k\ln(1-r/v)\) and \(rk/(v-r-k)+\ln(k\ln(v+1)+1)\). At \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\) the window is about \(8.4\) in \(\ln M\), nearly all logarithmic; at the minimal degree \(D=6\ell+1\) the main-term part grows, to about \(341\) at \(\ell=32\), against a logarithmic part of about \(19.5\). So there is room for a saving below the summed ranks on unrestricted coordinate families: a correlation certificate could enter the logarithmic part, while the main-term part would need a certificate selecting exactly \(k\) rows; nothing in this notebook tests either. Pang's adaptive stage is untested too: lem:joint-word-shortening leaves its word-space dimension \(\dim Z_h\) open with only \(\dim Z_h\le\sum_ad_a\).

Coordinate count window

Statement. In Braun's setting (\(\mathbb F_2\), \(m\) rows of \(\ell\) bits, \(v=m\ell\), row-linear \(\mathcal L_k\) of dimension \(H\)), let proper coordinate blocks pin sets \(P_1,\ldots,P_M\) of bits to constants, each set of size at least \(r\), with \(x=1-r/v\), \(\rho=(m-k+1)\ell/k\) and \(\rho x>1\). Then each restriction rank is at most \(H\,x^k\,\rho x/(\rho x-1)\), so if

\[M\,x^k\,\frac{\rho x}{\rho x-1}<1,\quad\text{that is}\quad\ln M<-k\ln x-\ln\frac{\rho x}{\rho x-1},\tag{1}\]

the summed exact ranks are below \(H\) and a nonzero \(f\in\mathcal L_k\) has zero restriction to every flat. Here \(rk/v\le-k\ln x\le rk/(v-r)\). By prop:common-kernel-ceiling, at the same \(k\) and \(r\) some family with \(\ln M\le rk/(v-r-k)+\ln(k\ln(v+1)+1)\) has no such \(f\). The main terms \(-k\ln x\) and \(rk/(v-r-k)\) agree to a factor \(1+O((r+k)/v)\); their difference is about \((rk/v)(r/(2v)+k/v)\).

Proof. A pin \(x_i=1\) becomes a pin to zero under the translation \(x_i\mapsto x_i+1\), which preserves row-linearity and degree, so assume all pins are to zero. As in the ceiling proof, the restriction to \(x_P=0\) then has rank equal to the number of row-linear monomials of degree at most \(k\) avoiding \(P\). With \(\ell_i'=\ell-|P\cap\text{row }i|\), the number of degree \(j\) is \(e_j(\ell_1',\ldots,\ell_m')\). Maclaurin's inequality for nonnegative reals, \((e_j/\binom mj)^{1/j}\le e_1/m\), gives \(e_j\le\binom mj\bar\ell^{\,j}\) with \(\bar\ell=\ell-|P|/m\le\ell x\). Put \(a_j=\binom mj\ell^j\); then \(a_{j+1}/a_j=(m-j)\ell/(j+1)\ge\rho\) for \(j<k\), so \(a_j\le a_k\rho^{-(k-j)}\), and the rank is at most \(\sum_{j\le k}a_jx^j\le a_kx^k\sum_{i\ge0}(\rho x)^{-i}=a_kx^k\rho x/(\rho x-1)\le Hx^k\rho x/(\rho x-1)\). Summing over the blocks and using rank–nullity on the stacked map gives (1). Finally \(y\le-\ln(1-y)\le y/(1-y)\) at \(y=r/v\), and \(rk/(v-r)\le rk/(v-r-k)\), so the certified range lies below the ceiling at every admissible parameter.

Numbers. The saved check coordinate_window.json gives, at \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\), \(k=K_D=303\), \(r=r_D=14593\): \(\rho x\approx3397\), certified \(\ln M<4.2461\), ceiling family at \(12.62\) with main term \(4.2775\), published reach \(1.40\). The sweep coordinate_window_sweep.json at the minimal degree \(D=6\ell+1\) gives main-term gaps \(0.04\), \(0.34\), \(3.16\), \(31.8\), \(341\) at \(\ell=16,20,24,28,32\), against logarithmic terms \(8.4\), \(11.2\), \(14.0\), \(16.7\), \(19.5\). For coordinate blocks the published count's loss against the ceiling is in its single-flat estimate; for Braun's general affine flats this is not shown.

Nonvacuity. The window ledger is realized at \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\) by any \(M\le69\) coordinate blocks of rank \(r_D\), for example pinning disjoint or overlapping sets of \(14593\) bits; \(e^{4.246}\approx69.8\), and the hypotheses \(\rho x>1\) and \(r+k<v\) are checked in the script.

Degenerate cases. \(M=0\) needs nothing. If \(r\ge v\) no proper coordinate block of that size exists. If \(\rho x\le1\) the geometric bound is void and the statement says nothing. Duplicated blocks count twice in (1), as in any summed count.

Architecture review

Necessary target. For joint-error-savings: some independently checkable structural hypothesis on actual errors, an all-parameter bound on their joint action strictly below the summed ranks on a nontrivial unbounded family, in one protected quotient, with a gain in a sufficient multiplier budget or admissible instance range after all composition costs; duplication, coordinate changes and re-presentations of one kernel excluded. Downstream. Through lem:exact-common-kernel-selection, any certificate of the exact stacked criterion feeds thm:certified-repair-composition at unchanged later cost; through thm:shared-span-affine-exclusion it already enlarges Braun's admissible range for families clustered by shared spans. That consequence is an exclusion for a structured class of affine families; whether it meets the statement's wording, or only a domination case the reviewers have twice judged too close to re-presenting one kernel, is the open question of this line.

Extra requirements. The line has demanded a saving that is not domination. That demand is not in the open statement's words; it came from the reviewers' reading. The worst-case results do not show it unattainable on structured families, and the window above shows room even on unrestricted coordinate families. Phrased by containment it could never be met on coordinate families, because every common kernel there is spanned by monomials meeting every pinned set and so yields a cover afterwards. The demand is therefore stated in terms of the certificate: its hypothesis is checkable without being given a cover, and it does not follow from the cover bound \(\sum_cr(K_c)<H\) of lem:restriction-rank-overlap for any cover the hypothesis supplies.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the route, the medium reviewer received the goal, the open statements and the registered result statements only (review record). It proposed probabilistic existence certificates for the coordinate kernel, joint savings in Pang's adaptive stage, and several glued multipliers, ranking Pang's stage first. Compared with the route, which tested only Braun's kernel step: the symmetric local lemma is falsified below for that step; Pang's stage remains untested, since \(\dim Z_h\) is open and the innovation codimension of lem:conditional-seed-contraction measures per-round contraction, not the word space; gluing pays a degree shift linear in the number of clusters. In the second stage the same reviewer proposed a correlation (Harris) certificate on coordinate families, adopted as the decisive test below.

Rank normalization. All ranks in this line are measured in one fixed space: ordinary restriction images of \(\mathcal L_k\), or the evaluation quotient of the curve model. A correlation certificate is a relative statement: it bounds the probability that a random monomial meets every pinned set, not a sum of absolute ranks; it must be compared with the summed count in the same space, \(\mathcal L_k\) at one \(k\).

Decision. Keep joint-error-savings open, with the non-domination requirement stated in terms of the certificate, and keep the route on it. The next entry is lemma-first: prove the correlation (Harris) certificate for coordinate kernels, whose missing steps the reviewer supplied, and evaluate it across the sweep. It targets the logarithmic part of the window; the growing main-term part would need a certificate selecting exactly \(k\) rows. Pang's word-space dimension and the affine-flat extension of the coordinate count stay queued.

Decisive test. Trivial cases. Duplicated pinned sets change neither the event “a monomial meets every set” nor a certificate's product over distinct events; a translation of pins does not change the event; one pinned set needs no certificate. Power. The summed count certifies \(\ln M<-k\ln x\) by (1); the ceiling forbids beyond \(rk/(v-r-k)+\ln(k\ln(v+1)+1)\); at \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\) these are \(4.25\) and \(12.62\). In the Bernoulli row-selection model the chance of avoiding a pinned set is about \(e^{-\mu r/v}\), so the certificate's main term is at most \(kr/v\), below the count's \(-k\ln x\) by about \((kr/v)(r/2v)\), and it gains only an additive term of about \(\ln k\); by the sweep's figures that loss exceeds the gain at minimal degree from \(\ell=28\) on. Attempt. Fix one bit \(b_i\) per row and select each row independently with probability \(\pi=\mu/m\); the selected bits form a row-linear monomial. Given \(b\), each event “the monomial meets \(P_a\)” is increasing in the selection indicators, so Harris's inequality gives \(\Pr[\text{all meet}\mid b]\ge\prod_a(1-(1-\pi)^{c_a(b)})\), with \(c_a(b)\) the number of rows whose chosen bit lies in \(P_a\). Writing \(\varphi(c)=-\ln(1-(1-\pi)^c)=\sum_{j\ge1}(1-\pi)^{cj}/j\), the generating function of the independent Bernoulli\((s_i)\) indicators gives \(E_b(1-\pi)^{c_aj}\le\exp(-(1-(1-\pi)^j)|P_a|/\ell)\); a Chernoff lower tail makes every \(c_a\ge r/(2\ell)\) except with probability at most \(Me^{-r/(8\ell)}\); and \(\Pr[\mathrm{Bin}(m,\pi)>k]\le e^{-\mu h(k/\mu)}\), \(h(t)=t\ln t-t+1\). The remaining step is the sum over large \(j\) on the good event, which this review does not write. Test disposition. New operation. Operation. Prove the certificate: some \(b\) gives \(\sum_a\varphi(c_a(b))\le(1+o(1))M(-\ln(1-e^{-\mu r/v}))\), so a common kernel exists when this is below the selection mass beyond the degree tail; evaluate it with the optimal \(\mu\) at \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\) and across the minimal-degree sweep. Prediction. It beats the summed count where \((kr/v)(r/2v)<\ln k\) roughly, as at \(\ell=16\) with the reviewer's estimate \(\ln M<6.1\) against \(4.25\), and falls below it at \(\ell\ge28\) at minimal degree. A win would be a joint saving whose hypothesis needs no cover. If the large-\(j\) sum loses a factor of order \(k\), the certificate collapses to the union bound. Record search. A search for Harris and FKG correlation inequalities with random monomials, increasing events and kernels returned, in the near-half-pc-degree notebook, prop:same-sign-saddle-bound (biased product measures of sets containing no member of a family) and lem:avoiding-sets-fraction (fractions of \(j\)-sets avoiding a family), which bear on the same event; the next entry reads both before proving anything.

A note for effective-annihilator-degree

A first draft of this review posed a Castelnuovo–Mumford regularity test for the third open statement. The reviewer answered its stopping step from the cited statements, and the author checked the answer: \(k[x]/(x_i^2-x_i)\cong k^{2^n}\), so every ideal quotient of the Boolean ring is a product of copies of \(k\), and a defect that is a genuine ideal there is a reduced set of cube points, with no nilpotents and filtration degree equal to multilinear degree. Nilpotent behaviour arises only modulo a degree-\(D\) pseudoideal, which defines no scheme; and an unsatisfiable source system generates the unit ideal, so its scheme is empty. A regularity bound therefore needs a pseudoideal analogue, such as the Hilbert function of the truncation, or a non-Boolean encoding. This is recorded for that statement's development and is not a test of this route.

Outside leads

  • Maclaurin's inequality for elementary symmetric means. It targets joint-error-savings by sharpening single-flat ranks in Braun's count. Answers. It separates the published count's loss into single-flat looseness and a genuine joint window, so that any claimed joint saving is measured against the right baseline. Test. Passed: prop:coordinate-count-window above, proved with the inequality in its standard form \((e_j/\binom mj)^{1/j}\le e_1/m\) for nonnegative reals. Break point. Non-coordinate flats, where the restriction image has no monomial basis; the queued development is the affine-flat extension.
  • The elementary greedy transversal bound. It targets joint-error-savings on coordinate families. Answers. Whether a covering argument certifies the kernel beyond the summed count. Test. Falsified as an improvement: if every pinned set has at least \(r\) bits and at most one bit per row is chosen, after \(t\le k\) choices each remaining set keeps at least \(r-\ell t\) bits outside used rows, so some bit meets a fraction at least \((r-\ell k)/v\ge2k/m\) of the remaining sets when \(r\ge3\ell k\); \(k\) choices meet all sets once \(M(1-2k/m)^k<1\), that is about \(\ln M<2k^2/m\), below the summed count's \(-k\ln x\approx3k^2/m\). The fractional-cover version (Johnson, Lovász) was not read or tested; the one-bit-per-row constraint makes it a cover under a partition matroid, where the classical factor does not transfer directly.
  • Lovász Local Lemma (Erdős–Lovász; algorithmic form Moser–Tardos), the reviewer's first alternative. It targets the coordinate kernel without the union bound. Answers. Existence of a transversal monomial when the avoidance events have sparse dependence. Test. Falsified as a main-term improvement, for the symmetric lemma with the natural dependency graph: events for pinned sets \(P_a,P_b\) are dependent when the sets meet. By Cauchy–Schwarz on the bit degrees, \(\sum_{a\ne b}|P_a\cap P_b|\ge(Mr)^2/v-Mr\), and \(|P_a\cap P_b|\le r\), so on average a set meets at least \(Mr/v-1\) others. The condition \(e\,p\,(\Delta+1)\le1\) then gains over the union bound \(Mp<1\) at most a factor about \(v/(er)\), which is \(26\) at \(r=r_D=14593\), \(v=1048592\): an additive \(\ln26\approx3.3\) in \(\ln M\). The lopsided form was not tested.

Absurd bridges

  • Statistical physics of random hypergraph covers. Translation: the ceiling's random coordinate family is a random \(r\)-uniform hypergraph on \(v\) vertices with \(M\) edges, and the kernel exists exactly when it has a transversal of at most \(k\) vertices in distinct rows; cavity-method thresholds for minimal covers would locate the transition. Answers. It would place the random-family threshold inside the window and say how much a joint certificate could recover there. Test. Not run: no cavity statement was read or applied; the only comparison available, that the summed count and the ceiling agree on the main term, is the proved sandwich itself and could not fail.
  • Information theory: Slepian–Wolf and Shearer for polymatroids. Translation: by lem:restriction-rank-overlap, the joint rank of subsets of errors is a polymatroid, and Shearer's inequality bounds the whole by averages over a covering family of subsets. Answers. It would bound the joint rank from joint ranks of smaller groups. Test. Falsified: the bound needs those group joint ranks, the quantity sought, and with singleton groups it is the summed bound; the main notebook's cor:filtered-shearer-tail already records a Shearer-type sufficient criterion of this kind.

New mathematics. prop:coordinate-count-window, a Maclaurin bound showing that on coordinate blocks the exact summed count reaches the main term of the common-kernel ceiling up to a factor \(1+O((r+k)/v)\), with the additive window measured; the greedy transversal bound; the falsification of the symmetric local lemma for the kernel step; and the note that Boolean ideal defects are reduced.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The review measures the room left for joint savings on unrestricted coordinate families, keeps the obligation open with its non-domination requirement stated in terms of the certificate, and names the correlation certificate, which targets the logarithmic part of that room, as the next lemma. Bearing. At \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\), the window ledger gives the certified \(\ln M<4.246\) against the ceiling family's \(12.62\), a window the correlation certificate could enter; at \(\ell=32\) and minimal degree the main-term gap of \(341\) is beyond its reach. Not covered. Non-coordinate flats; Pang's stage; the main-term gap at minimal degree; effective-annihilator-degree beyond the note above; hardness-preserving-repair.

Builds. Two builds were queued in this period and are still queued. admissible-parameter-check is built now, for the reviewer: the medium-reviewer definition asks every review to check comparisons at every jointly admissible parameter value; its second part, a prompt in the sentence tool, is dropped, since that tool cannot know a theorem's free parameters. unreviewed-label-check waits (16 October 2026): it needs a hook reading the agent's chat text while a review gate is open, and the gate state is not yet recorded anywhere a hook can read; that recording is its first step.

Record search. Searches covered joint stacked ranks of adaptive approximators with shared prefixes and word spaces (lem:joint-word-shortening, lem:conditional-seed-contraction, prop:duplicate-prefix-rank-law, read by statement), the local lemma, greedy transversals and Maclaurin's inequality with restriction ranks (no match in this setting), Castelnuovo–Mumford regularity with zero-dimensional schemes and annihilator degree (no regularity statement), weighted Shearer criteria (cor:filtered-shearer-tail), Harris and FKG correlation with random monomials (prop:same-sign-saddle-bound, lem:avoiding-sets-fraction), and row transversals with products of forms (lem:row-transversal-probe-criterion and lem:block-cover-transversal-reduction, in other settings). The resemblance check for prop:coordinate-count-window returned prop:common-kernel-ceiling at 0.36, related in the spec, and nothing above 0.26 otherwise.

Verification gate. Dependencies read: the registered statements of the line's results and of the Pang-stage laws, the full proof of prop:common-kernel-ceiling, and the two saved checks. The sole fresh-context medium reviewer worked in two stages. In the first it saw only the goal, the open statements and the result statements, and proposed the three alternatives above. In the second it passed the Maclaurin step, the geometric bound, the greedy argument and the local-lemma count, and swept the window over \(\ell\) and \(D\); the author reproduced the sweep with a committed script. In a follow-up check the reviewer supplied that certificate's stopping steps (the generating-function bound for the averaging over \(b\), a Chernoff good event, the degree tail); the author checked the first two and the tail, and the sum over large \(j\) is left to the next entry, which is now lemma-first. Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • A Harris certificate for coordinate kernels: fix a random bit per row, select rows independently with probability \(\pi=\mu/m\), apply Harris's inequality to the increasing events, average over the bits and subtract the degree tail; estimated \(\ln M<6.1\) at \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\). Record. Record. The search for row transversals and products of forms returned lem:row-transversal-probe-criterion and lem:block-cover-transversal-reduction, in other settings, and the earlier search returned prop:form-cover-multipliers in the odd-prime notebook, whose product-of-forms multiplier is this construction; whether it counts as domination is for the next review.
  • Test \(\dim Z_h\) against \(\sum_ad_a\) on a Pang family whose parent inputs are pairwise independent but whose word products coincide. Record. The word-space search returned only lem:joint-word-shortening, which leaves \(\dim Z_h\) open.

Elementary certificates dominate the Harris kernel certificate at computed parameters

16 October 2026 · Working proofs (coordinate blocks); no correlation saving at the computed parameters · Harris kernel certificate. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Chosen. The queue head, entry-2026-10-15-multiplier-separation:s1, asks to “test whether coding-theoretic product structure gives an independently proved bound on the joint row space of actual paid error maps, strictly better than adding their individual ranks”. This entry develops it through the route review's decisive test: a correlation certificate would bound the joint action of Braun's actual restriction observations below the sum of their ranks, with no product-code structure.

Owed attempt. The head's latest development named the duality bridge as the next attempt: “test whether a scalar moment pairing on only the necessary degree windows can represent the common error kernel”. It waits: the route review's decisive test, which the cycle rules require next, came first; the duality item remains queued separately.

Question. Does a Harris-inequality certificate give a common row-linear kernel for coordinate families beyond what the summed count certifies, and is that a correlation saving rather than an elementary one?

Architecture test. Decided. The review's first outcome occurred in form, with discrepancies: the certificate beats the summed count at \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\) (\(5.44\) against \(4.25\), not the estimated \(6.1\)), and falls below it at minimal degree already from \(\ell=18\), not from \(\ell=28\). But neither the review nor the first draft of this entry compared it with the elementary certificates, and these decide the test: at each of the eight computed rows the Harris range is below the greedy transversal certificate below (whose first case is the trivial bound: any \(M\le k\) blocks have a common kernel) or below the summed count. So at the computed parameters the correlation certificate gives no saving over elementary constructions, and the review's lower end of the window, the summed count, was the wrong baseline.

General statement. lem:harris-kernel-certificate is a correct all-parameter sufficient condition for a common kernel on coordinate families; lem:greedy-transversal-certificate is an elementary one; at the eight computed rows the larger of it and the summed count exceeds the Harris certificate, and it beats the exact summed count at moderate \(k\).

Harris kernel certificate

Statement. In Braun's setting (\(\mathbb F_2\), \(m\) rows of \(\ell\) bits, \(v=m\ell\), row-linear \(\mathcal L_k\)), let \(M\ge1\) proper coordinate blocks pin sets \(P_1,\ldots,P_M\) of bits to constants, each of size at least \(r\), and suppose that every bit pinned by several blocks is pinned by all of them to the same constant (consistent pins). Let \(0<\mu<k\), \(\pi=\mu/m\), \(0<\varepsilon<2\), \(J=\lfloor\varepsilon/\pi\rfloor\ge1\), \(c_0=\lceil r/(2\ell)\rceil\), \(y=(1-\varepsilon/2)\mu r/v\), \(g(y)=-\ln(1-e^{-y})\), \(\tau=e^{-\varepsilon c_0}/(J(1-e^{-\pi c_0}))\) and \(h(t)=t\ln t-t+1\). If

\[Me^{-r/(8\ell)}\le\tfrac12\qquad\text{and}\qquad 2M\bigl(g(y)+\tau\bigr)<\mu\,h(k/\mu),\tag{1}\]

then a nonzero \(f\in\mathcal L_k\) has zero ordinary restriction to every flat. Without consistent pins the same holds with \(\ell\) and \(v\) replaced by \(2\ell\) and \(2v\) in \(c_0\), \(y\) and (1).

Proof. Call a pair \((b,c)\) of a bit and a constant a literal, and let \(\Lambda_a\) be the literals block \(a\) pins, at least \(r\) of them and at most \(\ell\) in each row. For a set \(X\) of literals in distinct rows with \(|X|\le k\), the product \(f_X=\prod_{(b,c)\in X}(x_b+c)\) expands into row-linear monomials of degree at most \(|X|\), so lies in \(\mathcal L_k\), and is nonzero; if \(X\) meets \(\Lambda_a\) its restriction to block \(a\)'s flat has a zero factor. So it suffices to find such an \(X\), nonempty, meeting every \(\Lambda_a\). With consistent pins, give each bit the constant its pins share (zero if unpinned); this is one literal per bit, and \(\Lambda_a\) is identified with \(P_a\). Choose a bit \(b_i\) uniformly in each row \(i\), independently; let \(s_{a,i}=|P_a\cap\text{row }i|/\ell\) and \(c_a=\#\{i:b_i\in P_a\}\), a sum of independent Bernoulli\((s_{a,i})\) variables with mean \(\sigma_a=|P_a|/\ell\ge r/\ell\). Given \(b\), select each row independently with probability \(\pi\) and let \(X\) be the literals of the chosen bits of the selected rows. The event “\(X\) meets \(P_a\)” is that some row with \(b_i\in P_a\) is selected, an increasing event of the selection vector, with probability \(1-(1-\pi)^{c_a}\). Harris's inequality for increasing events under a product measure (the form used in lem:avoiding-sets-fraction) gives \(\Pr[X\text{ meets all }P_a\mid b]\ge\exp(-\sum_a\varphi(c_a))\), where \(\varphi(c)=-\ln(1-(1-\pi)^c)=\sum_{j\ge1}(1-\pi)^{cj}/j\). The Chernoff bound for the binomial number of selected rows gives \(\Pr[|X|>k]\le e^{-\mu h(k/\mu)}\), since \(k>\mu\). So a suitable \(X\) exists for this \(b\) when \(\sum_a\varphi(c_a(b))<\mu h(k/\mu)\); it is nonempty because \(M\ge1\).

Let \(G\) be the event that \(c_a\ge c_0\) for every \(a\). Since \(c_0-1<\sigma_a/2\), the Chernoff lower tail gives \(\Pr[c_a<c_0]\le e^{-\sigma_a/8}\le e^{-r/(8\ell)}\), so \(\Pr[G]\ge1-Me^{-r/(8\ell)}\ge\frac12\) by (1). Some \(b\in G\) has \(\sum_a\varphi(c_a(b))\le E[\sum_a\varphi(c_a)\mathbf 1_G]/\Pr[G]\le2\sum_aE[\varphi(\max(c_a,c_0))]\), as \(\varphi\) decreases. Split the series at \(J\). For \(j\le J\), \(E(1-\pi)^{jc_a}=\prod_i(1-s_{a,i}(1-(1-\pi)^j))\le\exp(-\sigma_a(1-(1-\pi)^j))\), and \(1-(1-\pi)^j\ge1-e^{-j\pi}\ge j\pi(1-j\pi/2)\ge j\pi(1-\varepsilon/2)\); so these terms are at most \(e^{-jy}/j\), whose sum over all \(j\ge1\) is \(g(y)\). For \(j>J\), \((1-\pi)^{j\max(c_a,c_0)}\le e^{-j\pi c_0}\), and \(\sum_{j>J}e^{-j\pi c_0}/j\le e^{-(J+1)\pi c_0}/((J+1)(1-e^{-\pi c_0}))\le\tau\), since \((J+1)\pi\ge\varepsilon\). Hence \(\sum_a\varphi(c_a(b))\le2M(g(y)+\tau)<\mu h(k/\mu)\). Without consistent pins, choose a literal uniformly among the \(2\ell\) of each row instead of a bit: then \(s_{a,i}=|\Lambda_a\cap\text{row }i|/(2\ell)\), \(\sigma_a\ge r/(2\ell)\), and the same argument runs with \(2\ell\) and \(2v\) in place of \(\ell\) and \(v\).

A correction to the first draft. The first draft reduced to pins to zero by translating each pinned-to-one bit. That is valid for one block at a time, as in the coordinate-count and ceiling proofs, which bound single restriction ranks, but not for a common kernel when two blocks pin one bit to different constants; the literal form above replaces it, and the author found this while rewriting after the review. Correction to the route review. The route review's Obstacle says that for coordinate blocks the common kernel exists exactly when a row-linear monomial meets every pinned set. That characterization is proved for pins to zero, which is the case prop:common-kernel-ceiling uses (its family pins \(x_P=0\), so the ceiling stands); for blocks pinning one bit to different constants it is not proved, and the lemmas here give only sufficient conditions through products of literal factors.

Greedy transversal certificate

Statement. In the same setting, let \(k\ge1\), let every block pin at least \(r>\ell(k-1)\) bits, and let \(V=v\) with consistent pins and \(V=2v\) otherwise. If for some integer \(0\le t\le k-1\)

\[M\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\Bigl(1-\frac{r-\ell i}{V}\Bigr)\le k-t,\tag{2}\]

then a nonzero \(f\in\mathcal L_k\) has zero ordinary restriction to every flat. At \(t=0\) this is: any \(M\le k\) blocks have a common kernel. It refines the greedy transversal bound of the route review, about \(\ln M<2k^2/m\), by a finishing step that adds \(\ln(k-t)\).

Proof. As in the previous proof it suffices to find a set \(X\) of at most \(k\) literals in distinct rows meeting every \(\Lambda_a\); for \(M\ge1\) it is nonempty, and for \(M=0\) take \(f=1\). Build \(X\) greedily. Let \(R_0\) be all blocks, and suppose \(i<t\) literals are chosen, in \(i\) rows, and \(R_i\) is the set of blocks not yet met. Each block of \(R_i\) pins at least \(r-\ell i>0\) literals in unused rows, since it pins at most \(\ell\) per row; there are at most \(V\) literals in unused rows that some block pins (one per bit with consistent pins, two otherwise). By double counting some such literal lies in at least \(|R_i|(r-\ell i)/V\) of the sets \(\Lambda_a\), \(a\in R_i\); add it, so \(|R_{i+1}|\le|R_i|(1-(r-\ell i)/V)\). After \(t\) steps (2) gives \(|R_t|\le k-t\). For each remaining block in turn add one of its literals in a row not yet used; one exists, since at most \(k-1\) rows are used and the block pins at least \(r-\ell(k-1)>0\) literals outside them. In total \(X\) has at most \(t+(k-t)=k\) literals in distinct rows and meets every block.

Comparison. The committed script baselines.py evaluates each certificate's largest certified \(\ln M\) at \(r=r_D\), \(k=K_D\) (baselines.json; greedy and Harris for consistent and general pins; summed count, which bounds single ranks and so holds for all pins):

\(\ell\), \(D\)summed\(\ln k\)greedy, consistentgreedy, generalHarris, consistentHarris, generalceiling

16, 975.165.817.816.305.975.1013.64

20, 5113.016.937.647.006.276.1312.77

In every row the Harris value, for each kind of pins, is below the larger of the summed count and the greedy value of the same kind. The greedy certificate beats the summed count in the \(\ell=16\) rows, at \(K\approx\sqrt m\) (the last two rows) and, for consistent pins, at \(\ell=18\) minimal degree; at larger \(k\) the summed count leads. All values lie below the ceiling, as the script asserts. An earlier version of this entry claimed a spread-family theorem in which the certificate beat the exact summed count; the reviewer found that the trivial bound \(M\le K\) already covers that regime at every computed row (spread_saving.json has \(L_H<\ln K\) in every row), and it is withdrawn before registration.

Degenerate cases. \(M=0\): \(f=1\). \(M\le k\): the greedy certificate at \(t=0\). \(r\le\ell(k-1)\): the greedy lemma says nothing; a block may then be pinned inside \(k-1\) rows. \(\mu\ge k\) or \(J=0\): the Harris lemma says nothing. Duplicated blocks change no transversal.

Nonvacuity. At \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\), \(k=303\), \(r=14593>\ell(k-1)=4832\), any family of \(M\le e^{7.13}\), for example \(1200\), coordinate blocks of rank \(r\) with consistent pins meets (2) at \(t=210\) by the saved check, where the summed count fails beyond \(e^{4.25}\approx70\) blocks. For the Harris lemma, the saved optimum at the same row, \(\mu=115.1\), \(\varepsilon=0.02\), with \(M\le e^{5.43}\), meets (1).

New mathematics. A Harris-inequality certificate for common row-linear kernels on coordinate families, in a literal form valid for arbitrary pinned constants; and the elementary greedy transversal certificate, whose \(t=0\) case says that any \(k\) coordinate blocks of rank above \(\ell(k-1)\) have a common kernel. At the eight computed rows the larger of the second and the summed count exceeds the first, and the second beats the exact summed count at moderate \(k\). The correction to the first draft's reduction to pins to zero is recorded above; the review's coordinate count is unaffected, since it bounds single ranks.

Selection gain. Unchanged at the goal's parameters. The greedy certificate enlarges the inventory for which Braun's common-kernel selection is certified, beyond the summed count, at moderate \(k\) (from \(e^{4.25}\) to \(e^{7.14}\) blocks at \(\ell=16\), \(D=107\), consistent pins); the correlation certificate adds nothing beyond it.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The route review's restated non-domination requirement asks for a certificate whose hypothesis needs no given cover and does not follow from the cover bound for a cover it supplies. The trivial bound \(M\le k\) meets those words, so they do not separate a structural saving from an elementary one, and meeting them is not evidence of progress; this goes to the next review, which must restate the requirement. Bearing. None. The goal's own parameters are a polynomial inventory, \(\ln M=O(\ell)\), at polylogarithmic degree. There \(K_D\) is about \(n/(2D)\), and the summed count, like the bound behind Braun's corollary on polynomial inventory and polylogarithmic degree, certifies \(\ln M\) up to order \(K_D^2/m\), far beyond \(O(\ell)\) (\(593\) at \(\ell=24\), \(D=145\) in the saved table), so no saving is needed there. The certificates of this entry gain only at degrees polynomial in \(n\), such as \(D\approx\sqrt n/2\), outside the goal's range. The cycle was spent because a correlation certificate could have given a saving that transfers to non-coordinate flats, where the single-flat estimate is not shown tight; it gives none on coordinate flats.

Small-inventory coordinate exclusion over \(\mathbb F_2\)

16 October 2026 · Working proof (coordinate high-rank blocks over \(\mathbb F_2\) only) · Small-inventory coordinate exclusion. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Chosen. This entry develops small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion, queued in this session, instead of the head entry-2026-10-15-multiplier-separation:s1. The head's last two entries (product-code comparison, Harris certificate) both declared no bearing, so the rules require a different item. This one bears on joint-error-savings directly: it asks whether the elementary transversal of the Harris entry widens the admissible range of Braun's exclusion, which that entry's bearing paragraph examined only at polylogarithmic degree.

Question. Braun's finite-parameter theorem uses its counting bound \(m\ln(4M)\le k^2\) only to obtain a nonzero common kernel in \(\mathcal L_k\). When the proper high-rank blocks are coordinate blocks, the transversal of lem:greedy-transversal-certificate supplies that kernel from \(M\le k\) alone. Does that give exclusion at degree about \(n/(2M)\), what do the published and exact summed counts give there, and is it a joint saving below the summed ranks?

General statement. thm:small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion: over \(\mathbb F_2\), a complete old-affine family whose proper blocks of rank above \(h(k+1)\) are at most \(k\) coordinate blocks has no ordinary-PC refutation through any \(D\ge2h+1\) with \(2k(D+1)-1\le n\); the greedy inequality may replace “at most \(k\)”. On spread coordinate families with \(\ln M\ge-(kr/m)\ln(1-1/\ell)\), roughly \(M\lesssim\sqrt{m\ln M/3}\) at \(k=M\), the exact summed restriction ranks already reach \(\dim\mathcal L_k\). The theorem is elementary: it fixes one pinned bit of each block, in fresh rows, at its unpinned value, and applies Braun's row separation.

Small-inventory coordinate exclusion

Setting. As in Braun's finite-parameter theorem (Section 5, claim audit:ordinary-restriction-affine-family), over \(\mathbb F_2\), the field of that publication: \(m>0\), \(\ell\ge2\), \(n=2^\ell\), \(h=3\ell\), \(v=m\ell\), a finite complete old-affine family adjoined to \(\mathcal Q_{m,\ell}\), and \(\mathcal L_k\) the span of monomials of degree at most \(k\) with at most one variable from each pigeon row. A block is a coordinate block when its input span is spanned by literals \(x_b+c_b\), \(c_b\in\{0,1\}\); if it is proper, these involve distinct bits, its rank is the number \(|P|\) of pinned bits, and its zero flat is \(\{x_b=c_b,\ b\in P\}\).

Statement. (a) Let \(k\ge1\) and suppose the proper blocks of rank greater than \(h(k+1)\) are coordinate blocks, \(M\) of them. If \(D\ge2h+1\) and \(2k(D+1)-1\le n\), and either \(M\le k\) or, with \(r=h(k+1)+1\), \(V=2v\) (or \(V=v\) when every bit pinned by several of these blocks has one constant), some integer \(0\le t\le k-1\) has \(M\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}\bigl(1-(r-\ell i)/V\bigr)\le k-t\), then the augmented system has no ordinary-PC refutation through degree \(D\). (b) In particular, for \(M\ge1\), a family whose proper blocks of rank greater than \(h(M+1)\) are at most \(M\) coordinate blocks has no refutation through \(D_M=\lfloor(n+1)/(2M)\rfloor-1\) whenever \(D_M\ge2h+1\). (c) Let \(M\le k\) spread coordinate blocks each pin exactly \(r\) bits in distinct rows. If \(\ln M\ge-(kr/m)\ln(1-1/\ell)\), the sum of their exact restriction ranks on \(\mathcal L_k\) is at least \(\dim\mathcal L_k\), so the exact summed count certifies no common kernel at this \(k\), while (a) gives one when \(r>h(k+1)\).

Proof of (a). Let \(P_1,\ldots,P_M\) be the pinned sets of the proper blocks of rank above \(h(k+1)\). Under \(M\le k\): each has more than \(h(k+1)\ge\ell(k-1)\) bits, at most \(\ell\) per row, so it meets at least \(k\) rows. Choose for each block in turn a pinned bit \(b_a\in P_a\) in a row not used before; at most \(k-1\) rows are used when a block is handled, so one exists. Put \(f=\prod_a(x_{b_a}+c_{b_a})\), with \(f=1\) if \(M=0\). The factors lie in distinct rows, so \(f\) expands into monomials with at most one variable per row, of degree at most \(M\le k\): \(f\in\mathcal L_k\), and \(f\ne0\). Substituting \(x_{b_a}=c_{b_a}\) makes a factor zero, so the ordinary restriction of \(f\) to block \(a\)'s zero flat is the zero polynomial. Under the product inequality, lem:greedy-transversal-certificate gives such an \(f\), a product of at most \(k\) literal factors in distinct rows. Braun's restriction-ideal lemma (claim lem:ordinary-affine-restriction-ideal) then gives \eqref{eq:literal-ideal} for each such block: \(f=\sum_ia_ig_i\) with \(\deg a_i\le k-1\). This is exactly what the common-kernel lemma supplies in Braun's proof, so the rest of that proof applies unchanged: weighted removal (claim lem:affine-family-removal-with-unit-blocks), with blocks of rank at most \(h(k+1)\) handled by packing and unit spans by constants, derives \(f\) from the old base through \(B=k(D+1)\), contradicting Braun's row separation (claim lem:cube-residual-dual-separation), which needs \(1\le k\le B\), \(4(k-1)<n\) and \(2B-1\le n\); the middle condition follows from the last with \(D\ge2h+1\). Part (b) is (a) at \(k=M\), where \(D_M\) is the largest \(D\) with \(2M(D+1)-1\le n\).

Proof of (c). Translating the pinned bits of one block to zero changes neither its restriction rank nor row-linearity, so the rank of block \(a\) is the number of row-linear monomials of degree at most \(k\) avoiding \(P_a\), as in the ceiling proof. Let \(R\) be the \(r\) rows it meets. The monomials of degree exactly \(j\) on a row set \(S\) avoiding \(P_a\) number \(\ell^j(1-1/\ell)^{|S\cap R|}\). For a uniform \(j\)-subset \(S\) of rows, \(E|S\cap R|=jr/m\), and \(t\mapsto(1-1/\ell)^t\) is convex, so the count over all \(S\) is at least \(\binom mj\ell^j(1-1/\ell)^{jr/m}\ge\binom mj\ell^j(1-1/\ell)^{kr/m}\). Summing over \(j\le k\) gives rank at least \((1-1/\ell)^{kr/m}\dim\mathcal L_k\), since \(\dim\mathcal L_k=\sum_{j\le k}\binom mj\ell^j\). The \(M\) blocks together give at least \(M(1-1/\ell)^{kr/m}\dim\mathcal L_k\ge\dim\mathcal L_k\) under the hypothesis.

What the theorem is. Two elementary facts the comparison below must be read with. First, with \(f=1\), Braun's removal and row separation already exclude every complete family whose proper blocks all have rank at most \(h(k+1)\), at every \(k\) with \(D\ge2h+1\) and \(2k(D+1)-1\le n\), with no counting condition. Second, over \(\mathbb F_2\) the weight \(\prod_a(x_{b_a}+c_{b_a})\) is the indicator of \(\{x_{b_a}=c_{b_a}+1\ \forall a\}\): weighting by it is restricting each chosen bit to the value its block forbids, which makes every high-rank block's flat unreachable. So the theorem says: kill each high-rank coordinate block by fixing one of its bits, in distinct rows, then apply row separation. Its saving below the summed ranks is a cover by \(M\) single literals, the most elementary construction available, and part (c) holds almost automatically, since a single spread block of rank \(r\ll v\) has rank close to \(\dim\mathcal L_k\).

Degree ranges. The committed script degree_range.py evaluates each certificate at the worst-case rank \(h(k+1)+1\) for its own \(k\), so each column is the degree guaranteed for every family with at most \(M\) proper high-rank coordinate blocks, whatever their ranks: the smallest certifying \(k\) and \(D=\lfloor(n+1)/(2k)\rfloor-1\) when at least \(2h+1\) (degree_range.json). The baselines' smallest \(k\) comes from a full scan of every allowed \(k\), which agreed with the binary search in every row; the greedy value, for arbitrary constants (\(V=2v\)), uses a closed form that agrees with the direct sum to \(6\cdot10^{-10}\) at \(\ell=16\). The published count needs \(k\ge\sqrt{m\ln4M}\); the summed count is the coordinate-count bound of prop:coordinate-count-window.

For \(2\le M\le n^{1/4}\) the greedy degree is \(D_M\) of (b), larger than the published and summed degrees by factors of order \(\sqrt{n\ln M}/M\); at \(M=1\) the summed count also certifies \(k=1\) and the degrees agree; at \(M=n^{1/2}\) the greedy certificate still leads at every \(\ell\) shown; from \(M=n\) the summed count leads.

Prior work. The main notebook's thm:safe-probe-affine-augmentation, with lem:row-transversal-probe-criterion and cor:direct-sum-safe-reader-endpoint, uses the same combinatorial operation: probes in distinct rows clamped to one, chosen by a Hall-type criterion for general bit-affine blocks of total rank \(a\le cN\), \(c<1/4\). It then maps to a near-balanced PHP source, whose endpoint excludes \(D\le N^\alpha\operatorname{polylog}N\) with \(2\alpha+\beta<1\), below \(\sqrt N\). The present theorem differs in the lower-bound engine applied after the clamp, Braun's row separation at \(B=k(D+1)\), which gives degree about \(n/(2M)\), and covers only coordinate blocks. thm:shared-span-affine-exclusion does not give (b): even with one shared subspace it needs \(m\ln4\le k^2\).

Correction to the Harris entry. The Harris entry declared no bearing, reasoning only at polylogarithmic degree, and cited Braun's polylogarithmic corollary by its source file; its registered claim is audit:ordinary-restriction-affine-family. Its greedy lemma does bear on Braun's admissible range, through (a) and (b) above, at degrees polynomial in \(n\).

Nonvacuity. In Braun's matched ledger at \(\ell=16\), \(n=65536\), \(m=65537\): one coordinate block pinning \(97>h(1+1)=96\) bits satisfies (b) with \(M=1\), \(D_1=32767\ge2h+1=97\), and \(2(D_1+1)-1\le n\). For (c), two spread blocks pinning \(r=145=h(2+1)+1\) bits each in distinct rows at \(k=2\): \(-(2\cdot145/65537)\ln(15/16)\approx2.9\cdot10^{-4}\le\ln2\).

Degenerate cases. No proper high-rank block: \(f=1\), which excludes the family at every \(k\) with \(D\ge2h+1\) and \(2k(D+1)-1\le n\), with no counting condition (see above). \(M>k\) without the product inequality: (a) says nothing. \(D_M<2h+1\), as for \(M\) above about \(n/(12\ell)\): (b) says nothing. A block that is not a coordinate block and has high rank is outside the hypothesis. Duplicated blocks need no extra factor.

New mathematics. thm:small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion: for complete families over \(\mathbb F_2\) whose proper high-rank blocks are coordinate blocks, fixing one pinned bit per block in distinct rows replaces Braun's counting bound in his common-kernel step, raising the certified exclusion degree for \(M\) such blocks to \(\lfloor(n+1)/(2M)\rfloor-1\); a Jensen bound shows that on spread families with \(\ln M\ge-(kr/m)\ln(1-1/\ell)\) the exact summed ranks already reach \(\dim\mathcal L_k\). The proof is Braun's, with one lemma replaced by an elementary transversal.

Selection gain. Braun's common-kernel selection is certified for coordinate families from the block count alone; on spread families in the range of (c) this is beyond the summed ranks, and the certified degree rises by factors of order \(\sqrt{n\ln M}/M\) for \(2\le M\le n^{1/4}\) in the computed table.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The theorem meets the words of joint-error-savings: a checkable hypothesis, a joint kernel where the summed exact ranks reach \(\dim\mathcal L_k\) on an unbounded family, and a larger admissible degree after Braun's full costs. But the saving is a cover by single literals, which is the domination the route review meant to exclude, and its restated wording does not exclude; and the range gain lies at degrees polynomial in \(n\), for coordinate high-rank blocks only, where restriction kills the blocks outright. Counting this as the obligation's discharge would make the obligation trivial; the next review must restate it. Bearing. None at the goal's own parameters, polynomial inventory at polylogarithmic degree, where the summed count already suffices and nothing here changes. The cycle was spent because the Harris entry's greedy lemma could have changed Braun's admissible range; it does, for coordinate families at polynomial degree only.

Braun's Section 5 lemmas and theorem and Section 4's row separation were read in full; their registered claims were found with tools/publication_claims.py.

All-prime small-inventory coordinate exclusion

16 October 2026 · Working proof (coordinate high blocks, every prime field, compact base) · All-prime small-inventory exclusion. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Chosen. This entry continues small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion instead of the head. It bears on joint-error-savings as the previous entry did, and it comes first because the user asked for the general version: on that entry's restriction to \(\mathbb F_2\) they wrote “If we prove something more general, that's good”, and “If we get a general version for free, we do the general version.” The restriction had been made without naming a step that needs \(\mathbb F_2\); only the separation theorem fixes the base, and an all-prime one exists.

Owed attempt. The item's latest development named as next attempt “the sub-idea below, the disjoint-support packing for non-coordinate flats”. It was not made: the general version over every prime came first; it remains the item's queued sub-idea.

Best outcome. At the goal's own parameters, polynomial inventory at polylogarithmic degree, nothing: there the counting bound of thm:all-prime-affine-family-exclusion already suffices. The best outcome is the all-prime form of the previous entry's degree range, about \(n/(2M)\) for \(M\) high coordinate blocks over every \(\mathbb F_p\).

Question. Does the transversal replace the counting step of the main notebook's all-prime affine exclusion, giving the small-inventory range over every prime field, and which of its hypotheses are needed?

General statement. cor:all-prime-small-inventory-exclusion: for every prime \(p\), over the compact base \(\mathcal Q_{n,p}\) with accuracy \(h=3(p-1)\ell\), a complete one-level old-affine family whose proper high blocks are at most \(k\) coordinate blocks has no ordinary-PC refutation through any \(D\ge1\) with \(2k(D+1)-1\le n\).

All-prime small-inventory exclusion

Setting. As in the main notebook's thm:all-prime-affine-family-exclusion (its proof): a prime \(p\), \(n=2^\ell\), \(\ell\ge2\), \(m=n+1\) pigeon rows of \(\ell\) bits, \(v=(n+1)\ell\), the compact base \(\mathcal Q_{n,p}\) over \(\mathbb F_p\) (collision equations and Boolean bit equations, the encoding of lem:all-prime-compact-bit-decoder), accuracy \(h=3(p-1)\ell\), and complete one-level ENS blocks with inputs affine in the old bits and coefficient domains \(r^p-r\). A proper block of rank \(r\) is high when \((p-1)r>h(k+1)\), that is \(r>3\ell(k+1)\). A coordinate block has input span spanned by literals \(x_b-c_b\), \(c_b\in\mathbb F_p\); if it is proper they involve distinct bits, since two constants on one bit give a unit span, and its zero flat in \(\mathbb F_p^v\) is \(\{x_b=c_b,\ b\in P\}\), \(|P|=r\).

Parameter ledger. The budget the statement checks: kernel degree \(k\ge1\); replay ceiling \(B=k(D+1)\); separation conditions \(1\le k\le B\), \(4(k-1)<n\), \(n\ge2B-1\), of which only the last is a hypothesis; high threshold \(r>3\ell(k+1)\); inventory \(M\le k\), or the greedy inequality below.

Statement. Let \(k\ge1\), and suppose the proper high blocks are \(M\) coordinate blocks. If \(D\ge1\), \(2k(D+1)-1\le n\), and either \(M\le k\), or for some integer \(0\le t\le k-1\), with \(r=3\ell(k+1)+1\) and \(V=pv\) (\(V=v\) when every bit pinned by several of these blocks has one constant), \(M\prod_{i=0}^{t-1}(1-(r-\ell i)/V)\le k-t\), then the augmented system has no ordinary-PC refutation through degree \(D\). In particular \(M\ge1\) such blocks are excluded through \(D_M=\lfloor(n+1)/(2M)\rfloor-1\) whenever \(D_M\ge1\), that is \(M\le(n+1)/4\).

Proof. The kernel under \(M\le k\). Each high pinned set has more than \(3\ell(k+1)\ge\ell(k-1)\) bits, at most \(\ell\) per row, so it meets at least \(k\) rows; choose for each block in turn a pinned bit \(b_a\) in a row not used before, and put \(f=\prod_a(x_{b_a}-c_{b_a})\), or \(f=1\) if \(M=0\).

The kernel under the product inequality. Call a pair \((b,c)\), \(c\in\mathbb F_p\), a literal, and let \(\Lambda_a\) be the literals block \(a\) pins: \(|\Lambda_a|\ge r\), at most \(\ell\) in each row. Build a set \(X\) of literals in distinct rows. With \(R_0\) all high blocks, suppose \(i<t\) literals are chosen in \(i\) rows and \(R_i\) are the blocks not yet met. Each block of \(R_i\) pins at least \(r-\ell i>0\) literals in unused rows, and at most \(V\) literals in unused rows are pinned by any block (one per bit with consistent pins, at most \(p\) per bit otherwise); by double counting some such literal lies in at least \(|R_i|(r-\ell i)/V\) of the sets \(\Lambda_a\), \(a\in R_i\). Add it; \(|R_{i+1}|\le|R_i|(1-(r-\ell i)/V)\), so the inequality gives \(|R_t|\le k-t\). For each remaining block add one of its literals in a row not yet used; one exists, since at most \(k-1\) rows are used and \(r-\ell(k-1)>0\). Then \(|X|\le k\); put \(f=\prod_{(b,c)\in X}(x_b-c)\). This is the proof of lem:greedy-transversal-certificate, written over \(\mathbb F_p\)-literals.

In both cases \(f\) is a product of at most \(k\) affine factors in distinct rows, so it expands into monomials with at most one variable per row: \(f\in\mathcal L_k\), \(f\ne0\), and substituting a block's pins makes one factor zero, so the ordinary restriction of \(f\) to every high zero flat is the zero polynomial. The rest is the all-prime proof with its counting step replaced; it uses its count only to produce this kernel. Affine coordinates give literal expressions \(f=\sum_ia_ig_i\), \(\deg a_i\le k-1\), for each high tuple; the \(p-1\) coefficient rows \((\alpha a_i)_i\), \(\alpha\ne0\), turn the high product into \(1-f^{p-1}\); weighting by \(f\), a high companion is \(g_i(f-f^p)\), certified through \(pk+1\); low blocks, \((p-1)r\le h(k+1)\), use factor packing with companions through \((p-1)r+1\); zero and unit spans are removed by scalar cleanup. These ceilings fit \(k(e+1)\) for every axiom of original degree \(e\) at every \(k\ge1\), as that proof checks; a refutation through \(D\) uses only axioms with \(e\le D\), so it replays to an old derivation of \(f\) through \(B=k(D+1)\), whatever \(D\ge1\). The signed row separation (lem:signed-cube-row-linear-separation, Section 3 of that entry) excludes it when \(1\le k\le B\), \(4(k-1)<n\) and \(n\ge2B-1\): the first holds for \(D\ge0\), the second follows from the third as \(4(k-1)<2k(D+1)-1\) for \(D\ge1\), and the third is the hypothesis. The last sentence is the case \(k=M\).

Relation to the \(\mathbb F_2\) theorem and the count. At \(p=2\), \(h=3\ell\) and \(V=2v\), this is thm:small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion on Braun's base with \(m=n+1\), there with the hypothesis \(D\ge2h+1\), which Braun's removal lemma states and the present statement does not need at \(m=n+1\); that theorem also allows other \(m\), so neither statement contains the other. In the case \(M\le k\) the range \(D_M\) is the same at every \(p\); only the greedy refinement changes, through \(V=pv\), which lowers each greedy fraction by the factor \(2/p\) against \(p=2\); since the bearing here is none, it was not tabulated at odd \(p\). Against the counting kernel, \(k\approx\sqrt{(n+1)\log4M}\) gives about \(\sqrt{n/\log M}/2\), so the transversal gives the higher degree for \(M\) up to about \(\sqrt{n\log M}\), the crossover of the previous entry, which is field-independent.

Nonvacuity. In the parameter ledger at \(p=3\), \(\ell=16\), \(n=65536\), \(h=96\): one coordinate block pinning \(97\) bits to constants in \(\mathbb F_3\), with \(97>3\ell(1+1)=96\), is high and satisfies the statement with \(M=k=1\), \(D_1=32767\), \(2(D_1+1)-1\le n\).

Degenerate cases. No proper high block: \(f=1\), and the exclusion holds at every \(k\) with the degree conditions. \(M>k\) without the product inequality: nothing. \(D<2h+1\): the companions cannot occur, and the exclusion holds by the same proof, or by old stability with \(f=1\). \(M>(n+1)/4\): \(D_M\) is zero and the last sentence says nothing. A high block that is not a coordinate block is outside the hypothesis.

New mathematics. cor:all-prime-small-inventory-exclusion: the small-inventory exclusion holds over every prime field on the compact base and at every degree \(D\ge1\), with the transversal over \(\mathbb F_p\)-literals replacing the counting step of thm:all-prime-affine-family-exclusion; the greedy refinement carries over with \(V=pv\). The proof is that theorem's, with one step replaced; the previous entry's hypothesis \(D\ge2h+1\) turns out unneeded.

Selection gain. The common-kernel selection of the all-prime exclusion is certified for coordinate high blocks from the block count alone, over every \(\mathbb F_p\), extending the previous entry's degree range \(D_M\) to odd characteristic.

Goal progress. Unchanged. This generalizes the previous entry's elementary cover to every prime; it keeps its character, a cover by single literals at polynomial degree, which the next review must weigh against the restated non-domination requirement. Bearing. None at the goal's own parameters, polynomial inventory at polylogarithmic degree, where the all-prime counting bound already suffices; the cycle was spent on the general statement the user asked for, whose gain lies at polynomial degree.

Record search. A search for an all-prime small-inventory or literal-transversal exclusion returned the previous entry's theorem, cor:direct-sum-safe-reader-endpoint (binary) and thm:all-prime-unary-affine-source-exclusion, the unary-PHP analogue, whose kernel step is again a count; no prime-field transversal result is registered. The all-prime proof, its compact encoding and its signed separation section were read in full. For the next attempt, the disjoint-support packing, the transversal searches of the previous entry returned lem:row-transversal-probe-criterion, a Hall criterion for row-distinct minors, not for disjoint row supports.

Verification gate. Dependencies read: the main notebook's all-prime affine exclusion proof in full, its compact encoding and its signed separation section, and the registered statements of thm:all-prime-affine-family-exclusion, lem:signed-cube-row-linear-separation, lem:greedy-transversal-certificate and thm:small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion. The sole fresh-context medium reviewer (review record) passed the kernel, the \(\mathbb F_p\) greedy count, the claim that the count is used only for the kernel, every weighted ceiling at every \(k\) and \(p\), the setting lines against the source and the nonvacuity arithmetic. It found \(D\ge2h+1\) unused, so the statement now holds for \(D\ge1\), which also makes the odd-\(p\) range equal to the binary one and corrects the degenerate case \(D<2h+1\); it required the greedy step written out over \(\mathbb F_p\) rather than cited, a real ledger, Chosen instead of Picked, and the crossover with the count. All were done. Process audit. The reviewer found the degenerate-cases paragraph understating the result for not asking whether \(D\ge2h+1\) is used, the relation paragraph overstating agreement under that hypothesis, the greedy dependency recorded too strongly, the ledger link self-referential and the user's words a general remark rather than a pick of this item; all are corrected, and the title is its proposal.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • The unary-PHP analogue thm:all-prime-unary-affine-source-exclusion has rows of \(n\) variables, so the direct pick needs \(r>n(k-1)\) or a row-Hall hypothesis (any \(j\) blocks together occupy at least \(j\) rows); under the latter the kernel is a product of literals in distinct rows and the rest of that proof may follow. Domain-only stacked error rank is an interpolation rank

16 October 2026 · Working proof (domain-only model, not Pang's proper quotient) · Pang contraction joint count. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Chosen. This entry develops pang-contraction-joint-count, queued in this session, instead of the head. It is the one place found where a joint count could bear on joint-error-savings at the goal's own parameters, which the last four entries did not: in cor:pang-observation-calibration the \(S\) families must satisfy \(S(1-\eta)^t<1\), so the number of rounds \(t\), and with it the degree \(\Delta=(p-1)dt\), is set by \(\ln S\); a joint count could cut that degree by a constant factor.

Best outcome. A structural bound on the stacked rank of Pang's error maps by an effective count \(c\) in place of \(S\), giving rounds \(t\approx\ln c/\eta\) instead of \(\ln S/\eta\), a degree saving by the factor \(\ln S/\ln c\) at Pang's own parameters; or a proof that no structural saving of the admitted kind exists in a stated model.

Question. Where does the sum over families enter the recorded contraction engines, and is the stacked rank of Pang's error families the sum, or below it by a saving of which kind?

Where the sum enters. In cor:pang-observation-calibration each family \(\nu\) is contracted separately, because its rows \(q_{\nu,j}\) must lie in its own input span: Pang's Proposition 5.1 needs \(1-P_L=\sum_iU_{L,i}g_{L,i}\) in the ideal of line \(L\)'s own inputs; the calibration then states that “their stacked rank is at most the sum”. The main notebook's shared-seed engines draw shared rows from the span of all inputs, but in thm:partial-shared-cleanup the reference rank is bounded parent by parent: its proof applies the full-domain expectation to each parent and states “Summing their marginal expectations gives \(\rho H\), which bounds the stacked reference mean”, after “rank subadditivity and averaging”. The registered statements of thm:filtered-shared-cleanup and cor:full-row-weighted-cleanup keep the same \(\rho=M(1-\eta)^t\). The joint estimate is open in the record.

General statement. lem:domain-model-stacked-rank: over any field, on any finite point set, when the paid quotient carries only the relations “vanishes on the points”, the stacked rank of error maps whose products fit the degree window is the interpolation rank of the multiplier space on the union of the errors' supports; the sum of the individual ranks exceeds it by the cokernel of restriction to the pieces. For Pang's errors with only the Boolean relations this holds at his own cutoff.

Domain-model stacked rank

Statement. Let \(F\) be a field, \(X\subseteq F^n\) a finite set, \(B_D\) the polynomials of degree at most \(D\) vanishing on \(X\), \(U=V_K/(B_D\cap V_K)\), and \(E_1,\ldots,E_s\in V_e\) with \(K+e\le D\). Write \(S_j\subseteq X\) for the support of \(E_j\) on \(X\), \(S=\bigcup_jS_j\), and \(T_j:U\to V_D/B_D\), \(u\mapsto[E_ju]\). Then \(\operatorname{rank}T_j=\dim U|_{S_j}\) and the stacked map has \(\operatorname{rank}(T_j)_j=\dim U|_S\), where \(U|_A\) is the space of restrictions to \(A\) of the functions in \(U\). The restriction map \(\iota:U|_S\to\bigoplus_jU|_{S_j}\) is injective, so \(\sum_j\operatorname{rank}T_j-\operatorname{rank}(T_j)_j=\dim\operatorname{coker}\iota\).

Proof. Since \(K\le D\), \(B_D\cap V_K\) is exactly the degree-\(\le K\) part vanishing on \(X\), so classes of \(U\) are determined by their functions on \(X\). For \(u\in V_K\), \(E_ju\) has degree at most \(K+e\le D\), so \([E_ju]=0\) exactly when \(E_ju\) vanishes on \(X\); as \(E_j(x)\ne0\) is invertible in \(F\) at every \(x\in S_j\), that is exactly \(u|_{S_j}=0\). So \(\ker T_j=\{u:u|_{S_j}=0\}\) and the stacked kernel is \(\{u:u|_S=0\}\); rank–nullity on the restriction maps gives the ranks. A function on \(S\) vanishing on every \(S_j\) is zero, so \(\iota\) is injective, and \(\sum_j\dim U|_{S_j}\) is the dimension of its target.

Application to Pang. In cor:pang-observation-calibration the errors are \(E_{\nu,i}=P_\nu g_{\nu,i}\), of degree at most \(\Delta+d\), and the window \(\Delta+K+d\le D\) is exactly \(K+e\le D\). Over \(\mathbb F_p\) with only the Boolean axioms, a proper system, the degree-\(D\) PC consequences are exactly \(B_D\) for \(X=\{0,1\}^n\): multilinearization is degree-nonincreasing, each step a monomial times \(x^2-x\) (historical lem:fieldreduction). So with only domain relations the stacked rank of all of Pang's error maps is \(\dim U|_S\) over the union of their supports, and the recorded sum exceeds it by \(\dim\operatorname{coker}\iota\); overlap of supports is one source of that gap, failure of \(U\) to interpolate independently on disjoint pieces another.

Against the open statement. joint-error-savings says, verbatim: “Nor does union-of-supports counting in an evaluation model, nor domination”. In the domain-only model the lemma expresses the joint rank through the union of supports alone, as an interpolation rank, so every saving over the sum there is of that form; whether the exclusion's wording, written for the curve model where “joint rank is the union size by definition”, covers the interpolation deficit on disjoint supports is for the next review to confirm. Two further facts bound what a saving in Pang's proper quotient \(V_K/(C_D\cap V_K)\), with \(C_D\) the consequences of an unsatisfiable system, can be. First, domain-only savings carry into it: \(u|_S=0\) gives \(E u\in B_D\subseteq C_D\), so the proper-quotient kernel contains the image of the domain-only one. Second, a saving beyond that needs the relations of \(C_D\) beyond the domain equations.

Nonvacuity. For the lemma alone: \(F=\mathbb F_2\), \(X=\{0,1\}^n\), \(n\ge2\), \(K=e=1\), \(D=2\), errors \(E_j=x_j\), \(j\le s\), \(2\le s\le n\), on \(U=V_1\): each \(\dim U|_{S_j}=n\) (there \(x_j\) and \(1\) coincide), so the summed rank is \(sn\), while the union is the cube minus exactly \(2^{n-s}\) points, on which \(U|_S\) has dimension \(n+1\). In Pang's matched ledger, a witness of his form: \(p=2\), \(d=t=1\), so \(\Delta=1\); \(K=1\), \(D=3\), so \(\Delta+K+d=3\le D\); \(s\ge2\) families \((x_{2a-1},x_{2a})\) on \(n=2s\) variables with \(q_a=x_{2a-1}+x_{2a}\) and \(P_a=1+q_a\). The errors \(P_ax_{2a-1}\) and \(P_ax_{2a}\) are supported on \(\{x_{2a-1}=x_{2a}=1\}\), where \(U=V_1\) restricts to dimension \(2s-1\); the summed family ranks are \(s(2s-1)\), and the stacked rank is \(\dim U|_S=2s+1\), since an affine function vanishing whenever some pair is \((1,1)\) is zero for \(s\ge2\).

Degenerate cases. \(s=0\): rank zero. \(E_j=0\): empty support. Disjoint supports: \(\iota\) need not be surjective, and the sum can exceed the stacked rank when \(U\) cannot separate the supports, as with \(U\) the constants. \(K+e>D\): outside the statement; products are not paid.

New mathematics. lem:domain-model-stacked-rank, an exact formula at the rediscovery level: in a function quotient, multiplication by \(E\) kills exactly the functions vanishing on its support, so stacked rank is the interpolation rank on the union of supports, and the sum exceeds it by a cokernel. Applied to Pang's errors at his cutoff, it identifies every domain-only saving and shows a further saving needs non-domain relations; where the record sums over parents is identified.

Selection gain. Unchanged. The formula certifies selection (union interpolation rank below \(H\)) only in the domain-only model, a satisfiable control; such savings carry into Pang's proper quotient, but no new certificate there is obtained.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The item could have cut Pang's degree by \(\ln S/\ln c\); the entry shows that in the domain-only model every saving over the sum is a union-of-supports interpolation count, and leaves the proper quotient open. Bearing. None at the goal's own parameters: Pang's quotient is proper, and the lemma gives there only the carried-over support savings; it fixes what a further joint saving must use. The cycle was spent on the one place found where a joint count would change the degree at the goal's parameters.

Record search. Searches for shared approximation rows, equal-span batches and stacked rank in the Boolean function model returned historical lem:equalspan (identical spans share rows, the duplication case), thm:partial-shared-cleanup, whose proof was read where it sums over parents, and the registered statements of thm:filtered-shared-cleanup and cor:full-row-weighted-cleanup; nothing states the domain-model formula. For the next attempt, a search for stacked rank in proper quotients returned the main notebook's prop:orbit-kernel-saturation, on stacked injectivity of fewer than \(3s\) character translates in a proper charged \(\mathbb F_4\) quotient, and cor:charged-matching-saturation.

Verification gate. Dependencies read: the Pang calibration entry in full, the partial-shared-cleanup entry, and the registered statements cited. The sole fresh-context medium reviewer (review record) passed the lemma's proof, the identification of Pang's errors and window, the domain-only quotient, the nonvacuity arithmetic and the per-family own-span constraint. It required: replacing “loose exactly by support overlap” with the cokernel, since the draft's own degenerate case contradicted it; citing the partial-cleanup lines that sum over parents instead of a sentence about the high/low split; a Pang-form witness in Pang's window; the exact count \(2^{n-s}\); stating that domain-only savings carry into the proper quotient; quoting the open statement verbatim; and the general version over any field and finite point set, the Boolean narrowing kept to the application. All were done. Process audit. The reviewer found the overstatement, the misattributed source sentence, the witness unchecked against its ledger's window, the decisive comparison resting on a paraphrase, and the record-search wording on prop:orbit-kernel-saturation wider than its statement; all corrected, and the title is its proposal.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • In a reduced finite-dimensional algebra, by Artin–Wedderburn a product of fields, multiplication is diagonal in the primitive idempotents, so stacked rank is again a support interpolation rank; a semisimple character quotient would reproduce the support picture, and a saving not explained by supports needs nilpotents or degree truncation, as in \(F[x]/(x^3)\), \(U=\langle1,x\rangle\), \(E_1=x\), \(E_2=x^2\), with ranks \(2\), \(1\) and stacked rank \(2\). Record. The proper-quotient search returned prop:orbit-kernel-saturation and cor:charged-matching-saturation; no registered result treats the reduced-algebra case.
  • Test a truncated quotient of a small unsatisfiable system just above Pang's window with two Pang-form errors, and see whether the stacked rank falls below the domain-only count. Record. The same search; cor:pang-observation-calibration gives the window.

  • In a degree-truncated quotient \(V_K/(C_D\cap V_K)\) of a small unsatisfiable system, compare the stacked rank of two Pang-form errors with the domain-only interpolation rank, and find whether truncation gives a joint saving not explained by supports.
