Single-input killers enlarge truncated error kernels

16 October 2026 · Working proof and refutation, with finite check (4 pigeons, 3 holes, D=3, K=1) · Truncated quotient kernels. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Chosen. This entry continues pang-contraction-joint-count, the item the last entry developed, with its named next attempt. It bears on joint-error-savings at the goal's own parameters through Pang's contraction, where the summed count sets the degree.

Owed attempt. The item's latest development named “the sub-idea above, a degree-truncated quotient of an unsatisfiable system”; it is made here.

Best outcome. A proof that in Pang's proper quotient the error kernels are support kernels plus relations, which would close the item, since every joint saving there would be a support count; or a refutation with the mechanism that enlarges the kernels.

Question. For a proper degree-\(D\) closure \(C_D\) over \(\mathbb F_2\) and two-input errors \(E_i=(1+g_1+g_2)g_i\) with \(\deg E_i+K\le D\), is \(\ker_C=\{u\in V_K:E_iu\in C_D\}\) equal to \(\ker_B+(C_D\cap V_K)\), the support kernel of lem:domain-model-stacked-rank plus the quotient's relations, and if not, what enlarges it?

General statement. lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement: over \(\mathbb F_2\), for a proper degree-\(D\) closure containing Booleanity, inputs \(g_1,g_2\) of degree at most \(d\), \(P=1+g_1+g_2\) and \(2d+K\le D\), the kernel of either error \(Pg_i\) on \(V_K\) contains \(\ker_B+(C_D\cap V_K)+A_1+A_2\), \(A_i=\{u\in V_K:g_iu\in C_D\}\); when \(g_1g_2\) is a closure member of degree at most \(D-K\), the kernel is \(V_K\), and it strictly contains \(\ker_B+(C_D\cap V_K)\) exactly when \((C_D\cap V_K)|_S\ne V_K|_S\), \(S\) the support of \(g_1g_2\), so the support-plus-relations hypothesis fails in every such instance. The finite check check:truncated-kernel-beyond-support finds the inclusion an equality for the other families of one pigeonhole instance.

Two-input kernel enlargement

Statement. Let \(C\) be the degree-\(D\) PC closure of a system over \(\mathbb F_2\) containing the Boolean axioms, with \(1\notin C\); let \(g_1,g_2\) have degree at most \(d\ge1\), \(P=1+g_1+g_2\), \(E_i=Pg_i\), and \(2d+K\le D\). Put \(\ker_B=\{u\in V_K:E_iu\text{ vanishes on the cube}\}\) and \(A_i=\{u\in V_K:g_iu\in C\}\). Then (a) \(E_1\equiv E_2\equiv g_1g_2\) modulo Boolean consequences of degree at most \(2d\); (b) \(\ker_B+(C\cap V_K)+A_1+A_2\subseteq\{u\in V_K:E_iu\in C\}\) for \(i=1,2\); (c) if moreover \(g_1g_2\in C\) and \(\deg g_1g_2\le D-K\), then \(\{u:E_iu\in C\}=V_K\), and this strictly contains \(\ker_B+(C\cap V_K)\) exactly when \((C\cap V_K)|_S\ne V_K|_S\) for the support \(S\) of \(g_1g_2\) on the cube; in the multilinear ring a sufficient condition is \(\dim(C\cap V_K)<\dim V_K|_S\). The hypotheses \(1\notin C\) and \(d\ge1\) are not used in (a) and (b), and in (c) \(1\notin C\) follows from the condition. Here \(C\) is the degree-\(D\) PC closure in the convention of the computation below (members of degree below \(D\) multiplied by variables); that Pang's \(C_D\) is this closure is an assumption of the application.

Proof. (a) \(Pg_1=g_1+g_1^2+g_1g_2\), and \(g_1^2+g_1\) vanishes on the cube, so it is a Boolean consequence of degree at most \(2d\) by degree-nonincreasing reduction (historical lem:fieldreduction); likewise for \(g_2\). (b) A member of \(C\) times a polynomial stays in \(C\) when the intermediate degrees of the variable-by-variable multiplication stay below \(D\). For \(u\in\ker_B\), \(E_iu\) has degree at most \(2d+K\le D\) and vanishes on the cube, so it is a Boolean consequence, in \(C\). For \(r\in C\cap V_K\), multiplying by the monomials of \(E_i\) keeps the intermediate degrees at most \(K+2d-1<D\). For \(u\in A_j\), the member \(g_ju\) has degree at most \(d+K\), and multiplying it by the monomials of the other input keeps the intermediate degrees below \(2d+K\le D\), so \(g_1g_2u\in C\); by (a), \(E_iu-g_1g_2u=(g_i^2+g_i)u\) is a Boolean consequence of degree at most \(2d+K\le D\), so \(E_iu\in C\). (c) Every \(u\in V_K\) gives \(g_1g_2u\in C\), multiplying the member \(g_1g_2\) of degree at most \(D-K\) by \(u\), and then \(E_iu\in C\) as in (b). On the other side, \(E_i\) and \(g_1g_2\) are the same function by (a), so \(\ker_B=\{u:u|_S=0\}\) is the kernel of restriction to \(S\); hence \(\ker_B+(C\cap V_K)=V_K\) exactly when restriction maps \(C\cap V_K\) onto \(V_K|_S\). The sufficient condition follows from \(\dim(C\cap V_K)|_S\le\dim(C\cap V_K)\).

Pigeonhole families. For the pigeonhole system with pigeon axioms \(\prod_j(1+x_{ij})\) and hole axioms \(x_{ij}x_{i'j}\), write \(x_{ij}\) for pigeon \(i\) in hole \(j\). Single-variable inputs \(g_1=x_{ij}\), \(g_2=x_{i'j'}\) have \(d=1\). Each \(A\) contains the variables \(x_{kj}\), \(k\ne i\), for \(g_1\), since \(x_{ij}x_{kj}\) is a hole axiom, and \(x_{kj'}\), \(k\ne i'\), for \(g_2\). When the two inputs share a hole, \(j=j'\), \(g_1g_2\) is a hole axiom, and (c) applies whenever \(C\cap V_K\) is small.

The finite check

The computation. The committed script truncated_kernels.py computes the closure exactly in the multilinear ring (Booleanity built in) by echelon elimination on the monomials of degree at most \(D\), multiplying every echelon row whose leading monomial has degree below \(D\) by every variable until nothing new enters, and the three kernels on \(V_K\) by elimination over \(\mathbb F_2\); variables are indexed \(x_{3i+j}\) for pigeon \(i\), hole \(j\). Controls: the empty system gives equal sides, and \(\ker_B+(C\cap V_K)\subseteq\ker_C\) is asserted in every case. A first version had a non-canonical remainder and a closure that missed members of degree below \(D\) arising from cancelling top terms; it failed the containment assertion, and both were fixed before any result was used (commit history).

Result. From truncated_kernels.json: \(3\) pigeons in \(2\) holes have largest proper degree \(2\), below the window. With \(4\) pigeons and \(3\) holes the largest proper degree is \(D=3\) (so \(1\in C_4\)); at \(K=1\), \(\dim V_1=13\), \(C_3\cap V_1=0\), \(H=13\). The script took the first six pairs containing \(x_{00}\): \((x_{00},x_{01})\), \((x_{00},x_{02})\), \((x_{00},x_{10})\), \((x_{00},x_{11})\), \((x_{00},x_{12})\), \((x_{00},x_{20})\). Kernel codimensions are \(5,5,0,5,5,0\) against \(11\) each for the support kernel plus relations; summed \(20\) against \(66\); stacked \(9\) against \(12\). The stacked numbers depend on this arbitrary choice of families.

The trace. The pairs \((x_{00},x_{10})\) and \((x_{00},x_{20})\) share hole \(0\): \(g_1g_2\) is a hole axiom, and (c) gives codimension \(0\), with \(0<11=\dim V_1|_S\). For the other four, (b) accounts for the whole kernel: for \((x_{00},x_{01})\), \(\ker_B+A_1+A_2\) contains \(1+x_{00},1+x_{01}\) (in \(\ker_B\)) and \(x_{10},x_{20},x_{30},x_{11},x_{21},x_{31}\) (in \(A_1+A_2\) by hole axioms), eight independent elements, so it equals the computed kernel of dimension eight; for \((x_{00},x_{11})\), \(1+x_{00},1+x_{11}\) and \(x_{10},x_{20},x_{30},x_{01},x_{21},x_{31}\), again eight; the pairs with \(x_{02}\) and \(x_{12}\) are the same with hole \(2\). The stacked kernel is the intersection of the six, spanned by \(1+x_{00},x_{10},x_{20},x_{30}\), dimension \(4\), as computed.

Nonvacuity. In Pang's matched ledger: \(p=2\), \(d=t=1\), \(\Delta=1\), \(K=1\), \(D=3\), so \(\Delta+K+d=3\le D\) and \(2d+K=3\le D\); the system is unsatisfiable and \(C_3\) proper; the errors are \((1+g_1+g_2)g_i\), one fixed choice of approximation row; \(H=13\). The lemma's (c) applies to the two same-hole pairs.

Degenerate cases. The empty system: \(C\) holds only Booleanity; in (b) \(A_i\subseteq\ker_B\), since the support of \(g_1g_2\) lies in that of \(g_i\), so (b) reduces to \(\ker_B\), as the control asserts; (c) is vacuous there, since \(g_1g_2\in C\) forces \(S\) empty. Errors that are themselves members: (c). \(D\) at or above the refutation degree: the question is void.

New mathematics. lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement: in a proper truncated quotient, a two-input Pang error's kernel contains the multipliers that kill either input alone, and is everything when the inputs' product is a member, which refutes in every such instance the hypothesis that proper error kernels are support kernels plus relations; check:truncated-kernel-beyond-support finds this inclusion an equality for the four non-member families of one pigeonhole instance and traces it to hole axioms.

Selection gain. Unchanged as a theorem. Measured at the instance: proper-quotient ranks are smaller than support ranks, summed (\(20\) against \(66\)) and stacked (\(9\) against \(12\)), and the joint saving differs from the support count (\(11\) against \(54\)).

Goal progress. Unchanged. The lemma shows that proper error kernels can exceed support kernels plus relations, through members that kill single inputs; it gives no structural all-parameter joint bound. Bearing. None at the goal's own parameters as a bound: the lemma bounds single kernels from below, not the stacked rank from above below the sum.

Record search. Searches for truncated PHP closures with kernels of products of variables returned near-half-pc-degree's cor:pigeonhole-shattering-not-thin (a different encoding, linear pigeon sums with functionality axioms, and \(m>n\ge5\), not directly comparable with the product pigeon axioms at \(m=4,n=3\)) and prop:paired-block-relations, and historical thm:columnbarrier; none states this kernel comparison. For the next attempt, the paired-block relations are the nearest structural description of products of pigeon variables, in a different encoding.

Review. The stand-in review at the last checkpoint listed five objections. (1) The skipped sentence-tool run before the reviewer's brief had no committed check: right; the build sentence-tool-before-review was queued in that cycle, and this cycle ran the tool before the brief. (2) Claim identifiers were guessed and checked afterwards: right; the existing refusal of unregistered labels would have stopped a wrong one, and both were checked before use. (3) An unrecorded “quick estimate” of slack in Pang's stage drove a route proposal: right; it was an untested hypothesis, and the contraction entry later found the opposite for Pang's contraction, where the sum sets the degree. (4) “Probably very little” was an uncalibrated judgement: right; it should have been labelled untested. (5) The claim-refresh tool should have been built at the second manual rebuild: right; it now exists, and the finisher's stale-claim hint names it.

Verification gate. Dependencies read: lem:domain-model-stacked-rank, cor:pang-observation-calibration and prop:paired-block-relations by statement; the degree-nonincreasing Boolean reduction used in (a) is the two-line argument given there (historical lem:fieldreduction, not reread). In a follow-up the same reviewer passed (a)–(c) and the trace, and gave the exact condition now in (c), the trace wording, the vacuous degenerate case and the closure-convention assumption; all were applied. The sole fresh-context medium reviewer (review record) found no defect in the script's closure, remainder, kernels or controls, matched every number to the JSON, and passed the identity and the inclusion. It required: the inclusion argument written out; the family labels corrected, two pairs being different pigeons in different holes; the kernel traced instead of “not traced”; the all-parameter refutation from the degenerate case stated and registered; the saving sentences reduced to what was measured; the closure fix recorded with the remainder fix; and the record-search encoding caveat. All were done; in writing (c) the author found that “\(1\) is not in the sum” does not follow from \(1\) being outside each part, and replaced it by the dimension condition \(\dim(C\cap V_K)<\dim V_K|_S\). Process audit. The reviewer found that the degenerate case already refuted the hypothesis before any computation, the trace left undone without reason, the families mislabelled, the arbitrary family choice unstated, the closure fix omitted from the history, and saving claims unsupported by a measurement; it proposed the title now used.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Conjecture: for single-variable pigeonhole families at \(K=1\) below the refutation degree, the kernel equals \(\ker_B+(C\cap V_K)+A_1+A_2\); then the stacked kernel is the intersection of these sums, a closed-form hole-neighbourhood stacked rank. One case at \(5\) pigeons would test it; the converse direction needs a standard-monomial description of the closure in this window. Record. A tested module for exact truncated PC closures

16 October 2026 · Build (framework tool, tested) · PC closure module. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Where we stand. Unchanged: a tool build changes no mathematical state

Chosen. This entry develops the build pc-closure-module instead of the head. The item pang-contraction-joint-count, on joint-error-savings, had two entries in a row without bearing and must wait for a different item; its next test, at \(5\) pigeons and \(4\) holes, needs an exact closure with membership queries, which this build supplies.

Best outcome. At the goal's own parameters nothing directly: a tested tool that makes the next test of lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement at \(5\) pigeons reliable and fast enough to run.

General statement. No claim applies: this entry builds a framework tool.

The build. tools/pc_closure.py computes the degree-\(D\) polynomial-calculus closure over \(\mathbb F_2\) in the multilinear ring, with Booleanity built in, by echelon elimination on the monomials of degree at most \(D\) ordered by degree descending, multiplying every echelon row whose leading monomial has degree below \(D\) by every variable until nothing new enters; stored rows stay reduced at every pivot, so remainders are canonical and linear. It answers membership and remainder queries and gives the refutation degree. Its tests, tools/tests/test_pc_closure.py, compare the refutation degree with tools/truncated_buchberger.py, an independent method, on the pigeonhole system with \(3\) pigeons and on six random width-two clause systems on \(4\) variables, asserting that both refuted (degree \(2\)) and unrefuted systems occur among them; check that remainders are canonical; and include a member arising only from cancelling top terms, the case the first truncated-quotient script missed and the one control that distinguishes that error.

Measured. One timed run on pigeonhole systems with one hole fewer than pigeons (saved output): \(4\) pigeons at \(D=3\), \(299\) monomials, closure dimension \(178\), printed as \(0.0\) s; \(5\) pigeons at \(D=3\), \(1351\) monomials, dimension \(680\), \(0.1\) s; \(5\) pigeons and \(4\) holes at \(D=4\), \(6196\) monomials, dimension \(4905\), proper, \(13.2\) s.

New mathematics. No new mathematical result: the cycle produced a tested closure tool, whose correctness rests on the closure convention stated above and on agreement with an independent implementation, tools/truncated_buchberger.py, on seven systems, of which some are refuted and some not.

Selection gain. Unchanged: a tool proves no selection, survival or hardness-preservation improvement.

Goal progress. Unchanged. Bearing. None at the goal's own parameters: a tool. The cycle was spent because the next test of lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement, the mechanism found to enlarge Pang's error kernels, needs it, and the item that holds that test had to wait for a different item.

Record search. The tools/ directory holds tools/f2_closure_degree.cpp (refutation degree and closure dimension, no membership queries) and tools/truncated_buchberger.py (refutation degree by truncated completion); neither answers membership of a given product, which the kernel tests need. A registry search for PC closure membership and remainder tools over F2 in the multilinear ring returned only finite checks that used tools/f2_closure_degree.cpp, such as ex:parity-two-clause-closure-degrees, and no membership tool.

Cross-check. The committed script cross_check.py compiles tools/f2_closure_degree.cpp and compares, at every \(D\le3\), properness and closure dimension on the pigeonhole systems with \(3\) and \(4\) pigeons and eight random systems on \(5\) variables; the two agree everywhere, asserted in the script. The C++ tool uses the same elimination design, so this checks the implementation, not the closure convention, which rests on the comparison with tools/truncated_buchberger.py (cross_check.json; for \(4\) pigeons, dimensions \(18\) at \(D=2\) and \(178\) at \(D=3\)).

Verification gate. The module's four tests pass; its refutation degrees agree with tools/truncated_buchberger.py, an independent method, on seven systems, refuted and unrefuted; its properness and dimensions agree with tools/f2_closure_degree.cpp on ten systems at every \(D\le3\), a check of the implementation only. The sole fresh-context medium reviewer (review record) read the module and found the closure convention, the fully reduced echelon basis, the canonical remainder and the loop's termination correct, and required: the module's docstring claim about the C++ tool corrected; a saved source for the timings; the shared design of the two closure tools stated; a random test that provably meets refuted cases; the near-tautological multiplication check dropped as a control; out-of-range variables refused. All were done; the full suite passed, 1095 tests in 9.5 s. Process audit. The reviewer found the title accurate, the timings unsourced, the C++ agreement presented as independent confirmation, the prevention unwritten as a rule, and the Bearing paragraph without a claim ID; all are corrected.

Process assessment. The build answers the prevention named for the truncated-quotient script's errors. Prevention. The module, and the rule now in COMPUTATION_RULES.md that F2 closure work uses the tested tools rather than a closure written in a research script.

Follow-up. Developed: the module exists at tools/pc_closure.py with its tests. Not examined. The item names a module with membership and remainder queries, checked against tools/f2_closure_degree.cpp and tools/truncated_buchberger.py on small systems; all are done above, the second in the tests and the first in a committed provenance run, which needs a compiler, so nothing it names is left. Owed attempt. None was named: the build was queued directly with lead_queue.py add, and no follow-up on it named an attempt.

Pigeonhole joint savings are matching-domain savings

16 October 2026 · Working proof, refutation and finite checks · Joint-savings goal review. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Question. Ten research entries have worked on joint-error-savings since the architecture review of 15 October, six of them since the last route review, all with goal progress unchanged and the last seven with no bearing at the goal's own parameters (the article tags of the record and the turn guidance of compute.sh start). The open-item count has not dropped, so this review is goal-level. It asks what the line has established, why every saving found so far is support counting or domination, whether the user's proposal of 16 October (prefer a natural map or an exactly described kernel to a dimension count) gives a different mechanism, and whether the next cycles stay on this statement or switch.

General statement. The line's general claim is that the joint action of actual errors can be bounded below the sum of their ranks by their structure, in the protected quotient, with a gain in a sufficient multiplier budget or admissible range. Established on the line: thm:overlapping-curve-recovery, lem:exact-common-kernel-selection, lem:restriction-rank-overlap, thm:shared-span-affine-exclusion, thm:increment-count-exclusion, prop:common-kernel-ceiling, prop:coordinate-count-window, lem:harris-kernel-certificate, lem:greedy-transversal-certificate, thm:small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion, cor:all-prime-small-inventory-exclusion, lem:domain-model-stacked-rank and lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement. This review adds lem:factor-ideal-domination: when factors have a principal generator inside the window, the common kernel of their multiplication maps is that generator's annihilator; check:clique-factor-kernels shows that the pigeonhole joint savings computed so far are of this kind; and ex:support-saving-not-inherited corrects a transfer clause of lem:domain-model-stacked-rank. The reviewer's inertness argument, checked here, shows that the line's pigeonhole evidence lies in a domain model on hole-respecting assignments.

Obstacle

Every joint saving the line has produced has one of three forms: a union of supports in an evaluation model (curve recovery, the domain-only model of lem:domain-model-stacked-rank), domination by one coarser map (shared spans, the shared input of the truncated-quotient check), or a product of killers of individual factors (the transversal of thm:small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion, whose degree grows with the number of distinct factors). The non-domination wording of the obligation was met, on its letter and in a weak form, by the trivial transversal for \(M\le k\) blocks. And the one mechanism found in what the line took for a proper truncated quotient, the single-input killers of lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement, gives a joint saving that the checks below show to be a domain-model saving on hole-respecting assignments, also dominated by one annihilator: below degree \(n\) the product pigeonhole closure is the hole ideal, and these systems are never proper above degree \(n\). What the line is stuck on is a joint saving of none of the three kinds, and it has not yet computed one kernel in a window where the closure is not the degree part of a vanishing ideal.

Factor ideal domination

Statement. Over a field \(F\), let \(C\) be a degree-\(D\) PC closure (members of degree below \(D\) multiplied by variables), \(V_K\) the polynomials of degree at most \(K\), and for a polynomial \(w\) put \(\operatorname{Ann}_K(w)=\{u\in V_K:wu\in C\}\). Let \(w_1,\ldots,w_r\) have degrees \(e_i\le e\), and suppose \(s=\sum_ia_iw_i\) with \(\deg a_i+e_i\le K'\), \(\deg s\le\sigma\), \(sw_i-w_i\in C\) for every \(i\), \(K+K'\le D\) and \(K+\sigma+e\le D\). Then (a) \(\bigcap_i\operatorname{Ann}_K(w_i)=\operatorname{Ann}_K(s)\): the common kernel of the factor maps is one annihilator. (b) If errors satisfy \(E_\nu=h_\nu w_{i(\nu)}+c_\nu\) with \(c_\nu\in C\), \(\deg h_\nu\le g\), \(\deg c_\nu+K\le D\) and \(K+e+g\le D\), their common kernel contains \(\bigcap_i\operatorname{Ann}_K(w_i)\) directly, with no \(s\); by (a) it contains \(\operatorname{Ann}_K(s)\). Under \(sw_i\equiv w_i\) alone and \(K+\sigma+e+g\le D\), every map \([u]\mapsto[E_\nu u]\) also factors through \([u]\mapsto[su]\); since \(s=1\) always qualifies for this, the factorization says nothing by itself, and the content of the lemma is the principality in (a). (c) In a domain model, where \(C\) contains every polynomial of degree at most \(D\) vanishing on a finite set \(X\) and each \(w_i\) is \(0/1\)-valued on \(X\), \(s=1-\prod_i(1-w_i)\) qualifies whenever \(K+(r+1)e\le D\). (d) For the pigeonhole hole axioms \(x_{ij}x_{kj}\), the factors \(w_i=x_{ij}\), \(i\in I\), of one hole \(j\) and \(s=\sum_{i\in I}x_{ij}\) qualify whenever \(K+2\le D\).

Proof. (a) If \(w_iu\in C\) for all \(i\), then \(su=\sum_ia_i(w_iu)\), and each \(a_i(w_iu)\) is a member multiplied variable by variable through intermediate degrees below \(K+\deg a_i+e_i\le K+K'\le D\), so \(su\in C\). Conversely, if \(su\in C\), then \(w_iu=w_i(su)-(sw_i-w_i)u\); the first term multiplies a member of degree at most \(K+\sigma\) by \(w_i\) through degrees below \(K+\sigma+e\le D\), and the second multiplies the member \(sw_i-w_i\), of degree at most \(\sigma+e\), by \(u\) through degrees below \(K+\sigma+e\). (b) For \(u\in\bigcap_i\operatorname{Ann}_K(w_i)\), \(w_{i(\nu)}u\in C\) has degree at most \(K+e\); multiplying by \(h_\nu\) through degrees below \(K+e+g\le D\) and adding \(c_\nu u\in C\) gives \(E_\nu u\in C\). For the factorization, \(E_\nu u-h_\nu w_{i(\nu)}su=c_\nu u+h_\nu(w_{i(\nu)}-sw_{i(\nu)})u\), a sum of members by the same degree count; so \([E_\nu u]=[h_\nu w_{i(\nu)}\cdot su]\), and multiplication of a member of degree at most \(K+\sigma\) by \(h_\nu w_{i(\nu)}\) stays in \(C\), so the right side depends only on \([su]\). (c) On \(X\), \(s\) is the indicator of the union of the supports, so \(sw_i-w_i\) vanishes on \(X\) and has degree at most \((r+1)e\le D\); the inclusion–exclusion form \(s=\sum_iw_i\prod_{j<i}(1-w_j)\) gives \(K'=re\), and \(K+re\le D\). (d) \(sx_{ij}-x_{ij}=x_{ij}^2-x_{ij}+\sum_{k\in I,k\ne i}x_{kj}x_{ij}\) is a sum of Boolean and hole axioms of degree \(2\), with \(K'=\sigma=e=1\).

Relations. Part (c) recovers the union-of-supports kernel of lem:domain-model-stacked-rank as one annihilator: in a domain model every factor family is dominated, by the indicator of the union of its supports, at the degree cost \((r+1)e\). Part (d) is the column structure of historical lem:columnconditioning, whose indicator \(1-\sum_ix_{ij}\) of an empty column is \(1-s\). The degree of a principal generator of two disjoint monomials \(x_A,x_B\) is given by prop:disjoint-product-map-degree (near-half-pc-degree): coefficient maps of degree \(\min(|A|,|B|)\) are necessary and suffice as Boolean functions, so for two variables in different holes \(\sigma=2\).

Consequence for the route. A joint saving among errors with shared factors that is not domination needs the truncation to block every affordable principal generator: no \(s\) of degree at most \(D-K-e\) with \(sw_i\equiv w_i\). In a domain model the indicator of the union of supports is such a generator up to degree \((r+1)e\), so below that degree truncation is the only possible source.

Degenerate cases. \(r=0\): \(s=0\) and both sides of (a) are \(V_K\). \(r=1\): \(s=w_1\) qualifies when \(w_1^2-w_1\in C\), and (a) is trivial. A duplicated factor changes neither side. \(s=1\) satisfies the factorization in (b) for every family, which is why (b)'s factorization carries no content of its own. When \(K+\sigma+e>D\) the lemma says nothing.

Nonvacuity. In Pang's matched ledger with \(p=2\), \(d=t=1\): for (a), the pigeonhole system with \(5\) pigeons and \(4\) holes at \(D=3\), \(K=1\), \(w_i=x_{i0}\) for \(i\in\{0,1,2\}\), \(s\) their sum, \(K'=\sigma=e=1\): \(K+K'=2\le3\) and \(K+\sigma+e=3\le3\), with \(C_3\) proper. For (b), the same factors with \(E_\nu=y\,x_{i0}\) (\(g=1\)) at \(D=4\), where the closure is proper (php_degrees.json: dimension \(4905\), refutation degree \(5\)): \(K+\sigma+e+g=4\le4\).

The falsification check

The computation. The committed script factor_kernels.py uses the tested module tools/pc_closure.py on pigeonhole systems (pigeon axioms \(\prod_j(1+x_{ij})\), hole axioms) in the multilinear ring at \(K=1\). For single-variable inputs both Pang-form errors equal \(g_1g_2\), so a family's kernel is \(\{u\in V_1:g_1g_2u\in C\}\). Its predictions were written before each run, with the refuting outputs named.

Result. From factor_kernels.json: (A) at \(4\) pigeons, \(3\) holes, \(D=3\), the six families of check:truncated-kernel-beyond-support have stacked kernel equal to \(\operatorname{Ann}_1(x_{00})\), of dimension \(4\). (B) At \(5\) pigeons, \(4\) holes, \(D=3\) (\(\dim V_1=21\)), the \(15\) families \((x_{00},y)\), \(y\) outside hole \(0\), have stacked kernel \(\operatorname{Ann}_1(x_{00})\), dimension \(5\), while their codimensions sum to \(165\). (C) The \(30\) families with first input \(x_{00}\) or \(x_{10}\) have stacked kernel of dimension \(4\); the prediction, \(\operatorname{span}\{x_{20},x_{30},x_{40}\}\), was refuted in the first run (saved output), and the kernel equals \(\operatorname{Ann}_1(x_{00})\cap\operatorname{Ann}_1(x_{10})\), which contains \(1+x_{00}+x_{10}\): the element the prediction missed is \(1+s\) for the principal generator \(s\). (D) Predicted from that corrected reading before its run: the \(45\) families with first input in \(\{x_{00},x_{10},x_{20}\}\) have stacked kernel \(\operatorname{span}\{1+x_{00}+x_{10}+x_{20},x_{30},x_{40}\}\), dimension \(3\), equal to the intersection of the three annihilators, as observed.

Reading. In each case the computed stacked kernel equals \(\operatorname{Ann}_1(s)\) for one linear \(s\) (check:clique-factor-kernels); lem:factor-ideal-domination (a), (b) and (d) prove that it contains \(\operatorname{Ann}_1(s)\), and the equality is computed, not proved. The joint saving of these families, large against the summed count, is domination by one annihilator.

Inertness. The second-stage reviewer gave a reason that goes further, and the author checked it. Below the degree \(n\) of the product pigeon axioms they cannot enter a member of degree below \(D\le n\): a member \(h+\sum_i\lambda_iP_i\) of degree below \(D\), with \(h\) in the span \(H\) of monomials with two variables in one hole, would need the top monomials \(x_{i0}\cdots x_{i,n-1}\), which are standard, to cancel, so \(\lambda=0\). Hence \(C_D\cap V_{D-1}=H\cap V_{D-1}\), the degree part of the vanishing ideal of the hole-respecting assignments \(Y\). And the systems are never proper above degree \(n\): the committed check php_degrees.py gives refutation degree \(n+1\) at \(n=2,3,4\) holes (php_degrees.json). So every pigeonhole kernel computed on this line, in checks A to D and in check:truncated-kernel-beyond-support, is a domain-model kernel on \(Y\) (where \(\deg(g_1g_2u)=D=n\), as in A, a pigeon axiom still cannot enter: a nonzero combination of them keeps standard linear terms \(x_{ij}\), which no member of \(H\) and no multiple of \(g_1g_2\) contains): its savings are union-of-supports counts on \(Y\), by lem:domain-model-stacked-rank with \(X=Y\). The single-input killers of lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement are beyond supports on the cube, but they are supports on \(Y\). The next entry registers this inertness in general form.

A correction to the contraction count

Correction. The contraction-count entry stated in lem:domain-model-stacked-rank that domain-only savings “carry into Pang's proper quotient since \(B_D\) is inside \(C_D\)”. The inclusion gives only that each rank and the stacked rank can drop in the proper quotient; the saving, the sum of ranks minus the stacked rank, has no monotone relation between the two quotients, and the record already refutes the transfer. In check:truncated-kernel-beyond-support (\(4\) pigeons, \(3\) holes, \(D=3\), \(K=1\), where \(C_3\cap V_1=0\), so the support kernels are the domain-model kernels), the support ranks sum to \(66\) against a stacked support rank of \(12\), a saving of \(54\), while the proper-quotient ranks sum to \(20\) against a stacked rank of \(9\), a saving of \(11\). What survives is the inequality: the stacked proper rank is at most \(\dim U|_S\). ex:support-saving-not-inherited registers this and corrects the clause; the rest of the lemma is unaffected. The stage-one reviewer found the gap from the statement alone.

Across the goal

  • geometric-repair-application: resolved as a working proof on 16 October by thm:marked-curve-recovery and the Hermitian family; one research entry; no change since.
  • joint-error-savings: unchanged; ten research entries and one route review since 15 October. Gains: elementary range widenings for small coordinate inventories and exact descriptions of where savings come from; this review reduces the question further (Obstacle).
  • effective-annihilator-degree: unchanged; no research entry; the last review's note on reduced Boolean defects and nilpotents modulo pseudoideals stands.
  • hardness-preserving-repair: unchanged; no research entry.

record-audit gaps lists no open gap that no later entry took up.

Architecture review

Necessary target. For joint-error-savings: on a nontrivial unbounded family of actual errors, an all-parameter bound on their stacked rank in one protected quotient, strictly below the summed count, from hypotheses checkable on the errors and the system, giving a better sufficient multiplier budget or admissible range after costs; at Pang's parameters, an effective count \(c<S\) in \(S(1-\eta)^t\) that survives the adaptive choices. Downstream. Through lem:exact-common-kernel-selection any such certificate feeds thm:certified-repair-composition; through cor:pang-observation-calibration an effective count \(c\) would cut \(t\), and the degree \(\Delta=(p-1)dt\), by the factor \(\ln S/\ln c\). That is weaker than the goal's general repair theorem but is the explicit consequence this statement asks for.

Extra requirements. The approach has demanded non-domination. Restated with lem:factor-ideal-domination, the demand is that the certified common kernel is not \(\operatorname{Ann}_K(s)\) for one element \(s\) of the window through which every error factors, not a union of supports, and not spanned by products of killers of the individual factors, whose degree grows with the number of distinct factors. The first two are the open statement's own exclusions. The third is a change of target, flagged as such: the trivial \(M\le k\) transversal of thm:small-inventory-coordinate-exclusion met the old wording on its letter, in a weak form, and widens Braun's admissible range at polynomial degree; this review keeps that result as an elementary saving and excludes its kind from the endpoint because a product of per-factor killers pays in degree what the summed count pays in rank, one factor per error class, so it cannot give the effective count \(c\ll S\) the target asks for at Pang's parameters. What can be weakened is the requirement that the saving appear on arbitrary families; a saving on the actual error families of one application is enough.

Independent alternative. Before seeing the route, the medium reviewer received the goal, the open statement and the registered result statements only, with no open conjecture registered on the route (review record). It found no architecture that survives the exclusions. Its nearest candidate is an inverse-system Bonferroni bound: with \(\Lambda\) the functionals vanishing on \(C_D\), each kernel is the annihilator of \(E_j\Lambda\), and the functionals \(u\mapsto\mu(E_iE_ju)\) lie in \(E_i\Lambda\cap E_j\Lambda\) when \(2e+K\le D\), so the joint rank is at most \(\sum_jr_j-\sum_{j\ge2}\operatorname{rank}_C(E_jE_{b(j)})\) for any parents \(b(j)<j\). It is the shape of lem:restriction-rank-overlap in the dual, reduces to inclusion–exclusion of supports in the domain model, and near Pang's threshold, with nearly independent supports, its product ranks are second order and change \(t\) by \(O(1)\); an effective count \(c=S^{o(1)}\) needs nearly nested errors. Its second suggestion changes the specification: couple Pang's adaptive choices so that error supports nest; this is untested. Compared with the route: the Bonferroni bound is a transfer of a listed result, and lem:factor-ideal-domination explains where its product terms are large, namely when factors share a cheap generator; coupled choices are the one proposal that targets \(c\ll S\) at Pang's parameters, and they are recorded below for the next review rather than adopted, since nothing yet shows that nesting is compatible with Pang's per-family contraction.

Rank normalization. All ranks here are measured in one quotient, \(V_K/(C\cap V_K)\), and the lemma compares kernels, not dimensions, in that quotient; relative contraction is the form Pang's recurrence already uses, and an effective count is a relative statement of the same kind.

Decision. Core: joint-error-savings stays the worked statement, and the next entry is lemma-first. The reviewer answered the first draft's decisive test (products of killers at \(5\) pigeons, \(K=2\), \(D=4\)) by hand: by inertness that window is a domain model on \(Y\), where the common kernel is the product span plus \(C\cap V_2\), so the test could not fail. The next entry proves inertness in general form, for any system whose axioms of degree above the window have standard leading forms, and then poses the test where the closure is not the degree part of a vanishing ideal. If that test also finds only the three kinds, the next review switches cycles to effective-annihilator-degree, where a constructed annihilator, with its explicit degree, is itself the object sought.

Decisive test. Trivial cases. Duplicating a family changes no kernel; one factor gives one annihilator; factors in one hole have the linear generator of part (d); and the trivial instance is the product pigeonhole system below degree \(n\), a domain model on \(Y\), where every outcome is a union of supports. Power. The functional pigeonhole system over \(\mathbb F_2\), with linear pigeon axioms \(1+\sum_jx_{ij}\), functionality and hole axioms of degree \(2\), has proper closures above its axioms' degree: check:augmented-php-degree-small-boards records eight holes proper at degree \(4\) with refutation degree \(5\), and six holes at degree \(3\). There the linear pigeon axioms are active in every window, so the closure need not be the degree part of a vanishing ideal; six holes and seven pigeons give \(42\) variables and \(12384\) monomials of degree at most \(3\), within reach of tools/pc_closure.py, whose time there is to be measured first. Two variables in different holes and pigeons need a generator of degree \(2\) (prop:disjoint-product-map-degree), so at \(K=1\), \(D=3\) none is affordable. Attempt. For the product system the reviewer's inertness argument answers the question in a few lines; for the functional system the same argument fails at its first step, because the linear pigeon axioms enter every degree, and no recorded statement describes \(C_3\cap V_2\) there. Test disposition. New operation. Operation. At seven pigeons and six holes, \(D=3\), \(K=1\), check first whether \(C_3\cap V_2\) equals the degree-\(2\) part of the vanishing ideal of its common zeros; then compute the common kernels of the factor maps for factor sets in one hole, across two holes and across two pigeons, and compare each with the annihilator of the best affordable generator plus products of single killers plus \(C\cap V_1\). Prediction. Untested hypothesis, stated so that it can fail: the closure is not a degree part of a vanishing ideal, and every common kernel is still explained by the three kinds. A strictly larger common kernel would be the first joint saving outside them, measured where truncation is active, and the following entry would test it on Pang's adaptive families; equality would send the next review to effective-annihilator-degree. Record search. Searches for principal ideals of Boolean rings, annihilators of factor sets, products of killers and small exact closures returned prop:disjoint-product-map-degree (the generator degree), historical lem:columnconditioning (the column indicator), lem:relative-kernel-complex (standard families of nested kernels) and check:augmented-php-degree-small-boards with ex:parity-two-clause-closure-degrees (systems proper above their axioms' degree); none states the comparison.

Outside leads

  • Every finitely generated ideal of a Boolean ring is principal, \((a,b)=(a+b+ab)\) (Stone, 1936). It targets joint-error-savings: errors with shared factors have the factor ideal's annihilator as common kernel. Answers. It says when a joint saving is domination: exactly when the principal generator fits the window. Test. Passed: lem:factor-ideal-domination (c) and (d) above, checked on four pigeonhole family sets. Break point. The generator's degree: \(r\) factors of degree \(e\) need degree about \(re\), so in a truncated closure the theorem breaks once \(K+(r+1)e>D\), unless axioms supply a cheaper generator, as the hole axioms do; the test found the break does not occur within one hole. The test ran in a domain model on \(Y\), where it was bound to pass; whether the theorem's conclusion survives where the closure is not a vanishing ideal is the decisive test above.
  • Macaulay inverse systems (Macaulay, 1916). It targets joint-error-savings: the common kernel of multiplication maps is the degree-\(K\) part of an annihilator, dual to the quotient by the factor ideal. Answers. It computes the joint kernel's dimension from a Hilbert function. Test. Falsified as a stand-alone source of bounds: the duality is an exact restatement, and it yields a bound only with further input, such as the stage-one reviewer's Bonferroni chain of product functionals above.
  • Macaulay's bound on Hilbert function growth and Gotzmann persistence (Macaulay, 1927; Gotzmann, 1978). It targets joint-error-savings: growth bounds in one degree would bound the factor ideal's size in the next, and so the joint kernel from below. Answers. A joint kernel lower bound from one degree of the factor ideal, independent of the number of errors. Test. Not run: both theorems are stated for homogeneous ideals of a polynomial ring, and the closure is a non-homogeneous pseudoideal. The missed translation, named by the reviewer, is the squarefree analogue (Clements–Lindström, Kruskal–Katona) applied to the leading forms of the pseudoideal's degree filtration; the record's lem:kruskal-katona-growth and cor:intrinsic-shadow-ratio already bound one error rank's growth by Kruskal–Katona on standard monomials, and the joint version is not tested here.

Absurd bridges

  • Quantum stabilizer codes (Gottesman, 1997). Translation: the error maps are commuting multiplication operators on the window's quotient, and the common kernel plays the code space; in the stabilizer formalism the code space of a commuting family is fixed by a generating set, and its dimension falls by one qubit per independent generator. Answers. It would replace the count \(S\) by the number of independent generators of the errors' factor ideal. Test. Falsified in its quantitative half: on the four family sets one generator \(s\) fixes the common kernel, however many families, but it cuts the codimension by the rank of multiplication by \(s\), \(16\) in (B), not by one unit per generator; only the qualitative half, a common kernel fixed by generators, survives, and that is lem:factor-ideal-domination (a).
  • Chemical reaction network theory, Feinberg's deficiency \(\delta=n-\ell-s\). Translation: errors as reactions, the stacked rank as the stoichiometric rank, and the deficiency as a graph-theoretic count of how far the joint rank falls below a per-component sum. Answers. A joint saving read off a graph of shared factors. Test. Not run: no graph on the errors was found whose cycle rank matches the dependence of their kernels; in the computed cases the saving is a single annihilator, not a cycle count.

New mathematics. lem:factor-ideal-domination: the common kernel of factor maps with a principal generator inside the window is that generator's annihilator, and errors with those factors are dominated by multiplication by it; in a domain model the indicator of the union of supports is such a generator, and for pigeonhole factors in one hole the sum of the factors is. check:clique-factor-kernels: the pigeonhole joint savings computed so far are of this kind, with one refuted prediction recorded.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The review restates the non-domination requirement as three excluded kinds, flagging the third as a change of target, and shows that the latest evidence is a domain-model saving; no necessary implication changes. Bearing. None at the goal's own parameters: the lemma proves containments and classifies savings, and certifies none at Pang's or Braun's parameters. The cycle was spent because the line's only mechanism in what it took for a proper quotient needed classifying before more cycles went to it; the checks could have found a common kernel larger than one annihilator, which would have been a saving outside domination.

Builds. Three builds were queued in this period and are still queued. claim-refresh-command is built (tools/claim_refresh.py, with its tests) and waits only for a reviewer's reading before lead_queue.py close-built removes it. sentence-tool-before-review waits (16 October 2026): it needs the review gate's state recorded where a hook can read it, the first step of unreviewed-label-check, which also waits for that reason. stop-check-repeat waits (16 October 2026): it needs the stop hook's matching code read and a test of repeated flags, a framework turn of its own.

Record search. Searches covered common factors and annihilators of shared factors (thm:common-moment-radical, cor:feature-row-shortening, historical lem:sharedfeatures and thm:columnbarrier, none stating the principal-generator criterion), principal ideals of Boolean rings with annihilator intersections (historical lem:columnconditioning, the column indicator, here rediscovered as part (d)), the generator degree (prop:disjoint-product-map-degree), systems proper above their axioms' degree (check:augmented-php-degree-small-boards, ex:parity-two-clause-closure-degrees), and Kruskal–Katona bounds on leading forms (lem:kruskal-katona-growth, cor:intrinsic-shadow-ratio). The resemblance check put lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement nearest to the lemma, at \(0.19\), and check:truncated-kernel-beyond-support nearest to the check, at \(0.45\), both related in the spec; lem:domain-model-stacked-rank is nearest to the correction, which corrects it.

Verification gate. Dependencies read: the registered statements of the line's results, prop:disjoint-product-map-degree, lem:generic-relaxation-standard-degree and historical lem:columnconditioning. The sole fresh-context medium reviewer worked in two stages (review record). In the first it saw only the goal and the result statements; it found no surviving alternative architecture, proposed the Bonferroni chain and coupled choices recorded above, and found the unsupported transfer clause of lem:domain-model-stacked-rank, now corrected by ex:support-saving-not-inherited. In the second it passed (a) to (d), noted that each factor may carry its own degree (now stated), that (b)'s containment needs no \(s\) and its factorization holds for \(s=1\) (now said), corrected the Nonvacuity witnesses, the Reading (equality computed, not proved), the inverse-system and stabilizer outcomes, and the unflagged change of target in the third excluded kind; all were done. Attempt checked. The reviewer answered the step at which the first draft's Attempt stopped: below degree \(n\) the product pigeon axioms are inert, so \(C_D\cap V_{D-1}\) is the hole ideal and the first decisive test, at \(5\) pigeons, \(K=2\), \(D=4\), was a domain-model computation whose common kernel is the product span plus \(C\cap V_2\), \(25\) dimensions modulo the hole ideal. The author checked the inertness argument, added the committed refutation-degree check showing these systems are never proper above degree \(n\), and rewrote the Decision as lemma-first, with the test moved to the functional system. Process audit. The reviewer found the first title, “Factor-ideal domination and pigeonhole joint savings”, accurate but less informative than the one now used, which it proposed; a decisive test that could not fail with a Decision resting on it; the endpoint changed without being flagged; counts in the Question without a source; a Bearing saying the lemma could have shown a saving, which containments cannot; and a muddled Nonvacuity. All are corrected above.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • Coupled approximator choices: choose Pang's adaptive approximators so that error supports nest, the one route the reviewer saw to an effective count \(c\ll S\); probabilistic-degree lower bounds for OR might cap it. Record. The search for common factors of approximators returned historical lem:sharedfeatures (common-feature conditioning) and thm:columnbarrier, which bear on shared structure of inputs, not of Pang's choices.
  • Apply Clements–Lindström to the leading forms of the pseudoideal's degree filtration for a joint growth bound. Record. lem:kruskal-katona-growth and cor:intrinsic-shadow-ratio give the single-error version.

Inert top-degree axioms and functional pigeonhole kernels

16 October 2026 · Working proof and finite check · Constructed kernels where truncation is active. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Chosen. This entry develops kernel-by-construction, the user's question of 16 October, instead of the head. It bears on joint-error-savings, and it carries the decisive test of the goal review, which the next cycle on this route owes.

Best outcome. A common kernel, in a window where linear axioms are active above a domain-model frame, larger than the three elementary kinds explain: the first joint saving the line could carry to Pang's adaptive families, where an effective count \(c<S\) would cut the degree \(\Delta=(p-1)dt\) by the factor \(\ln S/\ln c\).

Question. The review rewrote its decision lemma-first: prove in general when high-degree axioms are inert, so that a window is a domain model; then, where the axioms are active, ask whether constructed common kernels of factor maps, the user's natural maps into the common kernel, give anything beyond one annihilator, supports or products of killers.

General statement. lem:top-degree-axiom-inertness: axioms of degree at least \(D\) whose degree-\(D\) parts are independent modulo the top parts of the low closure leave every member of degree below \(D\) a member of the low closure; for the product pigeonhole system this makes every window of degree at most \(n\) a domain model on hole-respecting assignments. check:functional-factor-kernels runs the review's test on the functional pigeonhole system, whose linear pigeon axioms lie inside the window, at the review's size and one below it.

Top-degree axiom inertness

Statement. Over a field, in the multilinear ring or with the Boolean axioms among \(G\), let \(G\) and \(P\) be finite sets of polynomials, \(H\) the degree-\(D\) PC closure of \(G\), and \(P_D\) the members of \(P\) of degree exactly \(D\); suppose every member of \(P\) has degree at least \(D\). Then (a) the degree-\(D\) closure \(C\) of \(G\cup P\) equals \(H+\operatorname{span}P_D\), and \(C\cap V_{D-1}=H\cap V_{D-1}\), if and only if \((H+\operatorname{span}P_D)\cap V_{D-1}\subseteq H\); a checkable sufficient case is that the degree-\(D\) homogeneous parts of the members of \(P_D\) are linearly independent modulo the degree-\(D\) homogeneous parts of members of \(H\), which form a subspace since taking the top part is linear on \(H\). (b) For the product pigeonhole system over any field, with \(G\) the hole axioms, \(P\) the pigeon axioms \(\prod_j(1-x_{ij})\) of degree \(n\), and \(D\le n\), the sufficient case holds, \(H\) is the degree-\(D\) part of the vanishing ideal of the set \(Y\) of hole-respecting assignments, and \(C\cap V_{D-1}=H\cap V_{D-1}\).

Proof. (a) Put \(T=H+\operatorname{span}P_D\). It contains every axiom of degree at most \(D\) and lies in \(C\). If \(T\cap V_{D-1}\subseteq H\), \(T\) is closed: a member of degree below \(D\) lies in \(H\), and its products by variables, within degree \(D\), stay in \(H\subseteq T\); as \(C\) is the smallest closed space containing the axioms, \(C=T\). Conversely, \(T\cap V_{D-1}\subseteq C\cap V_{D-1}\), so the conclusion forces the condition. Under independence, a member \(h+\sum_p\lambda_pp\) of degree below \(D\) has \(\sum_p\lambda_p\operatorname{top}_D(p)=-\operatorname{top}_D(h)\), so \(\lambda=0\) and the member is \(h\). (b) The hole axioms are monomials, so their closure \(H\) (with Booleanity) is the span of the monomials of degree at most \(D\) with two variables in one hole, and its degree-\(D\) parts lie in that span; the top monomials \(x_{i0}\cdots x_{i,n-1}\) of the pigeon axioms are distinct and standard, so independent modulo it, and for \(D<n\) the set \(P_D\) is empty. The standard monomials \(x_S\), \(S\) meeting each hole at most once, are as many as the points of \(Y\), and their evaluation matrix on \(Y\) is triangular for inclusion with ones on the diagonal, so they are a basis of the functions on \(Y\); hence a polynomial of degree at most \(D\) vanishes on \(Y\) exactly when its standard part is zero, that is, when it lies in \(H\).

Relations. This is the step the goal review's second-stage reviewer supplied for the product pigeonhole system, in general form; the committed check of that review (php_degrees.json) gives refutation degree \(n+1\) at \(n=2,3,4\), so for those systems every proper window has \(D\le n\). cor:frame-ratio-half (near-half-pc-degree) studies frames whose closure is their truncated vanishing ideal; part (b) says the product pigeonhole system is such a frame below its pigeon axioms' degree.

Degenerate cases. \(P\) empty: \(C=H\). All members of \(P\) of degree above \(D\): \(P_D\) is empty and \(C=H\), since axioms above \(D\) are not used. A dependence among the top parts of \(P_D\) modulo those of \(H\), for example two members with equal top parts: the sufficient case fails, and the combination, of degree below \(D\), is a member of \(C\) that the condition of (a) must place in \(H\) for the conclusion to hold. A member of \(P_D\) already in \(H\) breaks independence but not the condition.

Nonvacuity. In Pang's matched ledger with \(p=2\): the product pigeonhole system over \(\mathbb F_2\) with \(5\) pigeons and \(4\) holes at \(D=4\le n=4\), where \(P_D\) is the five pigeon axioms and the closure is proper (php_degrees.json of the goal review: dimension \(4905\) at degree \(4\), refutation degree \(5\)); the witness is a closure, not a Pang error family, so it verifies only that the lemma's hypotheses are met.

Architecture test

Architecture test. Decided. The goal review's predicted outcome occurred at the size it named, \(7\) pigeons and \(6\) holes, and at \(6\) pigeons and \(5\) holes: in the functional pigeonhole system every computed common kernel at \(K=1\) is the monomial frame's plus the relations, so by the review's decision the next review weighs a switch to effective-annihilator-degree. A first attempt at the named size stopped at the launcher's \(110\) s timeout inside the compiled refutation-degree search through degree \(4\), before any closure was built (saved output); with the search limited to degree \(3\) the named size ran in \(105\) s. The review's first check had a trivial answer it did not name among its Trivial cases: any proper closure containing the axioms of an unsatisfiable system fails to be the truncated vanishing ideal of a point set, since the axioms have no common zero. What distinguishes the system is narrower: its closure \(C\) contains \(H\), the closure of the monomial axioms, which is the degree-\(D\) part of the vanishing ideal of the partial matchings \(Y''\) by the triangularity of (b); so \(C\) is still a domain model in the containment sense of lem:factor-ideal-domination (c), and the test asks whether the linear pigeon axioms add common killers beyond that frame. At \(K=1\) products of single killers do not fit in \(V_1\), and the annihilators of affordable generators of the frame lie in \(\operatorname{Ann}_H(F)\), so equality with \(\operatorname{Ann}_H(F)+(C\cap V_1)\) implies the review's prediction.

The computation. The committed script functional_factor_kernels.py builds the functional pigeonhole system over \(\mathbb F_2\) (pigeon axioms \(1+\sum_jx_{ij}\), hole and functionality axioms), finds its refutation degrees with tools/f2_closure_degree.cpp, takes the smallest size with a proper window at degree \(3\) (or the size given), and computes with tools/pc_closure.py the common kernel \(\operatorname{Ann}_C(F)\) on \(V_1\) of the factor maps of several factor sets \(F\), beside \(\operatorname{Ann}_H(F)\) for the closure \(H\) of the monomial axioms alone and \(C\cap V_1\). Its hypothesis, written before the run with its refuting output, was equality \(\operatorname{Ann}_C(F)=\operatorname{Ann}_H(F)+(C\cap V_1)\); the one-hole and one-pigeon checks against lem:factor-ideal-domination, and the containment of \(\operatorname{Ann}_H(F)+(C\cap V_1)\) in \(\operatorname{Ann}_C(F)\), could not fail and are code checks. The positive control, added after review, is the reviewer's counterexample: on variables \(a,b,w,z\) with monomial axioms \(ab\), \(bz\) and the linear axiom \(w+a+z\) at \(D=2\), \(b\) kills \(w\) because it kills \(a\) and \(z\) and \(w\equiv a+z\); the script finds \(\dim\operatorname{Ann}_C(w)=3\) against \(2\) for \(\operatorname{Ann}_H(w)+(C\cap V_1)\), so it detects such a transported killer.

Result. From the first run (functional_factor_kernels_6x5.json, output): with \(3\) and \(4\) holes the system is refuted at degree \(3\), and with \(5\) holes at degree \(4\), matching ex:pigeonhole-binary-degree at \(4\) and \(5\) holes. The second version reran \(6\) pigeons and \(5\) holes with the positive control (functional_factor_kernels.json), with the same figures: \(D=3\), \(K=1\), \(\dim V_1=31\), \(\dim(C\cap V_1)=6\), the six pigeon axioms, which are independent, so no further linear consequence. At the named size (functional_factor_kernels_7x6.json), \(7\) pigeons and \(6\) holes, the closure is proper at \(D=3\), \(\dim V_1=43\), \(\dim(C\cap V_1)=7\), and the same factor sets have common kernels of dimension \(13\), \(11\), \(9\), \(7\), \(7\), each equal to \(\operatorname{Ann}_H(F)\) (dimension \(6\), \(5\), \(2\), \(0\), \(0\)) plus the relations, with the positive control again \(3\) against \(2\). The common kernels of \(\{x_{00},x_{10}\}\), \(\{x_{00},x_{01}\}\), \(\{x_{00},x_{11}\}\), \(\{x_{00},x_{11},x_{22}\}\) and \(\{x_{00},x_{11},x_{22},x_{33}\}\) have dimension \(11\), \(9\), \(8\), \(6\), \(6\), each equal to \(\operatorname{Ann}_H(F)\) (dimension \(5\), \(4\), \(2\), \(0\), \(0\)) plus the relations; the two controls equal the annihilator of the factors' sum. For three or four factors in distinct holes and pigeons nothing survives beyond the relations; for \(\{x_{00},x_{11}\}\) the two extra classes are the cross killers \(x_{10}\) and \(x_{01}\), each killing both factors through a hole and a functionality axiom, which are supports on the partial matchings.

Reading. At this size the linear pigeon axioms add no common killer at \(K=1\): constructed maps into the common kernel gave one annihilator where the factors share a cheap generator, supports of the monomial frame otherwise, and the relations. This is not generic for linear axioms: the positive control shows a linear axiom transporting killers. The reviewer's explanation for the pigeonhole case, unverified, is that \(x_{i0}\equiv1+\sum_{j>0}x_{ij}\), so a transported killer would also have to kill the constant. \(K=1\) is the weakest window, and two sizes were run; the result does not exclude a saving at larger \(K\) or another system.

New mathematics. lem:top-degree-axiom-inertness, in general form: high-degree axioms with independent top parts leave the closure below their degree equal to the low axioms' closure, and for the product pigeonhole system that closure is the vanishing ideal of hole-respecting assignments. check:functional-factor-kernels: in the functional pigeonhole system at \(6\) and \(7\) pigeons, the linear pigeon axioms add no common killer at \(K=1\) beyond the monomial frame and the relations, while a four-variable control shows that linear axioms can transport killers.

Selection gain. Unchanged: no selection, survival or hardness-preservation improvement; the test found no saving to select with.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The decisive test found only the elementary kinds where the linear axioms are active, at two sizes, which by the goal review's decision puts a switch to effective-annihilator-degree before the next review; no necessary implication changes. Bearing. None at the goal's own parameters: the lemma classifies windows and the check found no saving. The cycle was spent because the test could have found a common kernel larger than the frame and the relations, the first saving that could reach Pang's adaptive families.

Factor span domination

16 October 2026 · Working proof (generalizes the containment of factor ideal domination) · Transported killers. Produced by claude-code (claude-opus-5-5, reasoning medium).

Chosen. This entry continues kernel-by-construction, which the last entry developed, with its sub-idea on transported killers; it bears on joint-error-savings, whose goal review asked whether constructed kernels give anything beyond the elementary kinds.

Owed attempt. The constructed-kernels entry named the sub-idea: test whether killers transported along linear axioms give common kernels of several errors beyond the three elementary kinds, and prove that in the functional pigeonhole system a transported killer must kill the constant. The first half is answered here by reclassification: every transported killer, for its decomposition along the linear axioms, is a common killer of the factor maps it is transported from, so its saving is domination by their stacked map; for the five factor sets computed at two sizes in check:functional-factor-kernels the equality shows none beyond the frame at \(K=1\); whether the system has any in general is the open first sub-idea. The second half was not made: it needs a description of \(C_3\cap V_2\) for the functional system, which no recorded statement gives; it waits because the first half came first, since it decides whether transported killers can give a joint saving at all, and the pigeonhole proof matters only if they can. It stays the sub-idea.

Best outcome. A transported-killer mechanism outside domination that applies to Pang's error families, where an effective count \(c<S\) would cut the degree \(\Delta=(p-1)dt\) by the factor \(\ln S/\ln c\).

General statement. lem:factor-span-domination: errors that lie, modulo a degree-\(D\) pseudoideal and inside the window, in the span of products by a fixed set of factors have a stacked map that factors through the factor maps' stacked map, so their stacked rank is at most the factors' stacked rank, however many errors there are; transported killers are its instance for decompositions along linear axioms.

Factor span domination

Statement. Over a field, let \(C\) be a degree-\(D\) pseudoideal (a subspace of the polynomials of degree at most \(D\) closed under multiplying members of degree below \(D\) by variables), \(V_K\) the polynomials of degree at most \(K\), \(Q=V_K/(C\cap V_K)\), and \(v_1,\ldots,v_r\) polynomials. Let errors satisfy \(E_\nu=\sum_kc_{\nu k}v_k+m_\nu\) for \(\nu=1,\ldots,S\), with \(m_\nu\in C\), \(\deg m_\nu+K\le D\) and \(\deg c_{\nu k}+\deg v_k+K\le D\) for every term. Write \(T_v:Q\to V_D/C\), \([u]\mapsto[vu]\), for any \(v\) with \(\deg v+K\le D\). Then (a) \(\bigcap_k\ker T_{v_k}\subseteq\bigcap_\nu\ker T_{E_\nu}\); (b) the stacked map \((T_{E_\nu})_\nu\) factors linearly through \((T_{v_k})_k\), and \(\operatorname{rank}(T_{E_\nu})_\nu\le\operatorname{rank}(T_{v_k})_k\le\min\bigl(\dim Q,\sum_k\operatorname{rank}T_{v_k}\bigr)\), whatever \(S\) is. Part (b) uses only the conclusion of (a).

Proof. Each \(T_v\) is well defined, since a member of \(C\cap V_K\) times \(v\), multiplied variable by variable through degrees below \(\deg v+K\le D\), stays in \(C\); the error degrees satisfy \(\deg E_\nu+K\le D\) by the term bounds. (a) If \(v_ku\in C\) for all \(k\), each \(c_{\nu k}v_ku\) is a member multiplied through degrees below \(\deg c_{\nu k}+\deg v_k+K\le D\), and \(m_\nu u\in C\); so \(E_\nu u\in C\). (b) By (a) the kernel of the stacked factor map lies in that of the stacked error map; for linear maps on one space, kernel containment gives a linear factorization and bounds the rank of the second by that of the first.

Transported killers. The reviewer's control of the constructed-kernels entry, monomial axioms \(ab\), \(bz\) and the linear axiom \(\ell=w+a+z\) at \(D=2\), is, for the decomposition along \(\ell\), the case \(E=w\), \(v=(a,z)\), \(w=\ell-a-z\): \(b\in\ker T_a\cap\ker T_z\subseteq\ker T_w\). For several errors transported from the same \(r\) factors, the stacked rank is bounded by the stacked rank of the \(r\) factor maps, not by the sum over the errors. Every \(E\) also has the trivial decomposition \(v=(E)\), so the lemma is a statement about a chosen decomposition, not a characterization of killers.

Relations. The lemma generalizes the first sentence of lem:factor-ideal-domination (b), the containment of \(\bigcap_i\operatorname{Ann}_K(w_i)\) in the common kernel “directly, with no \(s\)”, from single products \(h_\nu w_{i(\nu)}\) to spans; it neither contains nor needs part (a) of that lemma, the principal generator. The single-input killers of lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement are its one-factor case. Its shape is that of lem:joint-word-shortening (a joint space bounded by a sum over generators).

What it gives at Pang's parameters. In Pang's setting, as the calibration entry states it, the error maps are \(u\mapsto([P_\nu g_{\nu,i}u])_i\) with \(P_\nu=\prod_{j=1}^t(1-q_{\nu,j}^{p-1})\), \(q_{\nu,j}\) in the span of the family's inputs of degree at most \(d\), so \(\deg P_\nu\le(p-1)dt=\Delta\); lem:two-input-kernel-enlargement is its two-input instance at \(t=1\). They always lie in the span of products by their inputs: take \(v\) the inputs, \(c=P_\nu\), so the term bound is \(\Delta+d+K\le D\), the calibration's own window (cor:pang-observation-calibration). So the product is no obstruction. But the bound is below the trivial \(\dim Q\) exactly when the factors have a nonzero common killer in \(Q\), and such a killer is already a surviving multiplier for every error; for survival the lemma is therefore no stronger than picking a common killer of the factors directly. And the bound does not involve \(P_\nu\) or \(t\), so it cannot reproduce or improve the contraction \((1-\eta)^t\). Families sharing inputs shrink \(r\), and help only if the shared inputs have a common killer, when the direct pick already works.

The open statement's wording. The factorization in (b) is domination by one coarser map, the stacked factor map. The goal review's restated exclusion names only the annihilator of one element; the first sentence of its own lem:factor-ideal-domination (b) already shows the gap for single products, and this lemma for spans. The next review should either widen the exclusion back to every factorization through one coarser map, or accept such savings, recording that by the paragraph above they add nothing to survival beyond a direct common killer.

Degenerate cases. \(r=0\): every error lies in \(C\) within the window, and both sides of (b) are zero. \(r=S\) with \(v_\nu=E_\nu\), \(c=1\): (b) is an equality. A factor in \(C\) has zero map and drops out. A term with \(\deg c_{\nu k}+\deg v_k+K>D\): the lemma says nothing.

Nonvacuity. The witness checks only the lemma's hypotheses, here measured against Pang's matched ledger's \(p=2\) and \(K=1\), and no other quantity of that ledger: the functional pigeonhole system at \(6\) pigeons and \(5\) holes, \(30\) variables, \(D=3\), proper (functional_factor_kernels.json of the constructed-kernels entry); factors \(v=(x_{00},x_{11})\) and the \(56\) errors \(y\,x_{00}\), \(y\,x_{11}\) for the \(28\) other variables \(y\), with term bound \(1+1+1=3\le D\). The common kernel of the two factor maps has dimension \(8\) in \(\dim V_1=31\), \(6\) of it the relations, so on \(Q\), of dimension \(25\), the stacked factor map has rank \(23\), and (b) bounds the stacked rank of the \(56\) errors by \(23\), against the trivial bound \(25\): a saving of \(2\); the JSON records a frame kernel of dimension \(2\), which the author derives to be spanned by \(x_{10}\) and \(x_{01}\) (each kills both factors by a hole or a functionality axiom), killers a direct pick already finds.

New mathematics. lem:factor-span-domination: over any degree-\(D\) pseudoideal, errors in the span of products by fixed factors have a stacked map factoring through the factor maps' stacked map; transported killers are an instance, and for survival the bound is no stronger than a direct common killer of the factors.

Selection gain. Unchanged: the lemma's bound beats \(\dim Q\) only through a common killer of the factors, which is itself the surviving multiplier a direct pick gives.

Goal progress. Unchanged. The lemma is a bound of the domination type and adds nothing to survival beyond a direct common killer. Bearing. None at the goal's own parameters: Pang's errors do lie in the span of products by their inputs within the calibration's window, but the resulting bound is independent of the contraction and is useful only where the inputs have a common killer, when the direct pick already works. The cycle was spent because transported killers were the one mechanism the decisive test left open, and the lemma could have shown them escaping domination.

Record search. Searches for errors in the span of factor products with stacked rank bounded by the factor maps returned lem:joint-word-shortening, historical lem:packedcoeff, lem:two-sided-frobenius-compression and cor:boundary-filter-rank, none stating this bound; the record's nearest statement is the containment sentence of lem:factor-ideal-domination (b), which the lemma generalizes. For the next attempt, the reviewer's mixed bound, a search for splitting errors into groups with a summed part and a factor-span part returned historical lem:sharedhybrid (one group handled by substitutions, the rest by common features, with additive weight cost), the same split in the historical weighting setting, and lem:weight-compatibility-factorization; neither states the mixed rank bound.

Reviewer's suggestions, unverified

  • A mixed bound: split the errors into groups \(A\) and \(B\); the stacked rank is at most \(\sum_{\nu\in A}\operatorname{rank}T_{E_\nu}+\operatorname{rank}(T_v)_{v\in\text{factors}(B)}\). It beats both the sum and the direct pick only if group \(B\)'s factors have a common kernel of small codimension and group \(A\)'s errors contract; tested against Pang's inputs, \(B\) would be the families whose inputs lie in a low-codimension common annihilator. Record. The search returned historical lem:sharedhybrid, the same split of a family into a group handled one way and the rest by common features, in the historical weighting setting.

Suggestion checked. The mixed bound's inequality holds: the stacked map of all errors is the stack of the map for group \(A\) and the map for group \(B\), the rank of a stack is at most the sum of the ranks of its blocks, the block for \(A\) has rank at most \(\sum_{\nu\in A}\operatorname{rank}T_{E_\nu}\) by the same subadditivity, and the block for \(B\) has rank at most that of the factor maps of \(B\) by (b). Whether any split is useful, beating both the summed count and a direct common killer, is the unverified part, and it is the next attempt.

Sub-idea follow-up

  • Its first half, whether transported killers give common kernels of several errors beyond the three elementary kinds, is answered by lem:factor-span-domination: they are domination by the stacked factor map, no stronger for survival than a direct common killer. Its second half, the pigeonhole proof, is not made and is restated as the first sub-idea below. Follow-up. Continuing.

Sub-ideas

  • In the functional pigeonhole system, prove that every transported killer at \(K=1\) is a frame killer plus a relation, giving the computed equality at every size.
  • State and test the reviewer's mixed bound as an all-parameter statement: for a split of the errors into \(A\) and \(B\), the stacked rank is at most \(\sum_{\nu\in A}\operatorname{rank}T_{E_\nu}+\operatorname{rank}(T_v)_{v\in\text{factors}(B)}\), useful when \(B\)'s factors have a common kernel of small codimension and \(A\)'s errors contract; test it on Pang-form families at \(t=1,2\) in the functional pigeonhole system. A split beating both the summed count and a direct common killer would make it the line's next mechanism; none would close the item.
